Practice Exam 1 (75 questions)

1 [ID-1027] Choose the right sentence regarding the decision management process. ▼
A)System analysis report is an output of the decision management process.
System analysis report is an input of the decision management process. Decisions are usually made based on these reports. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.3, p.79.
B)It is applied only on concept, development and production stages.
The decision management process is invoked throughout the whole system life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.3, p.78.
C)System analysis request is an input of the decision management process.
System analysis request is an output of the decision management process. The analysis is requested so the required information to make the decision is provided. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.3, p.79.
D)All the other options are wrong. ✓ Correct
See the other explanations.
2 [ID-1053] The Level of Repair Analysis (LORA) is used for determining: ▼
A)The best location to execute maintenance tasks. ✓ Correct
LORA is a decision-making tool used to determine the most cost-effective location for performing each maintenance task. The analysis considers factors such as cost, downtime, and logistical constraints to decide whether maintenance tasks should be performed at the operational level (on-site), intermediate level (nearby facility), or depot level (centralized maintenance hub). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.183.
B)If an item needs a mild repair or a full overhaul.
LORA is concerned with where maintenance or repair tasks should be conducted rather than the extent or nature of the maintenance task required.
C)The needed resources to perform maintenance tasks.
LORA is concerned with identifying the location that provides the most cost-effective repair capability, not the specific resources required for the maintenance tasks (the latter being an input to LORA). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.183.
D)If there should be preventive or corrective tasks for an item.
LORA focuses on identifying the most cost-effective location for repairs, not deciding between preventive or corrective tasks. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.183.
3 [ID-1025] Fill in blank:The purpose of the _________________ process is to create capability/assets that allow the organization to explore opportunities to re-apply existing expertise and skill. ▼
A)knowledge management ✓ Correct
The provided definition fits the knowledge management process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.3.6, p.67.
B)information management
The purpose of information management is to create, acquire, transform, retain, retreive, spread and dispose of information to appointed stakeholders. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.6, p.91.
C)infrastructure management
The purpose of the infrastructure management is to provide infrastructure and services to projects in order to support the organization and project goals. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.3.2, p.54.
D)cognizance management
This is a made up term.
4 [ID-1011] The Concept of Operations... ▼
A)is elaborated based on the system user's perspective.
This statement applies to the Operational Concept (OpsCon). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.1, p.106.
B)is also known as "Operational Concept".
Concept of Operations (ConOps) and Operational Concept (OpsCon) are two different things. The former is focused on how the organization will operate to achieve its missions, describing the higher level system in which the System of Interest(SOI) should operate. The latter focuses on how the SOI will be used in its operational environment from its user's perspective. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.1, p.106.
C)describes the way the organization will achieve its missions. ✓ Correct
The Concept of Operations (ConOps) describes the way the organization should operate to achieve its missions, objectives and goals. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.1, p.106.
D)describes the way the system will be used in its operational environment.
This statement applies to the Operational Concept (OpsCon). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.1, p.106.
5 [ID-1019] Choose the option that shows the right derivation order between technical measures: ▼
A)Measures of Performance -&gt; Technical Performance Measures -&gt; Measures of Effectiveness
This order is incorrect. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.7., p.97.
B)Measures of Performance -&gt; Measures of Effectiveness -&gt; Technical Performance Measures
This order is incorrect. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.7., p.97.
C)Technical Performance Measures -&gt; Measures of Performance -&gt; Measures of Effectiveness
This order is incorrect. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.7., p.97.
D)Measures of Effectiveness -&gt; Measures of Performance -&gt; Technical Performance Measures ✓ Correct
The correct order of derivation for technical measures starts with Measures of Effectiveness, which represent the overall success criteria that is independent of the solution adopted. Next, Measures of Performance are derived to quantitatively assess how well the system meets the effectiveness criteria. Finally, Technical Performance Measures are derived to evaluate design progress and compliance to performance requirements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.7., p.97.
6 [ID-1012] Which of these characteristics applies to both single requirement statements and sets of requirements? ▼
A)Verifiable
"Verifiable" is a characteristic that is applicable only to single requirement statements. See INCOSE SE Handbook Tables 2.7 and 2.8, p.116.
B)Able to be validated
"Able to be validated" is a characteristic that is applicable only to sets of requirements. See INCOSE SE Handbook Tables 2.7 and 2.8, p.116.
C)Unambiguous
"Unambiguous" is a characteristic that is applicable only to single requirement statements. See INCOSE SE Handbook Tables 2.7 and 2.8, p.116.
D)Feasible ✓ Correct
"Feasible" is a characteristic that is applicable to both single requirement statements and sets of requirements. See INCOSE SE Handbook Tables 2.7 and 2.8, p.116.
7 [ID-1055] Which of the following is NOT an example of emergent property? ▼
A)The stock market trends.
This is an emergent property. The trends in the stock market result from the collective behaviors and decisions of many individual traders and investors. The patterns or trends that emerge from this collective behavior are not predictable by analyzing just one person’s actions. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.2, pp.10 and 11.
B)The total mass of a car. ✓ Correct
This is not an emergent property because the total mass of a car is simply the sum of the masses of its components (engine, body, tires, etc.), and it does not arise from interactions between the parts. It is a straightforward additive property. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.2, pp.10 and 11.
C)An airplane’s ability to fly.
This is an emergent property. The ability of an airplane to fly arises from the complex interactions between its physical components (wings, engine, aerodynamics), none of which individually can fly. Only when all these components work together does the system exhibit the emergent property of flight. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.2, pp.10 and 11.
D)A traffic jam in a street.
This is an emergent property. A traffic jam arises from the interactions between individual drivers and vehicles, leading to a system-level phenomenon that cannot be predicted just by examining a single vehicle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.2, pp.10 and 11.
8 [ID-1035] Select the FALSE statement regarding system hierarchy. ▼
A)The highest hierarchy level of the SoI can be composed of one or more system elements. ✓ Correct
This statement is false. The highest hierarchy level of the System of Interest (SoI) is composed of only one element, as it represents the top-level system (the SoI itself) being considered. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.5, p.13.
B)A system element can be a system itself.
This statement is true. A system element can indeed be a system itself, as systems can be composed of other systems (e.g. a combat system as part of a naval ship). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.5, p.13.
C)A system element is called "atomic" when they are not further decomposed in other system elements.
This statement is true. An atomic system element is one that is not (considered to be) further decomposed into other system elements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.5, pp.12 and 13.
D)A "black box" representation of a system refers to its external view.
This statement is true. A "black box" representation of a system refers to its external view, where the internal workings and details are not visible or relevant to the observer. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.5, p.13.
9 [ID-1031] The following characteristics can be considered while deciding if a particular system of interest can better be viewed as an system of systems, EXCEPT: ▼
A)Geographical distribution of constituent systems.
Geographical distribution of constituent systems refers to the physical locations of the systems within the larger system. It is one of the indicators that the subject is a System of Systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.3.6, p.235.
B)Operational independence of constituent systems.
Operational independence of constituent systems refers to the ability of each system to operate autonomously without relying heavily on other systems. It is one of the indicators that the subject is a System of Systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.3.6, p.235.
C)Managerial independence of constituent systems.
Managerial independence of constituent systems relates to the governance and control structures of each system within a larger system. It is one of the indicators that the subject is a System of Systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.3.6, p.235.
D)High number of interactions between constituent systems. ✓ Correct
A high number of interactions between constituent systems is not a defining characteristic of a system of systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.6, p.235.
10 [ID-1005] Which one of the following is not a life cycle model approach? ▼
A)Evolutionary
The evolutionary life cycle model approach involves breaking down the system delivery into smaller increments. Requirements are not known from the beginning and new ones arise with each incremental deployment.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2, p.34.
B)Incremental
The incremental life cycle model approach involves breaking down the system delivery into smaller increments. Requirements are known from the beginning.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2, p.34.
C)Systemic ✓ Correct
There is no such thing as a "systemic" life cycle model approach.
D)Sequential
The sequential life cycle model approach follows a linear progression of stages. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2, p.34.
11 [ID-1008] Verifying a system means: ▼
A)Checking if the system was built right. ✓ Correct
Verification intends to ensure that something (e.g. system, element, artifact) was built right. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.138.
B)Checking if the system satisfies the stakeholder needs.
This checking would be against system requirements, not stakeholder needs.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.142.
C)Collecting evidence that the right system was built.
This refers to validation, not verification.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.138.
D)Checking whether the system satisfies the stakeholder requirements.
This checking would be against system requirements, not stakeholder requirements.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.142.
12 [ID-1052] Choose the correct sentence regarding system life cycle costs ▼
A)Committed life cycle costs rise linearly with time
The term "committed life cycle costs" refers to the decisions made early in the system's life cycle that determine or "commit" the majority of the system's total costs. These committed costs rise rapidly in the early stages of the life cycle (during concept and development) because most of the key decisions that lock in costs are made at that time. After that point, the committed costs plateau, since most financial decisions have already been made. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 6-8.
B)All the other options are wrong ✓ Correct
As it can be seen in the other explanations, this is the right choice.
C)Cumulative life cycle costs rise linearly with time
Cumulative life cycle costs typically do not rise in a linear fashion. Early stages of a project, such as concept and development often involve lower costs, while later stages, like production (with usually a higher spike) and utilization (usually with a more “flat” profile), incur much higher expenditures. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 6-8.
D)Costs to extract defects rise linearly with time
The cost of fixing defects generally rises exponentially, not linearly, as the project progresses through the life cycle. Detecting and correcting defects in the early stages (such as working with requirements) is far less expensive than addressing them later in the life cycle (e.g., during integration or post-deployment). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 6-8.
13 [ID-1059] Choose the correct answer regarding system complexity. ▼
A)There is no certainty about cause and effect relationships in a complicated system
Complicated systems have cause-and-effect relationships that can be understood and analyzed. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.7, p. 15
B)Complex systems are not wholly complex as they typically have some complicated or even simple characteristics ✓ Correct
The handbook states that systems are not entirely complex. Instead, they often contain a mix of complex, complicated, and even simple characteristics. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.7, p. 15
C)All the other options are wrong
There is a correct option to be chosen among the others. See the other explanations.
D)If the cause and effect relationships of a system can be determined, it can be said that the system is simple.
While simple systems have clearly defined cause-and-effect relationships, complicated systems do too—just at a larger scale. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.7, p. 15
14 [ID-1014] All of the following are generic life cycle stages according to ISO/IEC/IEEE 15288, except: ▼
A)Production
Production is a generic life cycle stage according to ISO/IEC/IEEE 15288. This is the stage where the assembling and/or construction of the system occurs. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, p.28.
B)Concept
Concept is a generic life cycle stage according to ISO/IEC/IEEE 15288. It involves activities related to problem formulation and the initial idea generation and planning for a system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, pp.27-28.
C)Support
"Support" is a generic life cycle stage according to ISO/IEC/IEEE 15288. It includes providing maintenance, updates, and assistance to users of the system or product throughout its useful life. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, p.29.
D)Operation ✓ Correct
"Operation" is a system life cycle process, not a system life cycle stage. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, pp.26-29.
15 [ID-1006] In a novel system development, the requirements are known from the beginning, but there is still a considerable technical risk for delivering the system. Which of the following should be the best life cycle model approach to adopt? ▼
A)all the other options are wrong.
There is a correct option to be chosen among the others.
B)an incremental approach. ✓ Correct
An incremental approach is used when there are successive incremental potential deliveries even though requirements are already known from the beginning. The incremental iterations of this approach should be able to deal with the mentioned considerable technical risk. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.36 and 37.
C)an evolutionary approach.
In an evolutionary approach, both the system and its requirements are delivered into smaller increments as new needs and requirements arise with each incremental deployment. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.38.
D)a sequential approach.
A sequential approach is appropriate when requirements are known from the beginning and a single delivery is planned (low technical risk). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.35.
16 [ID-1057] A system element that needs to be represented only as a black box for its requirements to be captured is also called: ▼
A)Complex element
Complexity or simplicity may suggest, but not determine if a system element is viewed as a black or white box. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.5, pp.13 and 14.
B)Systems on their own merit
“Systems on their own merit” are decomposed into further subordinate system elements and are thus not represented as black boxes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.5, pp.13 and 14.
C)Simple element
Complexity or simplicity may suggest, but not determine if a system element is viewed as a black or white box. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.5, pp.13 and 14.
D)Atomic ✓ Correct
An atomic system element is one that is treated as a black box—its internal structure is not considered, only its inputs, outputs, and external behavior. This abstraction is useful for capturing requirements without diving into internal complexity. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.5, pp.13 and 14
17 [ID-1054] Which breakdown structure is used as a base reference for performing a physical failure analysis? ▼
A)Work Breakdown Structure
The WBS is a hierarchical decomposition of the total scope of work required to complete a project, being suited for project management but not to physical failure analysis. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.183.
B)Product Breakdown Structure ✓ Correct
The PBS provides a hierarchical decomposition of the system into its physical elements, making it a suitable framework for analyzing potential physical failures at a given level. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.183.
C)Resource Breakdown Structure
The RBS is a hierarchical breakdown of resources such as personnel, equipment, and materials required for a project. It focuses on resource allocation and management, making it unrelated to physical failure analysis. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.183.
D)Functional Breakdown Structure
The FBS organizes the system based on its functions rather than its physical components, being suited for the analysis of functional failures instead. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.183.
18 [ID-1048] Which of the following traits is NOT associated with soft skills? ▼
A)The traits mentioned in the other options are all related to soft skills. ✓ Correct
Indeed. See the other explanations.
B)Subjective definitions
Subjective definitions are more related to soft skills, which involve interpersonal skills, emotional intelligence, and communication abilities. Hard skills, on the other hand, tend to be more tangible and quantifiable. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.1.1, p.263.
C)Social application
Soft skills are often focused on social application, as they involve interacting with and understanding others in various social contexts. These skills are essential for effective teamwork, leadership, conflict resolution, and overall communication in professional and personal settings. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.1.1, p.263.
D)Difficult to measure
Soft skills are often considered difficult to measure compared to hard skills, which are more tangible and quantifiable. Soft skills such as leadership, communication, and adaptability are subjective and can vary in interpretation and evaluation. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.1.1, p.263.
19 [ID-1002] The following are characteristics that should be observed in a single requirement satement, except: ▼
A)Unambiguous
A requirement statement should be unambiguous, meaning it is clearly and easily understood by all stakeholders. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., Table 2.7, p.116.
B)Appropriate
A requirement statement should be appropriate, meaning it is relevant and applicable to the system level it refers to. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., Table 2.7, p.116.
C)Feasible
A requirement statement should be feasible, meaning it is achievable within the constraints such as technology, budget, resources and time. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., Table 2.7, p.116.
D)Consistent ✓ Correct
Consistency in requirement statements ensures that there are no contradictions or conflicts between different requirements, thus being a characteristic that must be associated with a set of requirements. Consistent requirements help in maintaining a coherent and logical set of objectives, preventing confusion and ensuring a unified direction for the system to be engineered. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., Table 2.8, p.116.
20 [ID-1007] The demarcation between the system of interest and its context is called: ▼
A)Milestone
A milestone is a significant event or achievement within a timeline and it is not related to the demarcation between the system of interest and its context. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.2, p.7.
B)System structure
It is not specifically related to the demarcation between the system and its context. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.2, p.7.
C)Boundary ✓ Correct
The system boundary defines the scope of the system and separates it from external context and entities. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.1, p.8.
D)System architecture
System architecture refers to the overall design and structure of a system, including components, relationships, and interactions. It is not specifically related to the demarcation between the system and its context. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.2, p.7.
21 [ID-1034] Choose the correct option regarding system concepts. ▼
A)Interoperating systems are inside the SoI boundary.
Interoperating systems are external systems that have at least one interface with the SoI in its operational environment in order to perform a common function that contributes to the SoI's purpose. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.3, p.10.
B)All the other options are wrong. ✓ Correct
See the other explanations.
C)Enabling systems are inside the SoI boundary.
Enabling systems are external systems that contribute to any life cycle activity of the SoI, but are not an element of the operational environment. Pay attention on the bold part as it helps to differentiate enabling systems from interoperating systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.3, p.10.
D)Interfacing systems are inside the SoI boundary.
Interfacing systems are external systems that have at least one interface with the SoI. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.3, p.10.
22 [ID-1036] Choose the correct sentence regarding modes and states. ▼
A)"Low battery" can be a system mode or a system state of a computer. ✓ Correct
"Low battery" can be a state in the sense that it reveals the actual condition of the computer, but it could also be an operation mode with limited energy consuption. See the other options for the definitions of system state and system mode. See also INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.6, p.14.
B)All the other options are wrong.
There is a correct option to be chosen among the others.
C)"Combat" is a state of a naval ship.
This is an example of system mode, which is a specific operating capability during which a number of system's functions are performed to a specific degree. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.6, p.14.
D)"Broken string" is a system mode of a guitar.
This is an example of a system state, which is defined as an observable and measurable characterization of the current status, configuration or performance-based condition of a system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.6, p.14.
23 [ID-1024] Choose the right sentence regarding the acquisition process. ▼
A)The acquisition process ends when the supplier is selected.
It doesn't. "Establish and maintain an agreement", "monitor the agreement" and "accept the product or service" are further activities to be conducted within the acquisition process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.2.1, pp. 45-47.
B)An acquisition agreement is executed by the supplier through the execution of the other many system life cycle processes. ✓ Correct
The execution of the many system life cycle processes is necessary to deliver the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.2.1, p.45.
C)It has the purpose of obtaining a product or service in accordance with the supplier's requirements.
It should be in accordance with the acquirer's requirements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.2.1, p.45.
D)"Accept the product/service" isn't an activity of the acquisition process. It is associated with the verification process.
"Accept the product/service" is an activity of the acquisition process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.2.1, pp. 45-47.
24 [ID-1062] Which type of availability accounts for administrative and logistics delay times? ▼
A)Intrinsic availability
The term "intrinsic availability" is sometimes used interchangeably with inherent availability, though it's less common and non-standard in many frameworks. As it happens with the inherent availability (see INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, pp.178 and 179.), it also excludes logistics and administrative delays.
B)Achieved availability
Achieved availability includes both corrective and preventive maintenance, but does not include logistics and administrative delays. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, pp.178 and 179.
C)Inherent availability
Inherent availability considers only corrective maintenance time and does not include logistics and administrative delays. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, pp.178 and 179.
D)Operational availability ✓ Correct
Operational availability is the real-world measure of how often a system is actually available when needed. It includes all sources of downtime, including:Corrective maintenance timePreventive maintenance timeAdministrative delaysLogistics delaysSee INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, pp.178 and 179.
25 [ID-1009] Choose the right sentence regarding Systems Engineering: ▼
A)Systems Engineering deals with the realization of a system but is not involved with its use or retirement.
System use and retirement are also topics to be addressed by Systems Engineering. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, p.1.
B)Systems Engineering and Project Management do not overlap.
Both disciplines do overlap, specially in the early lifecycle stages. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, p.2.
C)Systems Engineering deals with the solution, not with the problem.
Systems Engineering also considers both problem and solution domains. See INCOSE SE Handbook section 5.3.3, p.269.
D)Systems Engineering focuses on engineered systems without disregarding natural systems. ✓ Correct
Natural systems are usually part of the context or even part of the System of Interest itself. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.8, p.213.
26 [ID-1015] System reliability is... ▼
A)calculated by uptime/total_time.
This is how Availability is calculated. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, p.178.
B)the same as mean time between failures.
Mean time between failures (MTBF) is a metric used as an estimate of the expected time between failures of a system. While it is related to system reliability calculation, MTBF and reliability are not the same thing. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, p.177.
C)a measure of how well it can be maintained.
This is how maintainability is loosely defined. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, p.179.
D)its ability to perform in stated conditions, without failure, for a certain period of time. ✓ Correct
This is a correct definition of system reliability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, p.177.
27 [ID-1058] Choose the option that correctly express a Systems Engineering heuristic: ▼
A)The original statement of the problem is normally the right one
It is the other way around.The correct heuristic would be something like “Don’t assume that the original statement of the problem is necessarily the best, or even the right one.”. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.4.4, pp. 21-23.
B)All the other options are wrong.
There is a correct option to be chosen among the others. See the other explanations.
C)Model before build, wherever possible. ✓ Correct
The principle is based on the idea that early modeling helps prevent costly errors later, especially in complex system development. It greatly supports evolutionary life cycles, rapid prototyping, and agile methodologies​. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.4.4, pp. 21-23.
D)In the latter stages of a project, unknowns are a bigger issue than known problems.
It is the other way around.The correct heuristic would be something like “In the early stages of a project, unknowns are a bigger issue than known problems.”. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.4.4, pp. 21-23.
28 [ID-1042] Which of the following is a Human Systems Integration perspective? ▼
A)Resilience
The ability of a system to provide the required capability when facing adversity. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.4, p.168 and 169. See also INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, p. 180.
B)Safety ✓ Correct
Safety involves designing systems to ensure the safety and well-being of users. It is indeed one of the Human Systems Integration perspectives. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.4, p.168-171.
C)Availability
Availability is the ratio between a system's uptime and its total time. It is not a HSI perspective. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.4, p.168 and 169. See also INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, p.178.
D)Interoperability
Interoperability refers to the ability of different systems or components to work together effectively and is not considered a Human Systems Integration (HSI) perspective. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.4, p.168 and 169.
29 [ID-1004] Choose the wrong statement: ▼
A)Stakeholder needs are not required to be stated as rigorously as stakeholder requirements.
Only requirements are obliged to be complete, correct, necessary, singular, unambiguous and so on. Needs may have these characteristics as well. See INCOSE SE Handbook section 2.3.5.2, p.109.
B)Stakeholder needs should be transformed into stakeholder requirements.
Stakeholder needs do not have to be written following the same strict rules as stakeholder requirements do. This transformation is thus necessary to assure that system concept and realization are driven in the right way without misunderstandings, information gaps, conflicting information, etc. See INCOSE SE Handbook section 2.3.5.2, p.109.
C)Stakeholder requirements are used as references to validate systems, elements and artifacts.
Stakeholder requirements serve as the basis for validating systems, elements, and given artifacts to ensure that they meet the intended purpose and align with the stakeholders' expectations. By referencing stakeholder requirements during validation activities, organizations can verify that the implemented solutions effectively address the identified needs and deliver the desired outcomes. See INCOSE SE Handbook section 2.3.5.8, p.134.
D)Stakeholder needs are not used as references to validate systems, elements and artifacts. ✓ Correct
Both stakeholder requirements and needs can be used for validation purposes. See INCOSE SE Handbook section 2.3.5.11, pp.146, 147 and 148. While a stakeholder requirement would certainly be a more objective reference to be used, validation is often performed against needs (for example, when asking a stakeholder directly whether a work product suits his expectations).
30 [ID-1066] Agile-Systems Engineering is about… ▼
A)rapidly developing, producing and commissioning a SoI.
Agility is not about speed. It concerns the ability to adapt to uncertainties.Regardless, Agility-Systems Engineering refers to an agile system (product), not an agile engineering process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.3, pp.165 and 166.
B)Engineering a SoI that can perform its mission faster than non-agile systems.
Agility is not about speed. It concerns the ability to adapt to uncertainties. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.3, pp.165 and 166.
C)the engineering of systems that are able to withstand uncertain and unpredictable operational environments. ✓ Correct
Agile-Systems Engineering refers to engineering agile systems — that is, systems that can adapt, respond, and continue to perform their missions effectively in the face of uncertain, changing, and unpredictable operational conditions. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.3, pp.165 and 166.
D)the Systems Engineering approach that is able to withstand uncertainty and unpredictability.
Agile-Systems Engineering is not about the application of SE being agile, but about making the system itself agile. The agile application of SE refers to Agile Systems-Engineering (pay attention to the hyphen), which is a different concept: the use of an agile approach in performing SE activities. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.3, pp.165 and 166.
31 [ID-1018] Regarding the validation process, it can be said that: ▼
A)Only the system and its elements can be subject to validation.
Validation is not limited to just the system and its elements. It also involves validating requirements, interfaces, and other aspects of the system to ensure that it meets the intended purpose and functions correctly. Actually any work product can be subject to validation. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.11, p.146.
B)"Verified system" is one of its outputs
It actually is one of its inputs. It is reasonable to first assess if the a system was built right (verification) and then assess if the right system was built (validation). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.11, p.146.
C)"validation criteria" is one of its outputs ✓ Correct
"Validation criteria" is both an input (a reference to be used to perform validation) and an output ( a result of preparing for validation). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.11, p.146.
D)It is too early to perform validation actions while executing the business or mission analysis process.
Not at all. Assessment of the OpsCon through operational scenarios is an example of an early validation activity performed during the execution of the business or mission analysis process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.11, p.148.
32 [ID-1032] Which system life cycle process has the purpose to synthesize system elements into a realized system that satisfies system requirements? ▼
A)Integration process ✓ Correct
This correctly fits the definition provided in the question. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.8, p.134.
B)Transition process
The purpose of the transition process is to establish capability for a system to provide services specified by the stakeholders in its operational environment. In simpler words, the transition process is responsible for installing the SoI on its operational and maintenance environment. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.10, p.143.
C)Implementation process
The implementation process has the purpose to realize a system element previously specified. Pay attention to the bold terms to differentiate this process from the integration process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.7, p.132.
D)Synthesization process
This is a made up term.

37 [ID-1029] Considering the SE relationships with both Software Engineering and Hardware Engineering, it is correct to say that: ▼
A)Assuring the quality of a software intensive system is usually easier when comparing it to a hardware intensive system.
Assuring the quality of a software intensive system is usually more challenging when comparing it to a hardware intensive system. Software systems are more prone to bugs, errors, and vulnerabilities due to their dynamic nature and the potential for unexpected interactions between so many lines of codes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.3.1, p.266.
B)During the support stage, It usually takes the same effort to change a software intensive system than it does to change a hardware intensive system.
This statement is incorrect. During the support stage, it typically takes less effort to change a software-intensive system compared to a hardware-intensive system. Software can be easily updated, patched, and modified without the need for physical changes, making it way more flexible and change friendly. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.3.1, pp.266 and 267.
C)All the other options are wrong
There is a correct option to choose among the others.
D)For hardware intensive systems, performance requirements normally have to be built into the initial delivery rather than iterated into the design. ✓ Correct
Unlike software systems, hardware systems may have limited flexibility for performance upgrades or changes after the initial development and production stages. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.3.2, p.266.
38 [ID-1023] Choose the WRONG complement: Technical reviews and audits... ▼
A)are used to determine the technical status of a SoI.
Technical reviews and audits are indeed used to determine the technical status of a System of Interest (SoI). They help identify any issues, risks, or areas for improvement during the system's life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.4., p.31.
B)are not the same thing.
Technical reviews and audits are not the same thing. While technical reviews assess the project against technical requirements, audits aim to confirm that the product/system achieved its required properties/functionalities. The terms in bold are key to understanding the difference between technical reviews and audits. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.4., p.31.
C)establish the transition between life cycle stages. ✓ Correct
Decision gates establish the transition between life cycle stages, even though technical reviews and audits may be part of decision gates. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.4., p.31.
D)should be flexibilized according to the system/project needs.
Tailoring the review process to fit the unique characteristics of the system/project is essential to ensure that the reviews/audits are effective and provide valuable insights. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.4., p.31.
39 [ID-1017] Fill in the blank with the best suitable option:The ________________ process has the purpose to generate detailed data and information about the system and its elements in order to realize them in accordance with system requirements. ▼
A)system analysis
The system analysis process focuses on creating rigorous data and information for technical understanding and to aid decision making. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.6., p.129.
B)design definition ✓ Correct
The design definition process focuses on creating detailed data and information about the system and its components to ensure that they align with the specified system requirements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.5., p.124.
C)verification
Verification is the process of confirming that a work product meets its specified requirements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9., p.138.
D)system architecture definition
The system architecture definition process has the purpose to generate candidate architectures and select the one that best fit the established requirements . See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.4., p.118.
40 [ID-1047] The established traceability between entities from the same system level is called: ▼
A)Horizontal traceability ✓ Correct
Horizontal traceability involves tracing entities across the same level of abstraction. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.3, p.201.
B)Vertical traceability
Vertical traceability involves tracing entities across different system levels of abstraction. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.3, p.201.
C)Bidirectional traceability
Bidirectional traceability refers to the ability to trace an entity to another entity while also establishing a reverse link back to the initial entity. An horizontal traceability link may not be bidirectional. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.3, p.201.
D)Asymmetric traceability
This is a made up term.
41 [ID-1060] When conducting Interoperability Analysis, which of the following is a key factor that must be considered to ensure effective system integration? ▼
A)Achieving interoperability at the interface level alone, as internal system architectures do not impact system-to-system communication.
While interface-level interoperability is critical, internal system architectures can impact data formats, security constraints, response times and more. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5, pp.180 and 181.
B)Minimizing system interactions to reduce complexity, as fewer dependencies lead to better interoperability.
While reducing complexity is beneficial, minimizing system interactions is most often than not an ineffective strategy for achieving interoperability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5, pp.180 and 181.
C)Addressing technical, operational, and organizational dimensions of interoperability to ensure seamless system interactions. ✓ Correct
Interoperability must be analyzed and managed across multiple dimensions.The three primary dimensions are:Technical – System architectures, data exchange protocols, and compatibility.Operational – How systems interact in real-world use cases.Organizational – Policies, regulations, and agreements governing system collaboration.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5, pp.180 and 181.
D)Prioritizing technical compatibility over operational and organizational considerations, as system interoperability is primarily a hardware and software issue.
Interoperability is not just a technical issue—it also involves organizational policies, operational procedures, and governance structures. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5, pp.180 and 181.
42 [ID-1070] In regard to Systems Engineering transformation, it is correct to say that: ▼
A)The transformation to model-based SE is limited to product modeling for engineering teams.
Model-based SE goes beyond product modeling; it includes SoS, enterprise-level modeling, virtual prototyping, immersive technologies, and supports Digital Engineering. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.5, p.274.
B)The transition from traditional to agile SE should not impact infrastructure or require changes to human resources.
Transitioning to agile SE requires new tools, infrastructure, and additional talent, in addition to a cultural shift. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.5, p.274.
C)The compression of the system life cycle increases the need for early SE involvement during concept and development stages. ✓ Correct
Compressed timelines demand earlier application of SE practices to manage risks and ensure system viability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.4, p.274.
D)Implementing SE for the first time in an organization can be done without modifying existing processes.
Adopting SE requires a cultural shift and the integration of life cycle processes into the organization's own processes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.5, p.274.
43 [ID-1050] All the following are activities of the portfolio management process, EXCEPT: ▼
A)Terminate projects.
The portfolio management process is the one responsible for authorizing the initiation of projects and also for terminating them. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.3.3, p.58.

Assessment of individual projects is done by the project assessment and control process, while the portfolio management process evaluates the portfolio as a whole. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.4.2, p.76 and section 2.3.3.3, p.58.
C)Define and authorize projects.
The portfolio management process is the one responsible for authorizing project execution. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.3.3, p.58.
D)Control individual projects. ✓ Correct
This is done by the project assessment and control process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.4.2, p.76.
44 [ID-1074] Choose the best answer. The process of using a model to study the behaviour or performance of a SoI is called: ▼
A)Analysis
Analysis is the process of systematic and reproducible examination to gain insights. It does not necessarily focus on behavioral aspects or require the use of models. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.1, p.192.
B)Modeling
Modeling refers to the conception, creation, and refinement of models, not necessarily their execution or use. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.1, p.192.
C)Simulation ✓ Correct
Simulation can be described as the use of a model to study the behavior or performance of a system. This matches the description provided in the question. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.1, p.192.
D)Validation
While validation can involve simulation, it is a broader process that confirms whether the system meets stakeholder needs and intended use—using various methods beyond just simulation. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.5, p.128 (Early Design Validation).
45 [ID-1075] Choose the correct sentence regarding the topic of modelling, analysis and simulation (MA&S): ▼
A)If an examination is performed with a physical model, it is called a test, not a simulation. ✓ Correct
Simulation refers to an examination using a digital model to understand or predict system behavior. When the examination uses a physical model, it is referred to as a test. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.1, p.192.
B)SE models are SE-specific and do not have to be shared across other disciplines.
On the contrary, sharing SE models across disciplines is essential to enable collaboration among project team members and stakeholders from different domains. Integrated and shared models help maintain consistency and support interdisciplinary decision-making. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.1, pp.197 e 199.
C)Physical models also allow full and continuous access to all model parameters and properties, which is often not possible with digital models.
It is the other way around. Digital models—unlike physical ones—typically allow full, continuous, and repeatable access to all model parameters and properties, facilitating rapid analysis and iterative refinement. Physical models are often limited in this regard. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.1, p.193.
D)Even informal models have to be expressed with explicitly defined semantics.
Informal models are typically described using human-understandable conventions (e.g., sketches or narratives) and do not require explicitly defined semantics. Their interpretation depends on context rather than strict formal rules. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.1, pp.196 and 197.
46 [ID-1033] Regarding the implementation process, it is correct to say that: ▼
A)A service is not considered to be a system element delivered by the implementation process.
Services are also considered to be system elements delivered by the implementation process. The four forms of system elements delivered by it are: hardware, software, operational resources and services. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.7, p.134.
B)Both fabricating and coding system elements are examples of tasks done within the implementation process. ✓ Correct
The implementation process is the one responsible for tasks like building hardware components and writing software code. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.7, pp.132-134.
C)The system design description is one its outputs.
The system design description is used as a reference during the implementation process, being one of its inputs. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.7, p.132-134.
D)It occurs exclusively during the production stage.
The "prepare for implementation" activity lists many tasks performed prior to the production stage. Besides, any modification or even some maintenance activities may require system elements to be produced. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.7, p.133.
47 [ID-1041] A maintenance mode that prevents system failure by implementing scheduled maintenance tasks is called: ▼
A)Corrective maintenance
Corrective maintenance involves repairing a system after it has already malfunctioned or broken down. It is reactive in nature and aims to restore the system to its normal functioning state after an issue has occurred. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.156.
B)Predictive maintenance
Predictive maintenance utilizes data collected to predict when maintenance should be performed to prevent potential failures. By analyzing data trends and patterns, maintenance tasks can be scheduled at optimal times to maximize system reliability and minimize downtime. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.156.
C)System modification
System modification refers to making changes or enhancements to a system to extend its useful life or to improve its capabilities. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.156.
D)Preventive maintenance ✓ Correct
Preventive maintenance is a proactive approach to maintenance that involves regularly scheduled tasks to keep a system in good working condition and prevent unexpected failures. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.156.
48 [ID-1071] Which of the following processes initiates the life cycle of a SOI while defining the problem or opportunity space? ▼
A)Stakeholder needs and requirements definition
This process focuses on eliciting, analyzing, and refining stakeholder needs and requirements after the problem/opportunity space has been defined. The activities performed by this process are constrained by the outputs of the business or mission analysis process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.2, p.107.
B)Project Planning
This process deals with planning the scope, costs, resources, and schedules required to execute a given project. However, it cannot be said to initiate the system life cycle, because a life cycle from the project management perspective (project start to project end) is defined differently than from the systems engineering perspective (system concept to system retirement). Moreover, this process is not responsible for exploring or defining the problem or opportunity space.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4, p.70, and section 2.3.4.1, pp.70 and 71.
C)Business or mission analysis ✓ Correct
The Business or Mission Analysis process initiates the system life cycle by identifying and defining the problem or opportunity space. It evaluates mission objectives, major stakeholder, business and environmental constraints and more, serving as the foundation for subsequent SE efforts. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.1, p.103.
D)Concept of Operations
The Concept of Operations (ConOps) is not a process. It is an organizational level document and an input of the mission/business analysis process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.1, pp.104 and 106.
49 [ID-1051] Which of the following is NOT an engineered system? ▼
A)A truck.
A truck is a physical system designed and built by humans, consisting of various mechanical, electrical, and software components that interact to perform transportation functions, making it an engineered system. Section 1.1. p.3.
B)An enterprise.
An enterprise is an organizational system that encompasses people, processes, and resources designed to achieve specific business objectives, making it a type of engineered system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.1., p.3.
C)The solar system. ✓ Correct
The solar system is a natural astronomical system formed through gravitational forces and other natural processes, not by human design or engineering. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.1., p.3.
D)A maintenance contract.
A maintenance contract is an engineered service system, composed of interacting components designed to fulfill a specific function. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.1., p.3.
50 [ID-1038] Choose the option that correctly reflects a Systems Engineering principle. ▼
A)Stakeholder needs are "frozen" at the end of the concept stage, otherwise no system can be developed.
Stakeholder needs may change and these changes have to be accounted for during the system life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.
B)Systems Engineering decisions are made based only on clearly optimum alternatives.
Since SE deals with complexity, it is quite common that decisions are made based on uncertainty, accounting for the risks. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.
C)A model can be used as a perfect representation of a system.
Only the real system can perfectly represent itself. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.
D)SE spans the whole life cycle. ✓ Correct
Systems Engineering encompasses the entire life cycle of a system, from concept through retirement. SE considers the system holistically and ensures that all aspects of the system are addressed. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.
51 [ID-1069] Choose the correct sentence regarding the concepts of Digital Engineering: ▼
A)Digital Engineering is the formal application of modelling to support requirements, design, analysis, verification and validation activities throughout a system’s life cycle.
This definition refers to MBSE, not to Digital Engineering. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.2.1, p.219.
B)For maximum benefits, Digital Engineering should consider both project and enterprise concerns together. ✓ Correct
To fully leverage Digital Engineering, it is crucial to align project-level engineering practices with enterprise-level strategies, tools, and data governance. This integrated perspective enhances decision-making, consistency, and efficiency. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.4, p.273.
C)A digital twin is a perfect digital representation of an actual system.
Though digital twins are used to emulate actual systems, they are not meant to be “perfect” representations. Its fidelity depends on the intended use, level of detail, and available data. The term does not imply flawless or complete replication, but rather a sufficiently accurate and useful model. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.4, p.273.
D)Digital Engineering and MBSE are unrelated.
MBSE is one of the core elements of Digital Engineering. Digital Engineering is a broader concept that leverages digital methods across the entire life cycle of a system, and MBSE provides a key methodology to create, manage, and analyze models as part of this digital ecosystem. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.4, p.273.
52 [ID-1046] In relation to an aircraft, the fuel truck can be considered to be: ▼
A)An interoperable and enabling system.
An interoperating system is a system that interfaces with the SoI in its operational environment to perform a common function that contributes for achieving the purpose of the SoI. The fuel truck and the aircraft do not perform a common function and are not even operating in the same operational environment. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.3, pp.10 and 11.
B)An interoperable, interfacing and enabling system.
An interoperating system is a system that interfaces with the SoI in its operational environment to perform a common function that contributes for achiving the purpose of the SoI. The fuel truck and the aircraft do not perform a common function and are not even operating in the same operational environment. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.3, pp.10 and 11.
C)An interfacing and enabling system. ✓ Correct
An interfacing system is an external system that interfaces with the SoI, which the fuel truck surely does in relation to the aircraft. On the other hand, enabling systems are external systems that facilitate a life cycle activity of the SoI but are not an element of its operational environment which is also the case of the fuel truck. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.3, pp.10 and 11.
D)An interfacing and interoperable system.
An interoperating system is a system that interfaces with the SoI in its operational environment to perform a common function that contributes for achieving the purpose of the SoI. The fuel truck and the aircraft do not perform a common function and are not even operating in the same operational environment. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.3, pp.10 and 11.
53 [ID-1068] Choose the correct sentence regarding the application of SE in commercial aerospace systems: ▼
A)SE is applied by aircraft manufacturers, not by airlines.
While manufacturers use SE heavily during development, SE also has its applications for commercial airlines. For example, it helps in maintaining the fleet during the support stage. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.3, p.250.
B)Aerospace systems use sequential and incremental life cycle models, but do not use the evolutionary life cycle model.
The commercial aerospace domain uses all three types of life cycle models: sequential, incremental, and evolutionary, depending on the context and system evolution needs. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.3, p.249.
C)Interface management is critical for commercial aerospace systems, as many system elements are produced in different parts of the world. ✓ Correct
Commercial aerospace systems involve global supply chains, with system elements developed in geographically dispersed locations. As a result, interface management is a key SE activity to ensure system integration. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.3, p.176.
D)Aerospace systems may be part of larger SoS, even though that might be uncommon.
Commercial aerospace systems are frequently part of a larger System of Systems (SoS) — for instance, when an aircraft is integrated into air traffic management systems, airport logistics, and maintenance networks. Saying this is uncommon contradicts this fact. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.3, p.249.
54 [ID-1044] Considering the Logistics Engineering concepts, all the following are considered to be support elements, EXCEPT: ▼
A)Reliability, Availability and Maintainability ✓ Correct
"Reliability, Availability, and Maintainability" is not considered to be a support element; instead, the RAM parameters are key performance metrics used to assess the effectiveness of logistics engineering, being heavily influenced by the support elements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.6, p.174.
B)Facilities and infrastructure
"Facilities and infrastructure" is considered a support element. It includes warehouses, hangars, IT services, dry docks, electrical services, etc. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.6, p.174.

55 [ID-1010] Systems Engineering is ... ▼
A)a transdisciplinary approach. ✓ Correct
Systems Engineering is considered a transdisciplinary approach because it integrates and transcends multiple disciplines to address complex systems problems. It goes beyond the boundaries of individual disciplines to provide a holistic and comprehensive perspective on system development, management and other related issues. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, p.1.
B)a multidisciplinary approach.
While Systems Engineering does involve multiple disciplines, it is more than just a multidisciplinary approach. It goes beyond parallel or independent contributions from different disciplines to create a unified and cohesive system solution. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, p.1.
C)a cross disciplinary approach.
Systems Engineering is not accurately described as a cross disciplinary approach, as this term typically refers to collaboration between different disciplines rather than the integration and synthesis of knowledge that Systems Engineering entails. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, p.1.
D)an interdisciplinary approach.
Systems Engineering is not solely an interdisciplinary approach, as it goes beyond simply combining different disciplines. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, p.1.
56 [ID-1049] All the following are examples of emergence, EXCEPT: ▼
A)The formation of a flock of birds flying in a V-shape.
This is an example of emergence, where individual birds following simple rules result in the complex behavior of a V-shaped flock. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.2, pp.9 and 10.
B)The "flight" property of an airplane.
No single element of an airplane is able to fly by itself. The ability to fly arises as an emergent property from the interactions of the airplane components. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.2, pp.9 and 10.
C)Complex traffic patterns from individual driver behaviors.
This is an example of emergence, where the interactions of individual drivers lead to complex traffic flow patterns. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.2, pp.9 and 10.
D)The assembly of a wall from individual bricks. ✓ Correct
This is not an example of emergence. It is a straightforward assembly process, not a new behavior arising from interactions. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.2, pp.9 and 10.
57 [ID-1064] The processes used to define requirements of a system and to transform them into an effective product are called: ▼
A)Technical management processes.
These processes provide planning, assessment, control, and decision support for the technical processes, but they are not directly responsible for defining and transforming requirements into a product. They also include processes such as risk management, configuration management, information management. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.4, p.70.
B)Stakeholder needs and requirements definition process and system requirements definition process
These two processes are part of the set of technical processes, specifically the ones focused on requirements. However, they are not sufficient by themselves to fully transform requirements into an effective product. Other processes such as design definition, implementation, integration, verification, transition, validation, operation, maintenance, and disposal are also involved in the complete transformation. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5, pp.101-103.
C)Technical processes ✓ Correct
The technical processes are used to define requirements of a system, to transform these requirements into an effective product, to use it, sustain it and dispose of it. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5, p.101. These processes include system requirements definition, design definition, implementation, integration, verification, validation, transition, operation, maintenance, and disposal.
D)Organizational project-enabling processes
These processes provide the needed resources to enable the conduction of projects by a given organization, but they are not directly responsible for defining and transforming requirements into a product. They include processes like quality management, knowledge management, infrastructure management, and others. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.3, p.51.
58 [ID-1022] Fill in the blank:_______ is an approach to reduce the likelihood of harming people, assets and/or the environment. ▼
A)Risk Mitigation Engineering
This is a made up term.
B)System Security Engineering
System Security Engineering is an approach that identifies, protects from, detects, responds to and recovers from anomalous/disruptive events. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.12, p.190.
C)System Safety Engineering ✓ Correct
This is the correct choice that fits the proposed definition. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.11, p.185.
D)Resilience Engineering
Resilience Engineering is an approach to allow the system to provide capability when facing adversity. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.9, p.180.
59 [ID-1045] Choose the life cycle model approach has the following characteristics:- requirements are not known from the beginning;- single deployment; and- single planned iteration. ▼
A)Sequential approach
In a sequential approach, we have:- requirements are known from the beginning;- single deployment; and- single planned iteration.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2, p.34.
B)All the other options are wrong. ✓ Correct
As it can be seen in the other explanations, none of the other options are in accordance with all the three characteristics given by the question. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2, p.34.
C)Incremental approach
In an incremental approach, we have:- requirements are known from the beginning;- potential multiple deployments; and- planned multiple iterations.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2, p.34.
D)Evolutionary approach
In an evolutionary approach, we have:- requirements are not known from the beginning;- typically multiple deployments; and- planned multiple iterations.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2, p.34.
60 [ID-1028] The Systems Engineering competencies theme that form the basis of SE as well as engineering in general is called: ▼

61 [ID-1056] Choose the correct option: ▼
A)An interoperable system is always an interfacing system. ✓ Correct
Interoperability requires two or more systems to effectively exchange and use information or functionality. This inherently means that an interoperable system must have an interface with another system, making it an interfacing system by definition. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.3, p.11.
B)An enabling system is always an interfacing system.
While enabling systems often interact with the system of interest, their primary role is to support lifecycle activities, and some may operate independently. For example, a logistics support system may enable a military aircraft's operations but might not directly interface with the aircraft itself. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.3, p.11.
C)An interfacing system is always an enabling system.
An enabling system supports the lifecycle activities of a SoI, such as manufacturing, maintenance, and training systems. However, not every system that interfaces with the SoI has an enabling function. For example, an operationally independent system interacting with the SoI is an interfacing system but not necessarily an enabling system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.3, p.11.
D)An interfacing system is always an interoperable system.
An interfacing system is any system that has a connection or interaction with another system, but this does not necessarily mean it achieves interoperability. Interoperability requires not just an interface but also effective and meaningful exchange of information or functionality. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.3, p.11.
62 [ID-1065] Choose the right option regarding the system life cycle stages: ▼
A)The mais aspect of the concept stage is the maturation of concepts and stakeholder needs/requirements into an engineering baseline that can be produced.
The Concept Stage focuses on exploring solution concepts, understanding stakeholder needs, and analyzing feasibility. The maturation into an engineering baseline happens during the development stage. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.1.2, pp.28 e 29.
B)The production stage is generally longer than the other system life cycle stage
Although there might be exceptions, the utilization stage is generally much longer than the other stages, including the production stage. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.1.2, p.28.
C)The utilization stage ends when it is decided that the SOI will no longer be supported.
See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.1.2, pp.27 e 28.
D)The development stage defines a SOI that complies with its stakeholder needs and requirements. ✓ Correct
“Defining the SOI” includes all the engineering work necessary to produce a baseline that is going to be built during the production stage. This happens during the development stage. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.1.2, p.28.
63 [ID-1061] Which of the following best describes the purpose of the Affordability Analysis in SE? ▼
A)"To minimize acquisition costs."
Affordability Analysis takes a life cycle approach, considering not only acquisition costs but also operational and support costs​. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.2, pp.160-165.
B)All the other options are wrong
All the other options are correct and one of them clearly presents a better description than the others. See the other explanations.
C)To ensure the system is developed within cost and schedule constraints while maximizing performance over its life cycle. ✓ Correct
Affordability Analysis balances performance, cost, and schedule while ensuring the system meets mission needs over its full life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.2, pp.160-165.
D)To reduce the life cycle cost (LCC) of the system.
While LCC is a main factor, affordability also considers the value and effectiveness of the system, not just cost reduction​. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.2, pp.160-165.
64 [ID-1072] Choose the alternative that correctly completes the following sentence:Resilience Engineering aims to provide the required ____________ to the system when it is facing __________. ▼
A)capability, adversity ✓ Correct
Resilience Engineering is concerned with enabling a system to provide the required capability when facing conditions of adversity. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.9, p.180
B)performance, risks
Resilience deals with capability, not performance (See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.9, p.180).Performance is a quantitative measure related to the execution of processes, functions, or tasks, while capability refers to the system’s ability to achieve specific objectives (See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., Appendix C, pp.312 and 313).A system may achieve its objective with varying levels of performance. Additionally, the term “risks” is too broad; “adversity” more precisely describes the unexpected disruptions or stressors that resilience engineering seeks to address.
C)availability, defects
The focus of resilience engineering is not on availability (system being available under stated conditions) but on the provision of the required capabilities in the presence of adverse conditions. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.8, p.178
D)safety, hazards
The focus of Resilience Engineering is not on safety, but rather on the provision of required capabilities under adverse conditions. In fact, resilience could theoretically be achieved even at the expense of safety. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.9, p.180

D)Risk Management Process
This is a system life cycle process that has a general approach to risk management, not specifically focused on system hazards. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.3.4, pp.81 and 82.
