68 [ID-1073] A set of practices, principles and conventions for the description of architectures is called: ▼
A)System architecture.
System architecture is the fundamental organization of a system embodied in its elements, relationships, and the principles guiding its engineering and evolution. It is produced using a framework, not being the set of practices and conventions itself. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.1, p.8 and Appendix E, p.332.
B)Architecture governance.
Architecture governance refers to establishing the relevant policies, strategies, goals, and directives to which the architecting efforts and products should comply. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.5, p.207.
C)Architecture description framework. ✓ Correct
The question provides the correct definition of an architecture description framework. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.5, p.206.
D)Architecture viewpoint.
It is provided by an architecture framework but not equivalent to it. A framework can provide many viewpoints. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.5, p.206.
69 [ID-1016] The following are fundamental objectives of resilience, EXCEPT: ▼
A)Withstand
Withstanding disruptions is a fundamental objective of resilience. A resilient system should be able to endure and maintain functionality during adverse conditions or unexpected events. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.9, p. 181.
B)Avoid
Avoiding disruptions is a fundamental objective of resilience. By proactively identifying and mitigating risks, a system can reduce the likelihood of experiencing disruptions and improve its overall resilience. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.9, p. 181.
C)Recover
Recovering from disruptions is a fundamental objective of resilience. The ability to quickly and effectively recover from disruptions, restore functionality, and adapt to changing circumstances is essential for a system to be considered resilient. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.9, p. 181.
D)Robustness ✓ Correct
Robustness is considered to be a means objective, not a fundamental objective of resilience. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.9, p. 181.
70 [ID-1020] Life Cycle Cost (LCC) equals to: ▼
A)Total Ownership Cost (TOC).
TOC may only include costs related to the period after purchasing/realizing the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.2., p.164.
B)Utilization and support costs.
LCC takes into account other costs as well. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.2., p.164.
C)Total Cost of Ownership (TCO).
TCO may only include costs related to the period after purchasing/realizing the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.2., p.164.
D)Something else that is not found in the other options ✓ Correct
This is the right answer as LCC = concept costs + development costs + production costs + utilization and support costs + retirement costs. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.2., p.164.
71 [ID-1063] Which of the following is NOT a typical goal of a decision gate? ▼
A)Confirm that the project deliverables satisfy the business case.
This is a typical decision gate goal. Decision gates help ensure that the project is still aligned with the business case and mission needs. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.1.3, p.29.
B)Confirm that the SOI is meeting its requirements. ✓ Correct
Decision Gates are focused on project progress, readiness, and strategic alignment, not in the SOI itself and its detailed technical compliance (a topic for technical reviews and audits). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.1.3, pp.29-31 and section 2.1.4, p.32.
C)Confirm that unsolved issues that have to be resolved in the current stage are addressed.
Part of their purpose of decision gates is to ensure that critical risks, issues, or technical concerns that have to be resolved before proceeding are appropriately addressed. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.1.3, p.29.
D)Confirm that the resources are sufficient for the current and subsequent stages.
This is a typical decision gate goal. Decision gates often assess whether funding, staffing, and infrastructure are in place to proceed with the next and further lifecycle stages. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.1.3, p.29.
72 [ID-1021] Choose the WRONG option: ▼
A)Enabling systems also have their own life cycles.
As anything else, enabling systems also have their own life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.1., pp.25 and 26.
B)System life cycle stages can occur concurrently.
System life cycle stages can occur concurrently and the most commonly observed situation is the parallelism between utilization and support stages. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.1., pp.25 and 26.
C)System life cycle stages may not be sequential.
System life cycle stages are not always sequential and can overlap. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.1., pp.25 and 26.
D)System life cycle stages have both entry and exit milestones. ✓ Correct
The correct name would be "decision gates", not "milestones". See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.1., pp.25 and 26.
73 [ID-1067] All of the following statements apply to System Security Engineering, EXCEPT: ▼
A)It involves the application of SE principles for analyzing vulnerabilities.
System Security Engineering applies SE principles to identify and analyze vulnerabilities that may be exploited by threats. This helps in designing security features into the system from early stages. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.12, p.190.
B)One of its goals is to mitigate hazards of systems to an acceptable level of risk. ✓ Correct
This is applicable to System Safety Engineering, not System Security Engineering. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.11, p.185.
C)It is applied with the intent to make a system less susceptible to adversities such an attack coordinated by an intelligent adversary.
The intent of System Security Engineering is correctly described here. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.12, p.190.
D)It relates to the identification, detection, protection from, response to and recovery from disruptive and anomalous events.
All these aspects are addressed by System Security Engineering. These activities ensure the system can function securely despite cyber or physical threats. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.12, p.190.
74 [ID-1037] Fill in the blank:In a ____________ system, the relationships between elements are not fully comprehended and there is considerable uncertainty between cause and effect. ▼
A)Volatile
Volatile systems are characterized by instability and rapid changes, but it says nothing about how the relationships between elements are characterized. In other words, any system (complex, complicated and simple) can also be volatile.
B)Simple
In a simple system, the relationships between elements are straightforward and easy to understand. There is little uncertainty between cause and effect, making it easier to comprehend and predict outcomes within the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.7, p.15.
C)Complex ✓ Correct
In a complex system, the relationships between elements are intricate, leading to a high level of uncertainty and unpredictability. This complexity makes it challenging to fully comprehend the system and determine clear cause-and-effect relationships. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.7, p.15.
D)Complicated
A complicated system may have many interconnected parts, but the relationships between elements are more structured and deterministic compared to a complex system. In a complicated system, cause-and-effect relationships are more predictable with reduced levels of uncertainty. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.7, p.15.
75 [ID-1013] Choose the correct answer regarding the relationships between system life cycle processes. ▼
A)Recursion happens when different processes are applied in the same system hierarchical level.
Recursion refers to the same process being applied to successive system hierarchical levels. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.1.2, pp.42-44.
B)All the other answers are wrong.
There is a correct answer among the other choices.
C)Iteration occurs when two processes interact at different system hierarchical levels. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.1.2, pp.42-44.
Iteration occurs when two processes interact at a given system hierarchical level.
D)Concurrency happens when two processes occur in parallel in a given system hierarchical level. ✓ Correct
Concurrency occurs when two or more processes are executed in parallel at the same system hierarchical level. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.1.2, pp.42-44.

77 [ID-2057] The ability to trace an entity to another while also establishing a reverse link to the initial entity is called: ▼
A)3-way traceability
This is a made up term.
B)Bidirectional traceability ✓ Correct
Bidirectional traceability refers to the ability to trace an entity to another entity while also establishing a reverse link back to the initial entity. This ensures that the relationship between entities can be traced in both directions, providing a comprehensive understanding of the dependencies and connections within a system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.3, p.201.
C)Horizontal traceability
Horizontal traceability involves tracing entities across the same level of abstraction, not necessarily establishing a bidirectional link between entities. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.3, p.201.
D)Vertical traceability
Vertical traceability involves tracing entities across different system levels of abstraction, not necessarily establishing a bidirectional link between entities. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.3, p.201.

79 [ID-2064] Which agility design principle states that modules should be independent units loosely coupled that cooperate towards a shared goal? ▼
A)Distributed control and information
Distributed control and information involve decentralizing decision-making and data storage across multiple nodes or components. It does not specifically address the independence or the loose coupling between modules. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.3, p.167.
B)Encapsulated modules ✓ Correct
Encapsulated modules are essential in agile architecture as they promote modularity, reusability, and maintainability of the system. They help in isolating components and reducing dependencies, making it easier to make changes and enhancements to the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.3, p.166.
C)Peer to peer interaction
Peer to peer interaction is an agility architectural design principle that emphasizes direct communication and collaboration modules. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.3, p.167.
D)Elastic capacity
Elastic capacity refers to the ability of a system to dynamically adjust its resources based on demand. It does not specifically address the independence or the loose coupling between modules. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.3, p.167.
80 [ID-2060] Choose the option that best expresses the concept of "Greenfield Systems Engineering". ▼
A)Modifying existing systems to adapt to new requirements.
This describes what would be an adaptive systems engineering approach, focusing on modifying and optimizing existing systems to meet new requirements or changes in the environment. This is not related to greenfield systems engineering.
B)Creating systems incrementally and iteratively.
This refers to agile or incremental systems engineering, which focuses on developing systems through iterative cycles and continuous feedback. It does not specifically address the absence of legacy constraints.
C)Designing and implementing new systems in a completely new environment. ✓ Correct
Greenfield systems engineering involves designing and implementing new systems from scratch in an entirely new environment without any existing infrastructure or legacy constraints. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.313.
D)Development of new systems within an existing legacy infrastructure.
This describes brownfield systems engineering, where new systems are developed and integrated within an existing environment with legacy systems and constraints. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.312.
81 [ID-2050] Choose the option that best reflects the concept of "intrisic hazard" ▼
A)A hazard that happens within the system of interest.
An intrinsic hazard is not just a hazard that happens within the system of interest; it specifically refers to hazards that are caused by inherent design factors of the system itself. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.11, p.187.
B)A hazard caused by the way the system is used and/or maintained.
While the way a system is used and maintained can contribute to hazards, intrinsic hazards specifically refer to those caused by design factors, not usage or maintenance. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.11, p.187.
C)A hazard caused by design factors of the system. ✓ Correct
An intrinsic hazard is one that is caused by design factors of the system, meaning it is a hazard that is inherent to the system's design/architecture. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.11, p.187.
D)There is no such thing as an intrinsic hazard.
See the other options explanations.

A made up term.

Verification aims to provide evidence that a product (or any entity) fulfill its requirements, but is not a defect prevention method. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.138.

87 [ID-2041] Choose the INCORRECT option about the integration process: ▼
A)The integration process runs only after the system elements are built. ✓ Correct
Integration activities start early in the concept stage and continue throughout the system's life cycle. One cannot wait until all system elements are built before starting integration because this will probably lead to incompatibilities that can't be solved without redesign. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.8, pp.134-137.
B)Bottom-up integration is a common integration technique.
Bottom-up integration is a common integration technique where lower-level system elements are integrated first before moving on to higher-level system elements. This approach allows for incremental integration and verification, which can help identify and resolve issues early. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.8, pp.134-137.
C)It is responsible for defining system interface.
The system interface definition is one of the outputs of the integration process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.8, p.135.
D)It has the purpose to synthesize system elements into a realized system that satifes system requirements.
This is correct. Pay attention to "synthesize system elements into a realized system" because this helps differentiate the integration process from the implementation process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.8, p.134.
88 [ID-2070] Which of the following is NOT related to failure analysis? ▼
A)FTA
The FTA (Fault Tree Analysis) is another technique commonly used in failure analysis. It is a top-down approach that models the logical relationships between system failures and their causes. FTA helps identify potential causes of system-level failures by tracing back through a tree of faults to the root causes. This method is highly useful in analyzing complex systems where multiple failure paths might lead to a system failure. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.174
B)LORA ✓ Correct
The LORA (Level of Repair Analysis) is not related to failure analysis. Instead, it is a decision-making tool used in Logistics Engineering to determine the most cost-effective location for performing maintenance tasks. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.174
C)RBD
The RBD (Reliability Block Diagram) is related to failure analysis as it is used to model the reliability of complex systems by representing the system as a series of blocks that describe how the system’s components work together. Each block represents a component or subsystem, and its failure or success impacts the overall system reliability. RBDs help in identifying potential failure points and analyzing the system's ability to continue operating despite failures in individual components. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.174
D)FMECA
The FMECA involves identifying potential failure modes for each component, assessing the effects of those failures on the system, and prioritizing them based on their criticality. FMECA is widely used to improve system reliability, enhance safety, and guide maintenance efforts by identifying and mitigating risks associated with system failures. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.174
89 [ID-2021] Choose the right sentence regarding configuration management . ▼
A)System's configuration should be audited to assure that the configuration management process is working as it should. ✓ Correct
Auditing the system's configuration is an essential part of the configuration management process to ensure that the established procedures and controls are being followed correctly. Regular audits help in verifying the accuracy, completeness, and consistency of the system's configuration information, thereby ensuring the effectiveness of the configuration management process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.5, pp. 87-90.
B)There is no need for items under configuration management to be uniquely identified.
It is essential for items under configuration management to be uniquely identified to ensure proper tracking, control, and management of changes of "the item" instead of "a item". See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.5, pp. 87-90.
C)Configuration management activities should be performed for every single item of the system.
While configuration management activities certainly should be performed for critical items of the system, it is not necessary to perform them for every single item. The focus should be on identifying and managing the key configuration items that have a significant impact on the system's functionality and performance, selecting candidate items. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.5, pp. 87-90.
D)Configuration management activities should be performed from the moment the system's utilization stage begins, not before.
Configuration management activities should actually start from the early stages of the system's life cycle, not just when the system's utilization stage begins. It is crucial to establish configuration management processes and controls early on to ensure the system's integrity and traceability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.5, pp. 87-90.

97 [ID-2026] Decision gates are typically located: ▼

101 [ID-2047] A maintenance task that promotes changes or enhancements to a system to extend its useful life is an example of: ▼
A)Predictive maintenance
Predictive maintenance utilizes data collected to predict when maintenance should be performed to prevent potential failures. By analyzing data trends and patterns, maintenance tasks can be scheduled at optimal times to maximize system reliability and minimize downtime. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.156.
B)Corrective maintenance
Corrective maintenance involves repairing a system after it has already malfunctioned or broken down. It is reactive in nature and aims to restore the system to its normal functioning state after an issue has occurred. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.156.
C)Preventive maintenance
Preventive maintenance is a proactive approach to maintenance that involves regularly scheduled tasks to keep a system in good working condition and prevent unexpected failures. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.156.
D)System modification ✓ Correct
System modification refers to making changes or enhancements to a system to extend its useful life or to improve its capabilities. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.156.

This is quite tricky because there are some management processes that fit into the technical management processes group and others that are part of the organizational project enabling processes. A good reasoning to adopt in order to associate the management processes to their right group would be: "If it relates more to the organization as a whole than it relates to the system/project, it is an organizational project enabling process. If it relates more to the system/project than it relates to the organization as a whole, it is a technical management process ".

Portfolio management is not a technical management process. It is an organizational project enabling process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3, p.41.This is quite tricky because not every management process fits into the technical management processes group. A good reasoning to adopt in order to associate the management processes to their right group would be: "If it relates more to the organization as whole than it relates to the system/project, it is an organizational project enabling process. If it relates more to the system/project than it relates to the organization as whole, it is a technical management process ".
D)Transition is a technical process.
Transition is indeed a technical process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3, p.41.
103 [ID-2040] It is correct to say that the transition process... ▼

104 [ID-2053] The waterfall model is an example of a: ▼
A)Incremental approach
In an incremental approach, there are incremental potential deliveries even though requirements are already known from the beginning. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.2, pp.36 and 37.
B)Sequential approach ✓ Correct
The waterfall model is a sequential approach. In a sequential approach, there is a single delivery and the SE activities are broken down into linear sequential stages where each of these stages depend on the deliverables of previous stages and on the feedback from subsequent stages. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.35.
C)All the other options are wrong.
There is a correct option to be chosen among the others.
D)Evolutionary approach
In an evolutionary approach, both the system and its requirements are delivered into smaller increments; new needs and requirements arise with each incremental deployment. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.3, p.38.

B)The decision to exit from a stage may be simply based on the need for experimental knowledge from another stage. ✓ Correct
This is applicable to entering a stage but not to exiting from it, which should be based on fixed requirements for stage completion. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.2, p.233.
C)Activities can be accommodated in any of the stages, even concurrently, without progressive sequencing.
In the Agile Systems Engineering life cycle model, activities can be performed in any stage without a clear sequencing, even though there are fixed requirements to be met for exiting a given life cycle stage. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.2, pp.222 and 233.
D)The retirement deals with older version and/or system elements that are retired frequently as the SoI evolves.
The experimental nature of an Agile Systems Engineering life cycle model increases frequency and focus of retirement activities through the agile system life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.2, p.233.

107 [ID-2038] Choose the WRONG sentence regarding emergence. ▼
A)Emergence cannot be observed in a single element.

There are both desirable and undesirable emergent properties. See INCOSE SE Handbook Section 1.3.2, p.9.

Emergence is a fundamental property of all systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook Section 1.3.2, p.9.

Interference between system elements is an example of an emergent property as it results from the interactions and relationships between the elements. See INCOSE SE Handbook Section 1.3.2, p.9.

109 [ID-2043] A representation of a system refers to its external view - where the internal workings and details are not visible or relevant to the observer - is called: ▼
A)White box
A white box representation of a system refers to viewing the system from an internal perspective, where the observer has visibility into the internal workings and details of the system. This is in contrast to a black box representation where only the external properties/behavior are considered. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.1, p.9.
B)System environment
System environment is a term used as a synonym of system context, that refers to entities that interact with the SoI and are beyond its boundary. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.1, p.8.
C)Black box ✓ Correct
A black box representation of a system refers to viewing the system from an external perspective, where the internal workings and details are not visible or relevant to the observer. This allows for a focus on the system's inputs, outputs, and behavior without needing to understand its internal mechanisms. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.5, p.13.
D)System context
System context refers to entities that interact with the SoI and are beyond its boundary. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.1, p.8.

111 [ID-2007] COTS solutions are often adopted expecting the following advantage: ▼
A)Adhesion to system requirements is increased.
Adhesion to system requirements is not expected to be increased by adopting COTS solutions. Customization or integration may be required to ensure full compliance with system requirements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.3.3, p.231.
B)Development time is reduced. ✓ Correct
Adopting COTS solutions can reduce development time as these solutions are already available, saving time on building a custom system element from scratch. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.3.3, p.231.
C)Life cycle cost is reduced.
While adopting COTS solutions can initially lead to cost savings in concept, development and production stages, it may not always directly result in a reduced life cycle cost. The LCC implications of COTS solutions depend on factors that are applicable in the utilisation and support stages, like licensing, maintenance, support, among others. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.3.3, p.231.
D)More control over the COTS element life cycle.
Adopting COTS solutions typically means relying on external vendors for the life cycle of the elements, rather than having direct control over it. Vendors may release updates, patches, or new versions independently, affecting the control over the product life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.3.3, p.231.
112 [ID-2075] Which of the following represents a significant and reasonable challenge in achieving interoperability between systems? ▼
A)Establishing a single global standard for system interfaces, as multiple standards across industries and countries hinder interoperability efforts.
While standardization is important, achieving a single global standard is unrealistic due to diverse regulatory, technological, and business requirements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5, pp.171 and 172..
B)Focusing on achieving full interoperability at the design stage, as ongoing system modifications rarely impact interoperability.
Interoperability is a continuous effort, not a one-time achievement at the design stage.System updates, software patches, hardware modifications, and evolving external systems can all impact interoperability, requiring continuous assessment and adaptation. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5, pp.171 and 172.
C)Reconciling differences in system architectures, communication protocols, and governance frameworks to enable seamless interaction. ✓ Correct
This aligns with one of the primary challenges outlined in the handbook: different architectures, inconsistent protocols, and governance frameworks create barriers to interoperability.Effective interoperability solutions involve bridging these gaps through integration frameworks, adaptation layers, and cross-domain agreements.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5, pp.171 and 172.
D)Ensuring that all systems follow the same development methodology, as interoperability depends primarily on process alignment rather than system architecture.
While consistent development methodologies can help with standardization, interoperability is primarily a technical and architectural challenge rather than just a process issue. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5, pp.171 and 172.
113 [ID-2067] Choose the option that reflects a consequence of optimum SE investment within a project. ▼
A)Increased costs but reduced schedule.
Cost and schedule overruns should be reduced, what could be accomplished due to better planning. Real cost and schedule could be lowered, but not necessarily. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 5 and 6.
B)Reduced total cost.
Costs overrun should be reduced, what could be accomplished due to better cost planning. Real costs could be lowered, but not necessarily. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 5 and 6.
C)Reduced cost overrun in relation with the planned costs. ✓ Correct
Based on the studies performed by Honour (2013), it can be said that the right amount of SE effort in a project contributes to lower costs overruns. This might be due to a reduction of the real total costs, more accurate cost estimations or both. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 5 and 6.
D)Reduced total schedule.
The schedule overrun should be reduced, but this might be due to a more accurate schedule planning. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 5 and 6.

C)all the other options are wrong.
There is a correct option to be chosen among the others.
D)an evolutionary approach ✓ Correct
In an evolutionary approach, both the system and its requirements are delivered into smaller increments as new needs and requirements arise with each incremental deployment. C)is concerned with the addressment of the possible losses associated with the system life cycle.
This is Loss-Driven Systems Engineering. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.3, p.224.
D)is focused on enabling system production (not in the whole life cycle) in a cost-effective and responsible manner.
This is Manufacturability analysis. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.7, p.175.
116 [ID-2074] Procedurally breaking down the problem into its parts and then solving and reassembling to form the whole solution is an approach that best fits: ▼
A)Simple systems
Simple systems have straightforward, easily understandable relationships between components that do not necessarily require procedural decomposition.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.7, p.15.
B)Complex systems
Complex systems exhibit emergent behaviors that cannot be easily decomposed and solved using traditional reductionist approaches. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.7, p.15.
C)Social systems
The approach will depend whether the social system is a complex, complicated or simple one. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.7, p.15.
D)Complicated systems ✓ Correct
Complicated systems have deterministic and predictable relationships between elements, making them suitable for reductionist approaches where the system is broken down, analyzed, and reassembled. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.7, p.15.
117 [ID-2039] Which system life cycle process has the purpose to realize a system element previously specified? ▼
A)Integration process
The integration process has the purpose to synthesize system elements into a realized system that satifies system requirements. Pay attention to the bold terms to differentiate this process from the implementation process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.8, p.134.
B)Transition process
The purpose of the transition process is to establish capability for a system to provide services specified by the stakeholders in its operational environment. In simpler words, the transition process is responsible for installing the SoI on its operational and maintenance environment. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.10, p.143.
C)Realization process
This is a made up term.
D)Implementation process ✓ Correct
This correctly fits the definition provided in the question. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.7, p.132.
118 [ID-2004] Choose the right sentence regarding the tailoring process: ▼
A)When in doubt, one can opt to use all system life cycle processes and activities just to be safe.
Using all system life cycle processes and activities without considering the specific needs of the project can lead to unnecessary complexity, inefficiency, and potential resource wastage. Tailoring is essential to ensure that the processes are appropriate and optimized for the project's requirements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.1, pp.215-219
B)Compliance to the standardized processes should be done at the right, customized level. ✓ Correct
Implementing the standardized processes at an appropriate level of detail and rigor helps to achieve the desired outcomes while avoiding unnecessary bureaucracy or inefficiencies. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.1, pp.215-219
C)The projects should be customized to fit the system life cycle processes.
It is the other way around. System life cycle processes should be tailored to fit the project needs to become better aligned with the specific goals, scope, and constraints of the project at hand. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.1, pp. 215-219.
D)Tailored baselines applied in other systems should be reused, avoiding the need of reapplying the tailoring process.
While reusing tailored baselines from other systems may appear to be beneficial in terms of efficiency and consistency, it is important to review and adapt them to fit the unique characteristics and needs of the current project. Blindly reusing tailored baselines without proper evaluation may not align with the project's objectives. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.1, pp.215-219

120 [ID-2011] Choose the right sentence: ▼
A)Systems Engineering is a multidisciplinary and integrative approach.

It goes beyond the boundaries of individual disciplines to provide a holistic and comprehensive perspective on system development, management, support, and other related issues. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, pp. 1 and 2.
B)A service is not considered to be a system.
Many things, including services, can be considered to be a system, as long as it fits the system definition. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, p.2.

The arrangement has to exhibit behavior/meaning that is not observed in the constituent elements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, p.2.

123 [ID-2002] Regarding the risk management process, it is correct to say that: ▼
A)It only addresses risks related to the current life cycle stage the system is at.
The risk management process is not limited to addressing risks related to the current life cycle stage of the system. On the contrary, problems that may happen later in the system life cycle should be identified and treated early by the risk management process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.4.4, pp.82-86.
B)The following are basic risk treatment approaches: acceptance, avoidance, mitigation and transference. ✓ Correct
The basic risk treatment approaches in risk management include acceptance (acknowledging and living with the risk), avoidance (changing the course of action to eliminate the risk), mitigation (reducing the impact or likelihood of the risk), and transference (shifting the risk to another party). These approaches help in effectively managing and responding to risks. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.4.4, pp.83-86.
C)It estimates the likelihood and cause of occurrence of each risk.
The cause is assumed, not estimated. Consequences are estimated. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.4.4, pp.82.
D)All the other options are wrong.
There is a correct option to be chosen among the others.
124 [ID-2012] Choose the right sentence ▼
A)Cumulative life cycle cost of a system is higher at its utilization stage than it is at its retirement stage.
The cumulative life cycle cost of a system will always be higher the later the system is in its life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.2, p.7.
B)It generally costs more to extract defects of a system during its utilization stage than it does during its production stage. ✓ Correct
The impact and consequences of defects tend to increase as the system is being used. This is because the system is already built and it also may require downtime and impact users. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.2, p.7.
C)All the other options are wrong.
There is a correct option among the others.
D)Life cycle cost spent = life cycle cost committed
Life cycle cost spent and life cycle cost committed aren't the same thing. The former represents the actual expenditure while the latter represents the projected cost. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.2, p.7.
125 [ID-2017] Resilience is... ▼
A)The ability to restore capability after degradation.

B)The ability to provide the required capability when facing adversity. ✓ Correct
This is the actual definition of resilience. INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, p. 180.
C)The ability to reduce exposure to adversity.
This relates just to "avoid", one of the fundamental objectives of resilience. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, pp. 180 and 181.
D)The ability to resist capability degradation.
This relates just to "withstand", one of the fundamental objectives of resilience. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, pp. 180 and 181.
126 [ID-2037] Which of the following characteristics indicates that a particular system of interest can better be viewed as a system of systems? ▼
A)Geographical distribution of stakeholders.
The geographical distribution of stakeholders is quite a common factor and is no indication at all that SoI is a SoS. On the other hand, geographical distribution of constituent systems would indeed suggest that the subject is a System of Systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.3.6, p.235.
B)All the other options are wrong. ✓ Correct
See the other explanations.
C)Complexity of constituent systems.
Complexity alone does not necessarily mean that it should be viewed as a system of systems. Complexity can be present in both single systems and systems of systems, so it is not a distinctive characteristic for this categorization. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.3.6, p.235.
D)Budgetarily independence of constituent systems.
Budgetary independence of constituent systems is not a defining factor for categorizing a system as a system of systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.3.6, p.235.
127 [ID-2066] Which of the following is an engineered system? ▼
A)An acquisition agreement ✓ Correct
An acquisition agreement is an engineered service system, composed of interacting components designed to fulfill a specific function. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.1., p.3.
B)Economic market
The complex interactions within an economic market might appear to be organized and engineered, but they are emergent systems resulting from countless individual transactions and decisions made by people, not a single engineered design. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.1., p.3.
C)The solar system
The solar system is a natural astronomical system formed through gravitational forces and other natural processes, not by human design or engineering. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.1., p.3.
D)A beehive
While a beehive has a complex structure and organization, it is a natural biological system created by bees through instinctual behavior, not by human design or engineering. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.1., p.3.
128 [ID-2072] What is a key challenge in applying Systems Engineering (SE) within the automotive industry? ▼
A)The lack of standardized regulations and safety requirements across different markets.
While regulatory differences exist, the automotive industry is highly standardized, with frameworks like ISO 26262 (Functional Safety) and ISO/SAE 21434 (Cybersecurity Engineering) guiding development​. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.1, pp.245-247
B)The short product development cycles, which eliminate the need for rigorous SE processes.
While product development cycles are shorter than in aerospace or defense, this does not eliminate the need for SE. Instead, SE helps manage fast-paced innovation while maintaining safety and reliability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.1, pp.245-247
C)The absence of reuse strategies, forcing manufacturers to redesign systems for every new model.
The automotive industry has long embraced reuse strategies, such as common platforms and modular architectures, to reduce costs and improve efficiency​. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.1, pp.245-247
D)The complexity introduced by electrification, connectivity, autonomy, and consumer demands. ✓ Correct
The primary challenge in applying SE within the automotive sector is managing growing complexity due to electrification, on-board connectivity, increasing levels of automation, and the evolving demands of consumers​. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.1, pp.245-247
129 [ID-2027] Fill in the blank:_______ is an approach that identifies, protects from, detects, responds to and recovers from anomalous/disruptive events. ▼

Human Systems Integration is an approach that aims to effectively integrate humans, technology and organizations. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.4., p.168.
B)System Safety Engineering
System safety engineering is an approach that reduces the likelihood of harm to people, assets, and the wider environment. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.11., p.185.
C)Resilience Engineering
Resilience Engineering is an approach to allow the system to provide capability when facing adversity. It is important to focus on the bold terms to differentiate Resilience from Security. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.9., p.180.
D)System Security Engineering ✓ Correct
System Security Engineering is an approach that identifies, protects from, detects, responds to and recovers from anomalous/disruptive events. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.12., p.190.
130 [ID-2046] Which of the following is NOT a Systems Engineering principle? ▼
A)Systems Engineering does not support system's operation and maintenance. ✓ Correct
Systems Engineering principles encompass operation and maintenance during the utilization/support stages and also in the previous life cycle stages, in order to prepare the system for its useful life. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.
B)Complex systems are engineered by complex organizations.
Not all organizations may have the expertise or resources to effectively engineer complex systems. Systems Engineering principles recognize the need for collaboration and coordination among various disciplines and stakeholders (thus, a complex organization) to successfully engineer complex systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.
C)Systems Engineering integrates engineering and scientific disciplines effectively.
By combining different areas of expertise, Systems Engineering can develop comprehensive solutions that consider all aspects of the system. Somebody has to do this work and it is up to the systems engineers to do it. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.
D)Systems Engineering manages interactions between disciplines within an organization.
Systems Engineering focuses on managing interactions between different disciplines within an organization to ensure that all aspects of the system are considered and integrated effectively. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.

132 [ID-2019] Choose the option that does not present a generic life cycle stage according to ISO/IEC/IEEE 15288. ▼
A)Utilization
"Utilization" is a generic life cycle stage that refers to the period during which the system is actively being used for its intended purpose. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, pp.28.
B)Retirement
"Retirement" is a generic life cycle stage that marks the end of a system's operational life. It involves decommissioning the system and transitioning it to another destination. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, p.29.
C)Development
"Development" is a generic life cycle stage according to ISO/IEC/IEEE 15288. In this period, the engineering design effort is performed until the solution can be built under acceptable risks. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, p.28.
D)Maintenance ✓ Correct
"Maintenance" is a system life cycle process, not a system life cycle stage. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, pp.26-29.
133 [ID-2001] Select the correct answer regarding iteration and recursion between system life cycle processes. ▼
A)Iteration happens between two processes in a given hierarchical level. Recursion happens when the same process is applied to successive hierarchical levels. ✓ Correct
Iteration occurs between two processes at a given hierarchical level, while recursion occurs when the same process is applied to successive hierarchical levels. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.1.2, pp.42-44.
B)Iteration happens when the same process is applied to successive hierarchical levels. Recursion happens when the two processes interact at the same hierarchical level.
It inaccurately states that iteration happens when the same process is applied to successive hierarchical levels, which is actually the definition of recursion. Additionally, it incorrectly states that recursion happens when the two processes are applied in the hierarchical level while this relates to iteration instead. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.1.2, pp.42-44.
C)Iteration and recursion occur when the same process is applied to successive hierarchical levels.
It is true for recursion, but not for iteration. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.1.2, pp.42-44.
D)Iteration happens between two processes in a given hierarchical level. Recursion is the parallel application of different processes in the same hierarchical level.
Recursion involves applying the same process to successive hierarchical levels, not parallel application of different processes at the same level. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.1.2, pp.42-44.

Indeed, the two types of prototypes are rapid and traditional. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.2, p.200.
B)Prototypes are the “first draft” of a production entity.
They are not. They are used for enhanced learning and should be set aside when their purposes are fulfilled. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.2, p.200.

3D printing is actualling expanding prototype options. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.2, p.201.

135 [ID-2065] A model that is expressed in a language that is machine readable with explicitly defined semantics is called: ▼
A)A physical model.
A physical model represents a physical object or system in a tangible form, such as a prototype or mock-up. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.1, p.196.
B)A digital model.
A digital model refers to a model that exists in a digital format, but it does not necessarily imply that the model is expressed in a language with explicitly defined semantics. Digital models can be either formal or informal. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.1, p.196.
C)A formal model. ✓ Correct
A formal model is expressed in a language that is machine-readable and has explicitly defined semantics. This type of model allows for precise analysis, verification, and validation of system requirements and designs. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.1, p.196.
D)An informal model.
An informal model lacks the precision and rigor of a formal model. It is typically expressed in natural language or diagrams that may be open to interpretation, making it less suitable for rigorous analysis and verification compared to a formal model. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.1, p.196.

There is a correct option to be chosen among the others.

The system architecture provides a basic framework upon which the design activities are performed. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.5., p.125.

This is the purpose of the implementation process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.5., p.132.
D)It focuses on developing a design solution detailed enough to allow the development of a functional architecture.
The design should be detailed enough to allow the realisation of the system. System architectures (e.g. functional, physical) function as an input for the design definition process (even though there might be some iteration). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.5., p.127.

138 [ID-2034] Considering the SE relationship with Project Management (PM), it is correct to say that: ▼
A)Both project managers and SE practitioners tend to focus on the same stakeholders.
While project managers and SE practitioners may have some overlap in terms of stakeholders, project managers tend to focus on project stakeholders (e.g. sponsors, executives) while SE practitioners focus on system stakeholders (e.g. designers, maintainers, operators). Therefore, it is incorrect to say that both project managers and SE practitioners tend to focus on the same stakeholders. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.3.3, pp.268 and 269.
B)SE and PM have an intersection, but the agreement processes are not part of it.
Agreement processes involve establishing agreements, contracts, and communication channels between stakeholders, which is a shared area between Systems Engineering and Project Management. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.3.3, pp.268 and 269.
C)All the other options are wrong. ✓ Correct
Indeed. See the other explanations.
D)SE and PM have no intersection.
SE and PM are closely related disciplines that often overlap in practice. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.3.3, pp.268 and 269. See Broadly speaking, SE is more focused on the technical aspects such as requirements, design and verification, while PM focuses on managing resources, schedules, and budgets to ensure project success. Both disciplines work together to achieve project goals and deliver successful outcomes.

D)Activate projects.
The portfolio management process is the one responsible for authorizing project execution, but the initiation is done by the project planning process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.4.1, p.71.
146 [ID-2078] Which of the following approaches is focused on ensuring that a system can function under disruptive events associated with malicious behaviour? ▼
A)Reliability, Availability, Maintainability Engineering
RAM Engineering focuses on enabling the system to perform without failure, to be operational when required, and to be easily maintained. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, p.176.
B)System Safety Engineering
System Safety Engineering is focused on mitigating hazards of systems to an acceptable level of risk. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.11, p.185.
C)System Security Engineering ✓ Correct
System Security Engineering is explicitly focused on ensuring that a system can operate effectively in the presence of disruptive events associated with malicious behavior, such as cyberattacks or sabotage. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.12, p.190.
D)Sustainability Engineering
The focus of Sustainability Engineering is to support the circular economy over the system’s life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.10, p.184.
147 [ID-2010] Which of the following is NOT an input of the validation process? ▼
A)Verification report
The verification report is an input to the validation process, as it provides information on the results of the verification activities that have been conducted to ensure that the system meets its requirements prior to the validation activities. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.11., p.146.
B)Validation criteria
"Validation criteria" is both an input (a reference to be used to perform validation) and an output ( a result of preparing for validation). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.11., p.146.
C)Installed system
The system itself usually has to be installed to be ultimately validated. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.11., p.146.
D)System requirements ✓ Correct
System requirements serve as input (and as reference) to the verification process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.11., p.146 and section 2.3.5.9, p.138.

A)Measure of success
This is a made up term.
B)Measure of Performance
Measure of Performance assesses how well the system meets the effectiveness criteria. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.7., p.97.

149 [ID-2058] Which of the following traits is NOT associated with hard skills? ▼
A)Measurable
Hard skills tend to be quantifiable, observable, and objectively measured through assessments, tests, or demonstrations. This makes them more concrete and verifiable compared to soft skills. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.1.1, p.263.
B)Individual application
Soft skills are typically related to interpersonal interactions and communication, rather than individual application, to which the hard skills are more suited. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.1.1, p.263.
C)The traits mentioned in the other options are all related to hard skills. ✓ Correct
Indeed. See the other explanations.
D)Concrete definitions
Soft skills are difficult to test or measure objectively compared to hard skills, which are more technical and quantifiable in nature. Evaluating soft skills often involves subjective assessments, observations of behavior, and feedback from others, making them challenging to assess in a standardized way. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.1.1, p.263.
150 [ID-2069] The temporal evolution of a system’s state is called: ▼
A)Dynamic behavior. ✓ Correct
Dynamic behavior refers to the temporal evolution of a system's state over time as it interacts with its environment and operates according to its designed intent. The dynamic behavior captures how the system's state changes in response to inputs, disturbances, or internal processes. This includes changes in performance, configurations, or operational conditions. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.1, p.9.
B)Temporal behavior.
This is a made up term.
C)Emergent behavior.
Emergent behavior refers to system behaviors that arise from the interaction of elements which is not observed in these elements individually. Emergent behavior is a key concept in Systems Engineering, particularly in complex systems, but it is not specifically related to the temporal evolution of a system's state. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.1, p.9.
D)Evolutionary behavior.
This is a made up term.
151 [ID-2056] A CAD (Computer Aided Design) software used to elaborate the design of a SoI is an example of a: ▼
A)Interfacing system
An interfacing system is an external system that interfaces with the SoI, which is not necessarily the case of the CAD software. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.3, p.10.
B)Interoperating system
An interoperating system is a system that interfaces with the SoI in its operational environment to perform a common function that contributes to achieving the purpose of the SoI. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.3, p.10.
C)All the other options are wrong.
There is a correct option to be chosen among the others.
D)Enabling system ✓ Correct
Enabling systems are external systems that facilitate a life cycle activity of the SoI but are not an element of its operational environment. The CAD software in this case facilitates the design activities of the SoI. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.3, p.10.
152 [ID-2073] Choose the WRONG option regarding the uncertainty related to a system. ▼
A)Epistemic uncertainty is due to lack of knowledge.
Epistemic uncertainty is caused by lack of knowledge about system behavior, performance, or environmental factors. It can be reduced through additional data collection, research, and testing​.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.4.1, pp. 15 and 16.
B)There is no other type of uncertainty besides “epistemic” and “aleatory”
The handbook clearly defines only two types of uncertainty:- Epistemic (knowledge-based, reducible).- Aleatory (randomness-based, irreducible).No other fundamental categories of uncertainty are mentioned. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.4.1, pp. 15 and 16.
C)Some uncertainties cannot be eliminated even by following the SE process.
The handbook states that uncertainty is always present in system performance, external environments, and operational conditions. While SE processes aim to reduce uncertainty, some aspects (especially aleatory uncertainty) remain unavoidable​. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.4.1, pp. 15 and 16.
D)Aleatory uncertainty is reduced with increased knowledge about the system. ✓ Correct
Aleatory uncertainty is due to randomness and inherent variability, meaning it cannot be reduced by gaining more knowledge. This is a fundamental distinction in SE, as opposed to epistemic uncertainty, which can be mitigated. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.4.1, pp. 15 and 16.
153 [ID-2068] The following are within the scope of Logistics Engineering, except ▼
A)To design a system for supportability.
Supportability is a key aspect of Logistics Engineering, which involves ensuring that the system can be supported effectively and efficiently throughout its life cycle. This includes designing systems with maintenance in mind, ensuring that spare parts, tools, personnel, and procedures are readily available to sustain operations. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.181.
B)To determine requirements for the system’s logistics support.
Determining the logistics support requirements is a key activity within Logistics Engineering. It involves identifying the necessary resources, processes, and strategies to support the system throughout its operational life. This includes defining maintenance requirements, spare parts inventory, personnel training, and support infrastructure. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.181.
C)To determine the stakeholder requirements of a logistics support system. ✓ Correct
Determining stakeholder requirements is within the scope of Requirements Engineering and is something done by stakeholder needs and requirements definition process, where the needs and expectations of stakeholders (e.g., end-users, operators, maintainers) are captured and translated into system requirements. While Logistics Engineers may use these requirements to design support strategies, the actual process of eliciting and determining stakeholder requirements is not a primary responsibility of Logistics Engineering. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.181.
D)To acquire system’s support elements.
Acquiring system support elements (such as spare parts, maintenance tools, technical data, and training) falls under Integrated Logistics Support (ILS) and is part of the broader scope of Logistics Engineering. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.181.
154 [ID-2055] Choose the life cycle model approach has the following characteristics:- requirements are known from the beginning;- potential multiple deployments; and- planned multiple iterations. ▼
A)Evolutionary approach
In an evolutionary approach, we have:- requirements are not known from the beginning;- typically multiple deployments; and- planned multiple iterations.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2, p.34.
B)Incremental approach ✓ Correct
In an incremental approach, we have:- requirements are known from the beginning;- potential multiple deployments; and- planned multiple iterations.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2, p.34.
C)Sequential approach
In a sequential approach, we have:- requirements are known from the beginning;- single deployment; and- single planned iteration.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2, p.34.
D)systemic approach
A made up term.

Agile Systems Engineering is a principle-based approach to design, build, sustain, and evolve systems under uncertainty. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.2, p.221.

D)Product Line Engineering

158 [ID-3087] Choose the right option regarding the uncertainty related to a system. ▼
A)Aleatory uncertainty cannot be completely reduced during the SE process. ✓ Correct
Aleatory uncertainty is caused by inherent randomness in system behavior, materials, environment, or operational conditions.No amount of additional knowledge or analysis can completely reduce it. ​See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.4.1, p. 15
B)There are 3 types of uncertainty: epistemic, probabilistic and aleatory.
The handbook defines only two types of uncertainty:Epistemic uncertainty – due to lack of knowledge and can be reduced with more information.Aleatory uncertainty – due to randomness and cannot be reduced.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.4.1, p. 15.

Uncertainties can be mitigated. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.4.1, p. 15.
D)Epistemic uncertainty cannot be completely mitigated during the SE process.
Epistemic uncertainty can be completely mitigated through additional data collection, system modeling, experimentation, and analysis. SE processes specifically aim to reduce epistemic uncertainty by increasing knowledge​. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.4.1, p. 15.
159 [ID-3069] Choose the option that best decribes the concept of "system interface". ▼

B)A shared boundary where two systems meet and interact. ✓ Correct

A user interface (UI) refers to the means through which a user interacts with a software application, such as screens, buttons, and menus. It is a specific type of interface that does not generally describe the interaction boundary between two systems.

The concept described in choice B is more aligned with the system context rather than system interfaces. The system context includes all external factors that influence or impact a system, such as stakeholders, regulations, environment, and constraints.

162 [ID-3090] MTBF is a metric for (choose the best answer): ▼
A)Reliability ✓ Correct
MTBF stands for Mean Time Between Failures, and it is a standard metric used to quantify reliability — specifically, the expected time between one failure and the next during normal system operation. It is applicable to repairable systems and reflects how often a system fails over time. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, p.177.
B)Mantainability
Maintainability is about how quickly and easily a system can be restored to full operation after a failure. The key metric for maintainability is MTTR (Mean Time To Repair), not MTBF. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, p.179.
C)Availability
While MTBF is part of the formula for availability MTBF itself does not directly measure availability by itself — it's just one of its inputs. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, pp.177-179.
D)Safety
MTBF does not measure safety, though higher reliability can contribute to overall system safety. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.11, pp.186 and 187.
163 [ID-3070] Which of the following audits is used to confirm that the configuration documentation accurately reflects the operational system? ▼
A)Functional Configuration Audit (FCA)
A Functional Configuration Audit (FCA) is conducted to verify that the system's functionality aligns with its specified performance requirements and functional documentation.
B)Compliance Audit
A Compliance Audit ensures that the system or process adheres to relevant laws, regulations, and standards. While it may include aspects of configuration, its primary focus is on regulatory compliance rather than verifying configuration documentation.
C)Physical Configuration Audit ✓ Correct
A Physical Configuration Audit (PCA) is conducted to verify that the as-built system or product matches its design documentation and specifications. This includes ensuring that the physical configuration of the system aligns with the documentation. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.314.
D)Process Audit
A Process Audit examines the procedures and methods used during the execution of processes to ensure they comply with predefined standards and practices. It does not specifically focus on the configuration of the system.

This ensures that the standard processes are implemented in a way that aligns with the organization's specific needs and objectives. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.1, pp.215-219.

173 [ID-3066] All of the following sectors have a "repair" approach to maintenance, EXCEPT by one that favours replacement. This EXCEPTION is: ▼
A)Aerospace
The aerospace sector typically opts for a repair approach to maintenance, where aircraft components are repaired or replaced to ensure safety and reliability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4, p.246.
B)Automotive
The automotive sector typically follows a repair approach to maintenance, where components are fixed or replaced as needed to keep vehicles running. This approach is cost-effective and practical for the automotive industry. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4, p.246.
C)Consumer electronics ✓ Correct
Consumer electronics sector tends to favor a replacement approach to maintenance rather than repair. In this sector, it is often more cost-effective and efficient to replace faulty electronic devices rather than attempting to repair them. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4, p.246.
D)Defense
The defense sector commonly utilizes a repair approach to maintenance, where military equipment and systems are repaired or replaced to ensure operational readiness. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4, p.246.
174 [ID-3004] Which of the following best defines Systems Engineering? ▼
A)A transdisciplinary approach that focuses on the realization of successful systems. ✓ Correct
Systems Engineering integrates and transcends multiple disciplines to address complex systems problems. It goes beyond the boundaries of individual disciplines to provide a holistic and comprehensive perspective on system development, management, support and other related issues. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, pp.1 and 2.
B)A discipline that is heavily focused on the business needs of a system.
Systems Engineering does consider the business needs of a system, but it is not solely focused on them. It takes into account a wide range of factors, including technical, operational and organizational aspects to ensure the overall success of the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, pp.1 and 2.
C)A set of tools and techniques used for engineering design.
Systems Engineering is not just about engineering design. It involves a holistic approach that addresses and integrates various topics (acquisition, construction, maintenance, retirement) across the system lifecycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, pp.1 and 2.
D)A discipline that focuses solely on the technical design of systems.
Systems Engineering is not solely focused on the technical design of systems. It also takes into account various business, organizational and managerial aspects. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, pp.1 and 2.

C)Complex systems can be engineered by any organization.
Not all organizations may have the expertise or resources to effectively engineer complex systems. Systems Engineering principles recognize the need for collaboration and coordination among various disciplines and stakeholders (thus, a complex organization) to successfully engineer complex systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.
D)SE holistic view encompasses the systems elements, enabling systems, the environment and all their interactions. ✓ Correct
This choice accurately describes a Systems Engineering principle by emphasizing the importance of taking a holistic view that includes all elements of a system, enabling systems, the environment, and their interactions. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.

179 [ID-3089] Which of the following sentences is correct about the application of SE in defense systems? ▼
A)Defense systems must function in extreme environments, require high availability, and often have long life cycles. ✓ Correct
Defense systems are designed to operate in extreme conditions (deserts, Arctic, underwater, space), must be highly available, and often have life cycles lasting decades, requiring robust SE approaches​. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.4, pp.250 and 251.
B)Defense systems require minimal human interaction, as they are increasingly automated and autonomous.
While automation is increasing, human interaction remains a critical factor in defense systems. Human-systems integration (HSI) is essential for usability, decision-making, and operational effectiveness in high-stakes scenarios​. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.4, pp.250 and 251.
C)Defense systems are primarily concerned with rapid prototyping and short development cycles, similar to commercial technology industries.
While some defense projects require rapid deployment in emergency situations, the majority involve long development cycles due to extensive testing, security, and integration requirements. Unlike commercial industries, defense systems must prioritize robustness and interoperability over speed​. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.4, pp.250 and 251.
D)Defense systems have relatively simple logistics requirements compared to other engineered systems.
Defense systems often require complex logistical planning, as they must be deployed, maintained, and sustained in diverse operational environments. Long life cycles and global deployment increase the importance of logistics. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.4, pp.250 and 251.

D)There should be no integration activities during utilization and support stages.

182 [ID-3083] The highest increase in % of committed LCC occurs during: ▼
A)Development stage
While significant design work occurs during the development stage, most of the critical cost-committing decisions have already been made during the concept stage. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 6-8.
B)Concept stage ✓ Correct
The concept stage is when the highest percentage of LCC is determined. During this phase, key decisions are made regarding the system's architecture, requirements, and fundamental design approach, which lock in a large portion of the system's overall costs. While actual expenditures may not be high during this stage, decisions made here can determine up to 70-80% of the total life cycle cost. Once these decisions are made, later stages incur costs based on the groundwork established in the concept phase. This is why systems engineering emphasizes the importance of thorough planning and analysis in the concept stage to minimize downstream costs. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 6-8.
C)Utilization stage
By the time a system reaches the utilization stage, virtually all costs have been locked in, and decisions made in earlier stages (especially in concept and development) drive these costs. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 6-8.
D)Production stage
During the production stage, the system is manufactured and prepared for operational use, meaning most costs have already been committed. This stage primarily involves capitalizing on the design and decisions made in previous phases. While production can involve high actual costs, the percentage of committed LCC does not significantly increase at this point, as the system’s design is already finalized, and changes are expensive and difficult to implement. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 6-8.
183 [ID-3027] Fill in the blanks with the best option:Given today's complexity and multidisciplinarity, plus the fact that is not possible for a single person to know everything about a SoI, Systems Engineering practioners should have both a deep knowledge of ______________________, and a breadth of knowledge about ___________________. ▼
A)Systems Engineering; all classical engineering disciplines.
This profile lacks the deep understanding of a classical/fundamental engineering discipline. Besides, "all classical engineering disciplines" still doesn't cover everything that is involved when the subject is a complex system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5, p.262.
B)Systems Engineering; a fundamental engineering discipline.
This is not enough as there are usually many other disciplines involved when the subject is a complex system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5, p.261.
C)systems and the multiple disciplines involved; a classical engineering discipline.
It should be the other way around, as it is unfeasible for the SE practitioner to have deep knowledge about everything. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5, p.261.
D)a fundamental engineering discipline; systems and the multiple disciplines involved. ✓ Correct
This is what is called a "T-shaped" expertise and is the usual required profile of a SE practitioner. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5, p.262.

D)Attribute ✓ Correct
Attributes are the characteristics or features of a system that can be observed. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.1, p.9.

189 [ID-3084] In Logistics Engineering, the element training and training support refers to: ▼

The training and training support element ensures that both maintainers and operators are equipped with the knowledge, skills, and resources they need to interact with the system effectively. However, training in Logistics Engineering covers both operators and maintainers, not just operators. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.6, p.173.

190 [ID-3019] Choose the WRONG sentence: ▼

192 [ID-3085] Choose the correct option regarding emergence. ▼
A)All the other options are wrong. ✓ Correct
Indeed. See the other explanations.
B)Individual elements can exhibit higher level-system emergence.
Emergence refers to system-level properties that arise from the interaction of multiple elements within a system, not from an individual element alone. The emergent properties are not exhibited by individual elements but rather by the system as a whole, as a result of the collaboration and interaction among its components. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.2, pp. 9 and 10.
C)Emergence can occur in a level below the elements involved.
Emergence occurs at a higher system level than the individual elements involved. Emergent behaviors or properties are observed at a level higher than that of the interacting elements, not at a lower level. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.2, pp. 9 and 10.
D)One element can produce emergence by itself.
Emergence is a phenomenon that arises from the interaction between two or more elements in a system. No single element can produce emergent properties on its own. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.3.2, pp. 9 and 10.

B)Adhesion to system requirements is increased. ✓ Correct
Adhesion to system requirements is not expected to be increased by adopting COTS solutions. Customization or integration may be required to ensure full compliance with system requirements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.3.3, p.231.
C)Cost to market is reduced.
Cost to market can be reduced by adopting COTS solutions as they eliminate the need for extensive development and production activities, allowing products to be brought to market faster and at a lower cost. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.3.3, p.231.
D)Development time is reduced
Adopting COTS solutions can reduce development time as these solutions are already available, saving time on building a custom system element from scratch. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.3.3, p.231.

197 [ID-3041] Fill in the blank:In a ____________ system, relationships are easy to understand and the relation of cause and effect is straightforward. ▼
A)Atomic
An atomic system refers to a system that, for some reason, is not broken down further into smaller components and works like a "black box". It doesn't say anything about the system's complexity.
B)Simple ✓ Correct
In a simple system, the relationships between elements are straightforward and easy to understand. There is little uncertainty between cause and effect, making it easier to comprehend and predict outcomes within the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.7, p.15.
C)Complicated
A complicated system may have many interconnected parts, but the relationships between elements are more structured and deterministic compared to a complex system. In a complicated system, cause-and-effect relationships are more predictable with reduced levels of uncertainty. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.7, p.15.
D)Complex
In a complex system, the relationships between elements are intricate, leading to a high level of uncertainty and unpredictability. This complexity makes it challenging to fully comprehend the system and determine clear cause-and-effect relationships. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.7, p.15.
198 [ID-3081] Choose the right answer regarding the ROI (program cost reduction per SE investment) of SE activities within a program. ▼
A)After reaching an optimum level of SE investment, the ROI decreases, but it is always positive.
The ROI can become negative beyond a certain level of SE effort. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 5 and 6.
B)The ROI can become negative after a certain level of SE investment. ✓ Correct
After a certain threshold, additional SE investments not only lead to diminishing returns but can also lead to a negative ROI. This may happen due to the implementation of excessive, unuseful processes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 5 and 6.
C)There is no relation between SE effort and program cost.
SE activities are specifically aimed at improving system performance, reliability, and efficiency, all of which are closely tied to program cost reduction. Therefore, there is indeed a strong relationship between SE efforts and program costs. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 5 and 6.
D)The more is the SE investment, the bigger is the ROI.
In reality, there is a diminishing return on investment after a certain point. Initial SE investments usually yield significant ROI, but continuously increasing the investment beyond an optimal level often results in smaller returns. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 5 and 6.
199 [ID-3068] The development of a system for a new environment with no significant legacy constraints related to the system boundary is called: ▼
