Brownfield systems engineering involves the development and integration of new systems or elements within an existing environment that already has legacy systems or constraints. This approach requires ensuring compatibility with the current infrastructure. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.312.
B)Incremental Systems Engineering
Incremental systems engineering would involve gradually adding new functionalities or components to an existing system. This approach would be more related to the integration of new elements with legacy systems.
C)Blankfield Systems Engineering
A made up term.
D)Greenfield Systems Engineering ✓ Correct
Greenfield systems engineering refers to the process of designing and implementing new systems or elements in a completely new environment, free from any existing infrastructure or legacy constraints. This approach allows for starting from a "clean slate." See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.313.

206 [ID-3032] Which of the following is a principal distinguishing characteristic of a System of Systems? ▼
A)Managerial independence of constituent systems. ✓ Correct
The managerial independence of constituent systems is a key characteristic of a System of Systems. In a System of Systems, each constituent system typically operates under its own management and control, with limited coordination or centralized authority. This independence can lead to challenges in integration and interoperability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.3.6, pp.235-238.
B)Geographical distribution of constituent systems.
Though geographical distribution of constituent systems is usually observed in an SoS, it is not a principal distinguishing characteristic, those being: - operational independence of constituent systems; and- managerial independence of constituent systems.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.3.6, p.235.
C)Emergent behavior.
Though emergent behavior is usually observed in an SoS, it is not a principal distinguishing characteristic, those being:- operational independence of constituent systems; and- managerial independence of constituent systems.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.3.6, p.235.
D)Evolutionary development process
Though evolutionary development process is usually observed in an SoS, it is not a principal distinguishing characteristic, those being: - operational independence of constituent systems; and- managerial independence of constituent systems.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.3.6, p.235.
207 [ID-3088] Which of the following best describes the role of sustainability engineering in systems engineering? ▼
A)Sustainability engineering ensures that a system minimizes its environmental impact, even if it compromises performance, cost, or schedule.
Sustainability engineering balances environmental, economic, and social factors. It does not require sacrificing performance or feasibility; rather, it seeks optimized trade-offs that align with long-term system value. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.10, pp.184 and 185.
B)Sustainability engineering is primarily concerned with reducing carbon emissions, with less focus on broader social and economic factors.
While carbon footprint is a key aspect, sustainability engineering encompasses a broader scope, including social responsibility, resource efficiency, economic viability, and ethical supply chains. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.10, pp.184 and 185.
C)Sustainability engineering supports the circular economy by integrating environmental, social, and economic factors across the system life cycle. ✓ Correct
Sustainability engineering aligns with circular economy principles, promoting resource efficiency and responsible design to ensure systems remain viable, adaptable, and minimally wasteful throughout their life cycle​. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.10, pp.184 and 185.
D)Sustainability engineering primarily addresses system disposal and end-of-life strategies rather than influencing design and operation.
While end-of-life strategies are crucial, sustainability engineering starts at the earliest stages of system development. It influences materials selection, energy efficiency, supply chain choices, and long-term resilience, not just disposal. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.10, pp.184 and 185.
208 [ID-3030] Which of the following is considered to be a system modeling language? ▼
A)OMG
OMG (Object Management Group) is a technology standards consortium that develops and maintains standards for software and systems modeling, including languages like UML and SysML. It is not a system modeling language itself.
B)MBSE
MBSE (Model-Based Systems Engineering) is an approach to system engineering that focuses on creating and using models as a primary means of communication and understanding within a project or organization. It is not a system modeling language itself.
C)SysML ✓ Correct
SysML (Systems Modeling Language) is a general-purpose modeling language for systems engineering applications. It extends the Unified Modeling Language (UML) with specific constructs for systems engineering, making it a widely used system modelling language in the industry. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.1, p. 220.
D)OOSEM
OOSEM stands for Object-Oriented Systems Engineering Method and it is not a modelling language.
209 [ID-3065] The invention of velcro based on the observation of bur seeds of the burdock plant is an example of: ▼
A)Biomimicry ✓ Correct
Biomimicry is the practice that aims to learn and mimic nature's strategies to solve human's design challenges. This is the right choice. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.8, p.213.
B)Biophilia
Biophilia refers to forging the connection between human and nature by leveraging or inserting instances of nature, natural patterns, or spatial conditions into the built environment.
C)Biometrics
Biometrics refers to the application of statistical analysis to biological data.
D)Bioengineering
Bioengineering is a discipline that applies engineering principles of design and analysis to biological systems and biomedical technologies.

213 [ID-3072] Which of the following statements best reflects the approach taken in developing an engineered system? ▼
A)Engineered systems are designed to be flexible and adapt to changing requirements without any predefined purpose.
Although flexibility can be a relevant aspect, engineered systems are developed with a specific purpose and predefined requirements, ensuring all components work together to achieve that purpose.
B)Engineered systems are developed through a trial-and-error process to find the most effective configuration.
While iterative testing may be part of the development process, engineered systems are primarily developed through systematic planning and design, not trial and error.
C)Engineered systems are created using a structured methodology that integrates various components to meet specific user needs and operational requirements. ✓ Correct
This statement best captures the development approach for engineered systems, which involves using a structured methodology to integrate components to meet specific needs and requirements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.1, pp.2 and 3.
D)Engineered systems evolve naturally over time without a formal development process, based on user feedback and operational experience.
Engineered systems are typically developed through a formal, structured process, not through natural evolution. User feedback and operational experience are important, but the development process is intentional and systematic.

220 [ID-3062] In relation to an airplane, its flight simulator being used for training is an example of a: ▼

An interoperating system is a system that interfaces with the SoI in its operational environment to perform a common function that contributes to achieving the purpose of the SoI. D)All the other options are wrong.
There is a correct option to be chosen among the others.

224 [ID-3082] Which of the following statements can be made about projects that properly apply systems engineering best practices? ▼
A)They are cheaper and faster than projects that do not.
This option is misleading because SE is not guaranteed to make projects both cheaper and faster. While it may lead to more efficient outcomes, SE aims at improving project success by ensuring quality and meeting stakeholder requirements, even if it means a higher upfront cost or a longer initial timeline. SE's real value lies in managing complexity and avoiding pitfalls that can lead to delays or cost overruns later, not necessarily in making projects faster or cheaper from the outset. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 6-8.
B)They resolve faster than projects that do not.
Projects using SE practices might take longer in the initial phases due to the thorough analysis and planning required. It emphasizes the importance of early-stage efforts to define requirements, identify risks, and plan the system's life cycle, which can delay early milestones but leads to smoother execution later on. While this may seem slower at first, the process ultimately mitigates risks and helps avoid delays that could arise from unforeseen technical challenges or requirement mismatches, but SE is not designed specifically to accelerate project resolution. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 6-8.
C)They perform better than projects that do not. ✓ Correct
Systems engineering ensures that all aspects of the system are considered from a holistic perspective, minimizing rework, enhancing communication between stakeholders, and reducing the chances of failure due to unforeseen issues. Projects that properly apply SE demonstrate superior performance in terms of meeting stakeholder needs and also tend to maintain better schedule/cost discipline due to a more thorough planning that considers all systemic aspects involved. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 6-8.
D)They are cheaper than projects that do not.
While SE practices can reduce costs in the long run by preventing costly rework or late-stage design changes, it is not guaranteed that the project will be cheaper. Systems engineering often requires a significant upfront investment in planning, requirements analysis, and design. The focus is on cost-effectiveness, meaning that the additional investment in SE can lead to better outcomes, but it may not necessarily result in immediate cost savings. Projects that skip SE may initially seem cheaper but often face cost overruns later or fail to deliver a system that is in accordance with the stakeholder requirements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp. 6-8.

232 [ID-3059] The spiral model known as ICSM is an example of a: ▼

C)Incremental approach ✓ Correct
The Incremmental Commitment Spiral Model (ICSM) is an incremental approach. In an incremental approach, there are incremental potential deliveries even though requirements are already known from the beginning. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.36 and 37.

In a sequential approach, there is a single delivery and the SE activities are broken down into linear sequential stages where each of these stages depend on the deliverables of previous stages and on the feedback from subsequent stages. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.35.

B)any work product. ✓ Correct
The verification process is applied to any work product, including documents, models, code, requirements and other artifacts produced during the system life cycle. Its purpose is to ensure that these work products are done right. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.138.
C)the system and its elements, nothing else.
The verification process is not limited to just the system and its elements; it encompasses all work products associated with the system life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.138.
D)system level only.
While the verification extends not only to all levels of the system hierarchy but to any related work product that is realized during the system's life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.138.

A)Able to be validated: the requirement is structured and worded in a way that allows its implementation to be verified.

236 [ID-3002] Choose the WRONG sentence regarding the generic system life cycle stages. ▼
A)In the production stage, the System of Interest becomes reality.
This stage involves the actual manufacturing, construction and/or assembly of the system components to bring the system to life. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, p.29.
B)The utilization stage of a system begins with its transition to use.
The utilization stage begins when the system (at least a part of it) is put into use. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, p.28.
C)The support stage ends only when the system ends its useful life. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, p.28. ✓ Correct
The support stage may end earlier, deliberately choosing to end the system's support before the utilization stage ends. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, p.29.
D)In the retirement stage, the system is removed from operation.
In the retirement stage, the system is no longer active or functioning in its intended capacity. It is essentially being decommissioned and is no longer in use. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, p.29.
237 [ID-3063] Choose the WRONG option regarding prototyping ▼
A)Prototypes are not the "first draft" of production entities.
Prototypes shouldn't be treated as the "first draft" of production entities. They are used to explore and validate design concepts and functionalities before the final product is developed. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.2, pp.200 and 201.
B)Even a full prototype does not completely represent a system. ✓ Correct
By the definition, a prototype is only considered to be a 'full prototype" when it functions as a complete representation of its related system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.2, p.201.
C)A partial prototype should be enough for verifying critical elements of a system.
This is exactly one of the main purposes of a partial prototype. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.2, p.201.
D)There are two types of prototyping: rapid and traditional.
Rapid prototypes are quick, low-fidelity representations of the final system that allow for fast feedback and iteration, while traditional prototypes follow a more structured and formal approach to develop a representation of the referenced system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.2, pp.200 and 201.

A good reasoning to adopt in order to associate the management processes to their right group would be: "If it relates more to the organization as a whole than it relates to the system/project, it is an organizational project enabling process. If it relates more to the system/project than it relates to the organization as a whole, it is a technical management process".

242 [ID-3092] Architecture frameworks are used to improve: ▼
A)the quality of system requirements.
Improving the quality of requirements is not the purpose of architecture frameworks, even though it could be an indirect gain. Architecture Frameworks are used to improve harmonization, consistency and reuse of architectures See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.5, p.206.
B)design speed and cost estimation accuracy of a system.
Architecture frameworks are not primarily focused on accelerating design timelines or producing more accurate cost estimates. These may be indirect benefits, but not the purpose of architecture frameworks. Architecture Frameworks are used to improve harmonization, consistency and reuse of architectures. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.5, p.206.
C)consistency and reuse of architectures. ✓ Correct
Architecture frameworks define standardized practices, principles, and conventions that improve the consistency of architectural descriptions and allow for reuse across systems and projects. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.5, p.206.
D)software code generation.
Architecture frameworks are not tools for generating software code. They guide architectural descriptions, not low-level implementation like coding. AArchitecture Frameworks are used to improve harmonization, consistency and reuse of architectures. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.2.5, p.206.
243 [ID-3008] System availability can be defined as: ▼
A)The ability os a system to perform in stated conditions, without failure, for a certain period of time.
This would be a definition for reliability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, p.178.
B)The ratio between the system's uptime and its total time. ✓ Correct
Availability = Uptime/(Uptime + Downtime). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, p.178.
C)The system mean time between failures.
The Mean Time Between Failures (MTBF) is a metric that measures the average time elapsed between failures.
D)A measure of how well the system related maintenance activities can be performed.
This would be a definition for maintainability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, p.179.
244 [ID-3054] Which of the following analyses is NOT useful to find failure modes and/or to classify their criticalities? ▼
A)FTA
Fault Tree Analysis (FTA) is a useful analysis method to identify potential failure modes by modeling the relationships between various events and their causes in a tree-like structure. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.6, p.174.
B)RBD
Reliability Block Diagram (RBD) is a graphical representation of the reliability of a system based on the reliability of its individual components and their interconnections. It is useful for analyzing the reliability of complex systems and identifying potential points of failure. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.6, p.174.
C)FMECA
Failure Modes, Effects, and Criticality Analysis (FMECA) is a systematic approach to identifying potential failure modes of a system, determining their effects on the system, and assessing the criticality of those failures. It helps in prioritizing the most critical failure modes for further mitigation. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.6, p.174.
D)LORA ✓ Correct
Level of Repair Analysis (LORA) is used to determine the optimum maintenance level (e.g. operator level maintenance, depot level maintenance) of a given maintenance task and is not designed to find failure modes and/or to classify their criticalities. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.6, p.174.
245 [ID-3071] Which of the following best describes a typical output of the System Analysis Process? ▼
A)A risk assessment and mitigation strategy document.
This relates to the risk management process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.4.4, pp.82 and 83.
B)A report summarizing the results of trade-off studies and sensitivity analyses. ✓ Correct
A report summarizing the results of trade-off studies and sensitivity analyses is a typical output of the system analysis process. This report provides insights and recommendations based on the technical evaluations conducted. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5.6, pp.129-132.
C)A set of detailed use cases and operational scenarios.
These are done by other technical processes (e.g. business or mission analysis process, stakeholder needs and requirements definition process).
D)A list of system requirements and high-level design concepts.
These are done by other technical processes (e.g. system requirements definition process).
246 [ID-3086] Which of the following is a major Systems Engineering challenge in the automotive industry? ▼
A)Eliminating all mechanical components from vehicles to transition to fully software-defined architectures.
While software-defined architectures are growing, mechanical components remain essential in vehicle design (e.g., braking, suspension, powertrain). SE must balance software integration with mechanical and electronic system interactions, not eliminate mechanics entirely. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.1, pp.245-247.
B)Avoiding regulatory compliance efforts to accelerate time-to-market for new vehicle models.
Regulatory compliance is mandatory in all major markets. SE plays a crucial role in ensuring compliance with evolving regulations, particularly for autonomous and electric vehicles, rather than avoiding these requirements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.1, pp.245-247.
C)Managing the increasing integration of software and electronic systems while ensuring compliance with safety and cybersecurity standards. ✓ Correct
Modern vehicles rely on complex electronic and software-based systems for automation, connectivity, and electrification. Systems Engineering is critical for integrating these technologies while ensuring compliance with regulations like ISO 26262 (Functional Safety) and ISO/SAE 21434 (Cybersecurity Engineering). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.1, pp.245-247.
D)Standardizing a single global automotive architecture to eliminate regional variations and customization.
Automotive architectures vary globally due to regional regulations, environmental factors, and consumer preferences. SE helps manage multiple vehicle platforms efficiently, rather than enforcing a single standardized architecture. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.1, pp.245-247.

A)Measurement process

B)Project assessment and control process
The Project assessment and control process focuses on monitoring and controlling project activities to ensure that they align with project plans and objectives. It is not specifically dedicated to assessing the life cycle processes themselves. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.4.2, p.75.
C)Life cycle model management process ✓ Correct
Assessing the life cycle process falls under the purview of this process and involves evaluating the effectiveness of the chosen life cycle model in achieving project goals and objectives. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.3.1, pp.51-54.
D)System analysis process
This process is focused on providing rigorous data and information to support assessments and decision making. It is not focused on assessing the life cycle processes, though it can support the Life cycle model management process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5.6, pp.129-132.

253 [ID-4064] In relation to an automobile, a car carrier taking the automobile to the workshop is an example of: ▼
A)An interoperable, interfacing and enabling system.
An interoperating system is a system that interfaces with the SoI in its operational environment to perform a common function that contributes to achieving the purpose of the SoI. The car carrier and the automobile are not perform a common function. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.3, pp.10 and 11.
B)An interoperable and enabling system.
An interoperating system is a system that interfaces with the SoI in its operational environment to perform a common function that contributes for achieving the purpose of the SoI. The car carrier and the automobile are not perform a common function. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.3, pp.10 and 11.
C)An interfacing and enabling system. ✓ Correct
Enabling systems are external systems that facilitate a life cycle activity of the SoI but are not an element of its operational environment. On the other hand, an interfacing system is an external system that interfaces with the SoI.The car carrier is an external system that has an interface with the automobile while carrying it, functioning as an enabling system that will facilitate the maintenance of the automobile. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.3, pp.10 and 11.
D)An interfacing and interoperable system.
An interoperating system is a system that interfaces with the SoI in its operational environment to perform a common function that contributes to achieving the purpose of the SoI. The car carrier and the automobile are not perform a common function. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.3, pp.10 and 11.
254 [ID-4104] Performing the verification of water pumping system after verifying its individual elements (pipelines, pump, control panel, etc) is an example of: ▼
A)Iteration
Iteration refers to repeating the application and interaction between two or more processes at the same hierarchy level of the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.1.2, pp.43 and 44. In the proposed scenario, a single process (verification) is applied in different levels.
B)Recursion ✓ Correct
Recursion refers to applying the same life cycle process at different levels of the system hierarchy. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.1.2, p.44. In this case, verification is first applied to individual system elements (e.g., pump, pipeline, control panel), and then again at the system level (the complete water pumping system). This is a textbook example of recursion — same process (verification), different hierarchy levels.
C)Validation
Validation is another life cycle process and is not mentioned or implied in the proposed scenario.
D)Concurrency
Concurrency involves performing two or processes in parallel at a given level of the system hierarchy. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.1.2, p.42. Only one process (verification) is being executed in the proposed scenario.
255 [ID-4075] Which of the following options best captures the definition of "system"? ▼
A)A group of elements that are being used together.
This definition gives room to lack of organization and interdependence. A system consists of organized, related components that interact and work together, not just a random collection of elements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.314.
B)A collective of products, people and processes.
This choice rightly includes products, people, and processes as part of a system, but it does not emphasize the interconnectedness and collaboration among these elements to achieve a common goal. The definition of a system goes beyond just the collection of components and involves understanding how they interact and work together. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.314.
C)A set of interconnected components that work together to achieve a common goal. ✓ Correct
This definition accurately captures the essence of a system, emphasizing the interconnectedness of its components and their collective effort to achieve a specific objective. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.314.
D)A system composed by other systems.
The definition of a system itself does not necessarily imply that it is composed of other systems. The primary definition of a system focuses on the interconnected components working together towards a common goal. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.314.
256 [ID-4012] Which statement best describes the purpose of Systems Engineering? ▼
A)To integrate and coordinate all aspects of a system to be realized in order to solve a problem. ✓ Correct
This choice accurately captures the holistic and systemic essence of Systems Engineering, which is to integrate and coordinate all aspects of a system to be realized in order to deliver a solution to a problem. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, pp. 1 and 2.
B)To focus on the detailed design and construction of individual components.
Focusing on the detailed design and construction of individual components is more aligned with traditional engineering disciplines rather than Systems Engineering. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, pp. 1 and 2.
C)To replace project management when the project relates to complex systems.
Project management and Systems Engineering are complementary disciplines that work together to ensure the successful delivery of complex systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4, p. 70.
D)To transcend involved disciplines and develop software solutions for complex engineering problems.
Systems Engineering doesn't have software development as its purpose. It is a broader discipline that encompasses and transcends various engineering domains, focusing on the integration and coordination of all aspects of a system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, pp.1 and 2.
257 [ID-4033] Choose the right sentence regarding the integration process: ▼
A)Vertical integration aims to ensure that system elements at a given hierarchy level are consistent with higher level system elements. ✓ Correct
Indeed. Vertical integration is applied between elements from different system hierarchy levels while horizontal integration is applied between elements from the same system hierarchy level. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.8, p.137.
B)There should be no integration activities during utilization and support stages.
Integration activities are essential throughout the entire system lifecycle, including the utilization and support stages (e.g. modifications, integration with enabling systems). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.8, pp.134-136.
C)All the other options are wrong.
There is a correct option to be chosen among the others.
D)The integration process should be executed independently and sequentially with the verification process and the validation process.
The integration process should be closely coordinated and iterated with the verification process and the validation process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.8, p.134.

260 [ID-4088] Which Systems Engineering approach is concerned with the addressment of the possible losses associated with the system life cycle? ▼

B)Manufacturability analysis
Manufacturability analysis is focused on enabling system production (not in the whole life cycle) in a cost-effective and responsible manner (not necessarily focusing on potential life cycle losses). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.7, p.175.

262 [ID-4081] The following statements are all related to the utilization stage, EXCEPT: ▼
A)It can coexist retirement stage.
The utilization stage can coexist with the retirement stage, as the system or product may still be in use while some of its elements are being retired. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.1.1, p.26.
B)It proceeds somewhat in parallel with the support stage.
The utilization stage may not necessarily proceed strictly in parallel with the support stage, though it generally is the case. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.1.1, p.26 and section 2.1.2, p.28.
C)It begins with the provisioning of support for the SOI in its operational environment. ✓ Correct
This refers to the support stage. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.1.2, p.29.
D)It tends to be much longer than the concept stage.
Though this lenght varies according to the nature of the system, the utilization stage tends to be longer than most of the other stages. The support stage is the exception, as it has somewhat the same lenght of the utilization stage. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.1.1, p.26 and section 2.1.2, p.28.
263 [ID-4054] Which of the following is NOT within the scope of Logistics Engineering? ▼
A)To elaborate logistics support requirements.
Elaborating logistics support requirements is a key for defining the necessary support elements to ensure the effective operation and maintenance of a system throughout its utilization and support stages. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.6, p.173.
B)To acquire all enabling systems. ✓ Correct
Enabling systems that are unrelated to system support is not within the scope of Logistics Engineering. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.6, p.173.
C)To provide logistic support to systems during utilization and support stages in a cost-effective manner.
Providing logistic support to systems during utilization and support stages in a cost-effective manner is a core responsibility of Logistics Engineering. This includes activities such as maintenance planning, spare parts management, and improvement actions based on failure recording. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.6, pp.173-175.
D)To influence system design for increased supportability.
Influencing system design for increased supportability involves integrating logistics considerations into the design process to enhance cost-effectiveness of the system's support. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.6, p.173.
264 [ID-4074] The development of new systems/elements in the presence of legacy systems/elements is called: ▼
A)Brownfield Systems Engineering ✓ Correct
Brownfield systems engineering involves developing and integrating new systems or elements within an existing environment that already has legacy systems or elements. This requires ensuring compatibility and integration with the current infrastructure. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.312.
B)Agile Systems Engineering
Agile systems engineering is an iterative and incremental approach that focuses on flexibility and customer feedback. While it can be used in brownfield projects, it does not specifically describe engineering in the presence of legacy systems.
C)Greenfield Systems Engineering
Greenfield systems engineering refers to designing and implementing new systems or elements in a completely new environment without any existing infrastructure or constraints. This approach starts with a "clean slate." See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.313.
D)Waterfall System Development
Waterfall development is a linear and sequential approach where each phase must be completed before the next one begins. It does not specifically refer to the presence of legacy systems.
265 [ID-4103] Choose the option that best provides subjects of vertical integration: ▼
A)Two generators that are going to operate in parallel with each other.
These two would be adequate subjects of horizontal integration, which refers to activities that are performed across elements that share the same hierarchical level within the system architecture. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5.8, p.137. Both generators would typically be at the same level of the system hierarchy and are being coordinated to work together.
B)A fuel system and one of its fuel pumps. ✓ Correct
These two would be adequate subjects of vertical integration, which involves actions taken to ensure compatibility between system elements located at different levels of the system hierarchy. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5.8, p.137. This would be the best option to be chosen, as the fuel pump will definitely be at a lower level in relation to the fuel system, being one of its elements.
C)A ship and a helicopter that are going to interoperate.
These two would be adequate subjects of horizontal integration, which refers to activities that are performed across elements that share the same hierarchical level within the system architecture. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5.8, p.137. Both the ship and the helicopter would arguably be at the “system” level.
D)A radar system and a sonar system that will work together for situational awareness.
These two would be adequate subjects of horizontal integration, which refers to activities that are performed across elements that share the same hierarchical level within the system architecture. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5.8, p.137. Both of these systems will likely exist at the same level in the system hierarchy.

B)System based on models
This is a made up term.

The traits mentioned in the question may or not apply to hardware intensive systems.

268 [ID-4070] In which of the following sectors is a higher skill level expected from the system user? ▼
A)Consumer electronics
In the consumer electronics sector, the skill level expected from the system user may vary depending on the complexity of the device or technology. While some consumer electronics may require basic knowledge to operate, the skill level expectation is generally lower compared to sectors like defense where specialized training is essential. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4, p.246.
B)Defense ✓ Correct
In the defense sector, a higher skill level is expected from the system user due to the critical nature of the systems involved. Users are often required to undergo specialized training to operate sophisticated defense systems effectively and securely, making the skill level expectation higher compared to other sectors. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4, p.246.
C)Automotive
In the automotive sector, the system user is typically expected to have a moderate skill level to operate the vehicle's features and technologies. However, the skill level required is not as high as in the defense sector where specialized knowledge and training are often necessary for using complex systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4, p.246.
D)The system user is expected to have the same skill level regardless of the sector.
Different sectors have varying levels of complexity and criticality in their systems, which directly impact the skill level expected from the users. In sectors like defense, where safety and security are paramount, a higher skill level is typically required compared to other sectors. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4, p.246.

See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5, p.263.

This would be a requirement for the project manager, not for the SE practitioner.

273 [ID-4102] What is a key distinction between the application of SE in healthcare delivery compared to traditional fields such as defense, aerospace, and automotive? ▼
A)SE in healthcare delivery follows the exact same processes as SE in the other mentioned areas.
SE is more product-focused in those other areas. Healthcare delivery SE, in contrast, focuses more on optimizing processes, patient flow, and operational efficiency within hospitals and care systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.2, pp.248 and 249.
B)SE in healthcare delivery often involves improving workflows or processes on a local scale before solutions are shared. ✓ Correct
Unlike traditional engineering domains, where large-scale integration is common, healthcare SE often starts with localized process improvements (e.g., optimizing emergency room workflow or patient scheduling). Once these smaller-scale solutions are tested and refined, they may be expanded or adapted for broader use across institutions, regions, or national healthcare systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.2, pp.248 and 249.
C)SE in healthcare delivery is primarily concerned with financial risk management.
Financial considerations are important in healthcare, but patient safety, quality of care, and system reliability are the top priorities in healthcare SE applications. Unlike industries where cost and schedule often take precedence, healthcare SE balances financial sustainability with ethical and clinical concerns, ensuring that safety is never compromised​. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.2, pp.248 and 249.
D)SE in healthcare delivery primarily focuses on large-scale systems with extensive requirements.
While some healthcare systems involve large-scale planning (e.g., national health IT infrastructure), many SE applications in healthcare focus on localized, incremental improvements in clinical workflows, patient safety, and operational efficiency. Unlike aerospace or defense, where large, highly integrated systems dominate, healthcare often deals with fragmented, adaptive, and evolving environments​. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4.2, pp.248 and 249.
274 [ID-4020] Good practices for technical review and audits include all the following, EXCEPT: ▼
A)Establish clear enter and exit criteria for the technical reviews and audits.
Establishing clear entry and exit criteria for technical reviews and audits is essential to ensure that the objectives of the review are met and the necessary outcomes are achieved. This helps in setting expectations, defining scope, and ensuring the effectiveness of the review process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.4., pp.32 e 33.
B)Make use of as many technical reviews and audits as you can. ✓ Correct
Simply using as many technical reviews and audits as possible is not a good practice. It is important to prioritize and schedule reviews and audits based on the criticality and complexity of the project/system to avoid unnecessary delays and resource allocation. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.4., pp.32 e 33.
C)Include experts and independent reviewers when conducting technical reviews and audits.
Including experts and independent reviewers in technical reviews and audits is a good practice to ensure thorough evaluation, diverse perspectives, and unbiased feedback. Experts can provide technical insights, while independent reviewers can offer impartial assessments. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.4., pp.32 e 33.
D)Follow up on actions that were raised during the technical reviews and audits.
Following up on actions raised during technical reviews and audits is crucial to ensure that identified issues are addressed, corrective actions are implemented, and improvements are made. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.4., pp.32 e 33.
275 [ID-4045] All the following sentences apply to the system design definition process, EXCEPT: ▼
A)It focuses on the essential concepts, functions, properties, features and behaviors of a system. ✓ Correct
This is applicable to the system architecture definition process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5.4, p.123.
B)It iterates with the system architecture definition process.
The system design definition process and system architecture definition process often influence each other. Changes in system design may impact the system architecture, and vice versa, leading to iterative refinement and alignment between the two processes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.4., p.123.
C)It iterates with the system requirements definition process.
The system design definition process and system requirements definition process iterate together to ensure that the system design meets the specified requirements. Changes in system requirements may necessitate adjustments in the system design, which in turn may impose constraints to system requirements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.3., pp.113-115.
D)Determining design drivers is part of its "prepare for design definition" activity.
Design drivers help guide the design decisions and ensure that the design aligns with the overall system objectives and constraints. It is part of the "prepare for design definition" activity of the system design definition process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.5., p.125.
276 [ID-4039] Which of the following is NOT a Systems Engineering principle? ▼
A)SE spans the whole life cycle.
Systems Engineering encompasses the entire life cycle of a system, from concept through retirement. SE considers the system holistically and ensures that all aspects of the system are addressed. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.
B)A model can be used as a perfect representation of a system. ✓ Correct
Only the real system can perfectly represent itself. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.
C)Complex systems are engineered by complex organizations.
Not all organizations may have the expertise or resources to effectively engineer complex systems. Systems Engineering principles recognize the need for collaboration and coordination among various disciplines and stakeholders (thus, a complex organization) to successfully engineer complex systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.
D)SE holistic view encompasses the systems elements, enabling systems, the environment and all their interactions.
This choice accurately describes a Systems Engineering principle by emphasizing the importance of taking a holistic view that includes all elements of a system, enabling systems, the environment, and their interactions. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.

B)Avoid: to eliminate or reduce the system's exposure to adversity.
This is the correct definition of "avoid". See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, p. 181.
C)Resilience should be considered early in the system's life cycle.
Considering resilience early in the system's life cycle is essential to influence the system's design so the system can become more resilient. The more the system's life cycle advances, the harder it becomes to make changes in requirements, design choices, etc. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, p. 181.
D)Recover: the ability to restore capability after degradation.
This is the correct definition of "recover". See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, p. 181.

An interoperating system is a system that interfaces with the SoI in its operational environment to perform a common function that contributes for achieving the purpose of the SoI. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.3, p.10.

283 [ID-4028] What is the name of the most popular modeling language used by Systems Engineering practitioners? ▼
A)BPMN (Business Process Model and Notation)
BPMN is widely used for modeling business processes and workflows. It is not specifically tailored for systems engineering applications.
B)ERD (Entity-Relationship Diagram)
ERD is used to model data relationships within databases. It is not typically used for systems engineering.
C)SysML (Systems Modeling Language) ✓ Correct
SysML is a general-purpose modeling language specifically designed for systems engineering applications. It customizes UML to support the needs of systems engineering, making it the most popular modeling language among Systems Engineering practitioners.
D)UML (Unified Modeling Language)
UML is a standardized modeling language used to visualize and document the design of a system. While it is popular in software engineering, it is not the most tailored or widely used for systems engineering.
284 [ID-4079] Choose the WRONG answer: ▼

B)Adding SE effort can increase overall project costs.
There is an optimum SE effort equivalent to a normalized range of 10% to 14% of the program/project cost. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp.6.
C)It is cheaper to fix issues if they are identified earlier.
Experience shows that, as times passes by, issues become harder and more expensive to correct.
D)The higher it is the Systems Engineering effort, the lower should be the total project cost. ✓ Correct
There is an optimum SE effort equivalent to a normalized range of 10% to 14% of the program/project cost. Beyond these percentages, data shows that the program/project cost rises as the Systems Engineering effort increases. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, p.6.

287 [ID-4018] Choose the WRONG option. ▼

288 [ID-4098] All the following metrics are specifically related to Reliability, Availability and Maintainability (RAM), EXCEPT: ▼
A)MTTR
MTTR is a key metric related to Maintainability. It measures the average time required to repair a system or element after a failure and is used to assess maintainability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, p.179.
B)MTBF
MTBF is a key metric related to Reliability. It represents the average time between failures for a system or component and is used to predict the system's reliability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, p.177.
C)Uptime
Uptime is a metric that specifically measures the amount of time a system or element is available for use. It is directly related to the availability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, pp.178 and 179.
D)LCC ✓ Correct
LCC (Life Cycle Cost) is a metric that measures the total cost of owning, operating, and maintaining a system or component throughout its life cycle. While LCC is heavily influenced by RAM considerations and can constrain possible design and maintenance solutions, LCC is not specifically related to RAM. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.2, pp.164 and 165.
289 [ID-4071] Which of the following traits is associated with soft skills? ▼
A)Low self awareness required
Soft skills actually require a high level of self-awareness. Individuals with strong soft skills are able to understand their own emotions, motivations, and behaviors, which allows them to better navigate social interactions and work effectively in teams. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.1.1, p.263.
B)Difficult to test ✓ Correct
Soft skills are difficult to test or measure objectively compared to hard skills, which are more technical and quantifiable in nature. Evaluating soft skills often involves subjective assessments, observations of behavior, and feedback from others, making them challenging to assess in a standardized way. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.1.1, p.263.
C)Individual application
Soft skills are typically related to interpersonal interactions and communication, rather than individual application. They involve working effectively with others and understanding their perspectives. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.1.1, p.263.
D)Concrete definitions
Soft skills are often characterized by abstract concepts and qualities, rather than concrete definitions. They include traits like empathy, communication, adaptability, and emotional intelligence, which are not easily quantifiable or defined in a straightforward manner. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.1.1, p.263.
290 [ID-4058] How many system deployments are expected while using a sequential approach? ▼
A)Multiple deployments.

B)Any number of deployments.
In a sequential approach, there is a single delivery. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.35.
C)One deployment. ✓ Correct
In a sequential approach, there is a single delivery and the SE activities are broken down into linear sequential stages where each of these stages depend on the deliverables of previous stages and on the feedback from subsequent stages. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.35.
D)No deployments.
In a sequential approach, there is a single delivery. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.35.
291 [ID-4050] Choose the option that best reflects the meaning of "capability". ▼
A)a characteristic or property of an entity.
This definition fits the concept of "attribute". See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., Appendix C, p.311.
B)The ability to realize a specific objective under stated conditions. ✓ Correct
Capability refers to the ability to achieve a specific objective or goal under given conditions. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., Appendix C, p.312.
C)An action or reaction of an entity in response to stimuli or inputs.
This definition fits the concept of "behavior". See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., Appendix C, p.312.
D)A measure to assess if the system meets its performance or design requirements.
This definition fits the concept of "measure of performance". See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., Appendix C, p.313.
292 [ID-4055] What kind of leadership is critical to perform the role of a Systems Engineer? ▼
A)Technical leadership ✓ Correct
Technical leadership is critical for a Systems Engineer, involving a wide understanding of the technical aspects of the system being developed and general leadership skills, functioning as the intersection of both. This type of leadership is critical because Systems Engineers work across and integrate a broad range of disciplines and stakeholders to achieve the desired outcomes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.1.3, pp.263 and 264.
B)Leadership is critical for the Project Manager, not for the Systems Engineer.
Leadership is critical for a Systems Engineer as well, even though it is a different typ of leadership that a PM shoud exhibit.
C)Social leadership
Social leadership emphasizes building relationships, fostering collaboration, and creating a positive team culture. While social leadership is important for a Systems Engineer to work effectively with team members and stakeholders, it is not the critical trait to perform the role.
D)Transactional leadership
Transactional leadership focuses on exchanges between leaders and team members, such as rewards for performance or punishment for non-compliance. While this type of leadership can be effective in certain situations, it is not a specific trait of a Systems Engineer.

294 [ID-4037] A specific operating capability during which a number of system's functions are performed to a specific degree is called: ▼
A)System mode ✓ Correct
The proposed definition fits the concept of system mode. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.6, p.14.
B)System state
System state is an observable and measurable characterization of the current status, configuration or performance-based condition of a system.
C)System availability
System availability measures the portion of the total time that a system is ready to perform its functions when required. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, pp. 176-179.
D)System life cycle stage
System life cycle stage refers to the period that a system is currently in within its life cycle, such as concept, development, support, etc. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.1.2, pp. 26-29.
295 [ID-4101] Which of the following best represents the role of sustainability engineering in SE? ▼
A)Sustainability engineering applies primarily to material selection and energy efficiency.
Sustainability engineering also explicitly includes social considerations, such as fair labor practices, human well-being, and responsible supply chain management. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.10, pp.184 and 185.
B)Sustainability engineering integrates environmental and social considerations throughout the entire system life cycle to support a circular economy. ✓ Correct
Sustainability engineering supports the circular economy by addressing environmental and social aspects across all stages of a system’s life cycle, including design, production, use, and disposal. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.10, pp.184 and 185.
C)Sustainability engineering primarily focuses on minimizing carbon emissions during system operation.
Sustainability engineering considers the entire life cycle, including development, production, operation, and disposal, rather than just carbon emissions during system operation. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.10, pp.184 and 185.
D)Sustainability engineering ensures that products are fully recyclable at end-of-life, even if it increases production costs and complexity.
While recyclability is a key factor, sustainability engineering balances economic, environmental, and social aspects rather than focusing only on recyclability, which might not always be the best solution​. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.10, pp.184 and 185.

297 [ID-4057] What is the name of the overarching process for achieving quality goals within a project or organization? ▼
A)Project assessment and control process.
Project assessment and control process refer to the activities related to monitoring and controlling project performance. While these activities are important for project success, they are not specifically focused on achieving quality goals. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.2, pp. 75 and 76 and section 2.3.3.5, p.63.
B)Verification process.
Verification process typically refers to the process of evaluating whether the deliverables meet their specified requirements. While verification is a key aspect of quality management, it is not the overarching process that encompasses all quality-related activities within a project. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section section 2.3.5.9, p.138 and section 2.3.3.5, p.63.
C)Quality assurance process.
While quality assurance is an important component of the overall quality management process, it specifically focuses on the processes used to create and deliver the project deliverables. It is not the overarching process that encompasses all quality-related activities. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.8, p.99 and section 2.3.3.5, p.63.
D)Quality management process. ✓ Correct
The quality management process is the overarching process that encompasses all activities related to achieving quality goals within a project or organization. The term "overarching process" should give you a hint that we are talking about quality management and not quality assurance. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.3.5, p.63.
298 [ID-4003] Choose the right sentence regarding the verification process. ▼
A)Ensuring that enabling systems used for verification will be available when required is not a concern of the verification process itself.
Ensuring that enabling systems used for verification will be available when required is a crucial aspect of the verification process and part of the "prepare for verification" activity. Without the necessary tools and resources, verification activities cannot be effectively carried out, leading to potential issues in the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.139.
B)Verification activities are performed only after the system is built.
Verification activities are not limited to being performed only after the system is built. Verification is an ongoing process that starts early in the system life cycle and is applicable to any work product. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, pp. 138-140.
C)There are different verification methods, like test and analysis. ✓ Correct
Indeed. The defined verification methods are: inspection, analysis, demonstration and test. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.142.
D)If a system was verified, it can be said that the right system was built.
While verification is essential for confirming that the system meets its requirements, it does not guarantee that the right system was built, which is done through the validation process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.138.
299 [ID-4013] In general terms, the higher percentage of the life cycle cost of a system is spent during: ▼
A)Production stage
The production stage typically involves manufacturing, assembling, and delivering the system to the end users. While production costs can be significant, they usually do not account for the majority of the life cycle cost of a system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.2, p.7.
B)Development stage
While development costs can be substantial, they are usually not as significant as the costs incurred during the utilization stage. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.2, p.7.
C)Utilization stage ✓ Correct
The utilization stage is when the system is in operation and being used for its intended purpose. During this stage, maintenance, upgrades, repairs, and operational costs can accumulate, making it the stage where the highest percentage of the life cycle cost is spent. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.2, p.7.
D)Concept stage
Costs during this stage are relatively low compared to the other stages of the system life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.2, p.7.
300 [ID-4014] Fill the blank:___________ is an interaction of the system with its operating environment. ▼
A)Measurement

B)Functionality ✓ Correct
The interaction of the system with its operating environment is called functionality. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.1, p.9.
C)State
The state of a system typically refers to its condition or configuration at a specific point in time, rather than a specific property that can be observed. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.1, p.9.
D)Attribute
Attributes are the characteristics or features of a system that can be observed. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.1, p.9.

302 [ID-4001] A system life cycle process is being applied to successive system hierarchical levels. This is called: ▼
A)Recursion ✓ Correct
Recursion happens when the same system life cycle process is applied to successive hierarchical levels. This happens, for example, while applying verification to single equipment and then verifying the integrated set afterwards. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.1.2, pp.42-44.
B)Concurrency
Concurrency is the parallel application of different system life cycle processes in the same hierarchical level. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.1.2, pp.42-44.
C)Iteration
Iteration occurs between two processes at a given hierarchical level. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.1.2, pp.42-44.
D)Integration
Integration involves combining different elements into an unified set. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.1.2, pp.42-44.

305 [ID-4008] All of the following are technical management processes, EXCEPT: ▼
A)Quality management ✓ Correct
Quality management is an organizational project-enabling process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.3.5, p.63.This is very tricky because:1- There are some management processes that fit into the technical management processes group and others that are part of the organizational project enabling processes. A good reasoning to adopt in order to associate the management processes to their right group would be: "If it relates more to the organization as a whole than it relates to the system/project, it is an organizational project enabling process. If it relates more to the system/project than it relates to the organization as a whole, it is a technical management process ".2- It can get mistaken for quality assurance. To avoid this, remember: quality assurance deals with the effective application of the quality management process to the project - so it is a technical management process - while quality management relates to the quality objectives of the organization, thus being an organizational project enabling process.
B)Quality Assurance
Quality Assurance is one of the technical management processes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.8, p.98.Beware to not mistake it with the quality management process, which is an organizational project enabling process.
C)Project assessment & control
Project assessment & control is one of the technical management process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.2, p.75.
D)Decision management
Decision management is a technical management process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.3, p.78. This is quite tricky because there are some management processes that fit into the technical management processes group and others that are part of the organizational project enabling processes. A good reasoning to adopt in order to associate the management processes to their right group would be: "If it relates more to the organization as a whole than it relates to the system/project, it is an organizational project enabling process. If it relates more to the system/project than it relates to the organization as a whole, it is a technical management process ".

309 [ID-4085] The process of using a model to predict the behavior of a system is called: ▼
A)Modeling
Modeling refers to the process of creating a representation of a system using a model. While modeling is a crucial step for simulation, it is not specifically focused on using the model to predict system behavior as the question describes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.1, p.192.
B)Analysis
Analysis involves the systematic reproducible examination to evaluate data or information to gain insights. While analysis may involve using models, it is not specifically related to using a model to predict the behavior of a system as described in the question. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.1, p.192.
C)Simulation ✓ Correct
Simulation is the process of using a model to mimic the behavior of a system to allow the prediction of its behavior under certain conditions. In the context of the question, simulation is the correct choice as it specifically relates to predicting system behavior using a model. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.1, p.192.

There is a correct option to be chosen among the others.

313 [ID-4056] Choose the option that best reflects what it typically means for a system to be "acceptably safe": ▼
A)The likelihood of each hazard risk is lower than 5%.
There is no "rule of thumb" number and a 5% likelihood may be unacceptable if the impact is considerable. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.11, p.186.
B)The system is 100% safe
Claiming that a system is 100% safe is unrealistic and unattainable in practice. It is practically impossible to eliminate all hazards completely. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.11, p.186.
C)The impact of each hazard is small.
Acceptable safety levels require an approach that considers both the likelihood and severity of potential hazards, making this choice incomplete in assessing system safety. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.11, p.186.
D)The hazard risks have been mitigated to be as low as reasonably possible. ✓ Correct
This choice accurately reflects what it typically means for a system to be "acceptably safe." It signifies that the hazard risks within the system have been identified and mitigated to the lowest possible level that is reasonable and feasible. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.11, p.186.

317 [ID-4090] Choose the WRONG sentence in relation to Systems Engineering transformation in an enterprise ▼
A)When going from traditional SE to agile SE, the organization has a more flexible and incremental paradigm to produce work products throughout the system's life cycle.
Agile SE emphasizes iterative and incremental development, customer collaboration, and responding to change over following a plan, which fundamentally changes the approach to the system life cycle processes to be used. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.5, pp. 274 and 275.
B)Transforming document based SE to model based SE is a matter of going from textual documents to 2D and 3D visual representations of the system. ✓ Correct
Transforming from document-based Systems Engineering to model-based Systems Engineering involves more than just converting textual documents to visual representations. It includes a shift towards using models as the primary means of communication, analysis, and decision-making throughout the system life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.5, pp. 274 and 275.
C)Going from no SE to full use SE requires the implementation of the system life cycle process within the organization's original set of processes.
Implementing Systems Engineering practices in an organization that previously had no formal SE processes in place requires the establishment of SE formal processes, such as system requirements definition, configuration management, verification, validation, etc. into the organization's workflows.
D)Going from no SE to full use SE requires a cultural shift of the organization as a whole.
Moving from no Systems Engineering to full use of Systems Engineering involves cultural changes, training, process improvements, and organizational buy-in to fully integrate Systems Engineering practices into the enterprise. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.5, pp. 274 and 275.

"Attribute" refers to an observable characteristic or property of a given entity. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., Appendix C, p.311.
C)Behavior
Behavior refers to the actions or reactions of an entity in response to stimuli or inputs. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., Appendix C, p.312.
D)Requirement
Requirement is a statement that expresses the obligation of an entity to possess a quality or perform a function. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., Appendix C, p.314.

B)Incremental approach

DevOps is an evolutionary approach. In an evolutionary approach, both the system and its requirements are delivered into smaller increments as new needs and requirements arise with each incremental deployment. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.38.

327 [ID-4021] Choose the WRONG sentence regarding the acquisition process. ▼
A)A change request can only be performed by the acquirer. ✓ Correct
This statement is incorrect. A change request can be initiated by either the acquirer or the supplier during the acquisition process. B)A RFQ is one of its typical outputs.
A RFQ (Request for Quotation) is one of the typical outputs of the acquisition process. It is used to solicit bids or quotes from potential suppliers for the required products or services.
C)"accept the product/service" is an activity of the acquisition process.

328 [ID-4105] Consider the following sequence of interactions between the system requirements definition (SRD) process and the risk management (RM) process.SRD: Elicits requirementRM: Identifies risk related to the requirement and proposes mitigationSRD: Adjusts requirement to mitigate the identified riskRM: Update the risk register.These interactions form an example of: ▼
A)Recursion
Recursion refers to applying the same life cycle processes at different levels of the system hierarchy. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.1.2, p.44. In the scenario described by the question, the interactions occur within the same level, between two processes.
B)Paralellism
“Concurrency” would be the correct name to use when two processes are occurring in parallel at the same abstraction level. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.1.2, p.42. Nonetheless, the sequence given by the question illustrates something else.
C)Concurrency
Concurrency involves performing two or processes in parallel at a given level of the system hierarchy. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.1.2, p.42. Although SRD and RM might operate concurrently in other contexts, the scenario given shows a sequential exchange of information, not parallel execution.
D)Iteration ✓ Correct
Iteration refers to repeating the application and interaction between two or more processes at the same hierarchy level of the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.1.2, pp.43 and 44. In this case, the SRD and RM processes are interacting in a loop, refining requirements in response to risk insights, and updating risks based on requirement changes.

"Slow" prototyping is a made up term.

330 [ID-4004] Which of the following statements is FALSE? ▼
A)Epistemic uncertainty can always be reduced by gathering more knowledge.
This is correct for epistemic uncertainty, though it is not for aleatory uncertainty. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.1, pp.15 and 16.
B)Systems Engineering practitioners must eliminate uncertainty before deciding. ✓ Correct
Even though epistemic uncertainty can be eliminated, a certain degree of aleatory uncertainty will always be present. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.1, pp.15 and 16.
C)Cognitive bias can cause engineers to overlook critical information and make flawed decisions.
Cognitive bias are judgment errors under uncertainty caused by simplistic information processes, being potentially harmful to information processing and decision making. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.2, p.17.
D)There are two types of uncertainty: due to lack of knowledge and due to randomness.
These are the two types of uncertainty considered in the handbook. The former is called "epistemic" while the latter is called "aleatory". See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.1, pp.15 and 16.
331 [ID-4067] A requirement of a propulsion system is traceable to a requirement of one of its propulsion motors (and vice versa). What type(s) of traceability are being established? (Choose the best answer) ▼
A)Horizontal traceability
Horizontal traceability involves tracing entities across the same level of abstraction. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.3, p.201.There is no horizontal traceability in the situation described by the question.
B)Vertical traceability
Vertical traceability involves tracing entities across different system levels of abstraction. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.3, p.201.The situation described by the question exhibits both bidirectional traceability and vertical traceability.
C)Bidirectional traceability
Bidirectional traceability refers to the ability to trace an entity to another entity while also establishing a reverse link back to the initial entity. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.3, p.201.The situation described by the question exhibits both bidirectional traceability and vertical traceability.
D)Bidirectional traceability and vertical traceability ✓ Correct
Bidirectional traceability refers to the ability to trace an entity to another entity while also establishing a reverse link back to the initial entity. This is exactly what is happening between the requirements described in the question.Besides, the propulsion system is in a higher level than the propulsion motor (one of its elements), characterizing the vertical traceability as well.See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.3, p.201.
332 [ID-4077] Which of the following activities is a task performed by the System Analysis Process? ▼
A)Developing system test and evaluation plans.
These are done by the verification and validation processes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5.9, p.138 and section 2.3.5.11, p.146.
B)Creating detailed design documents for system components.
Creating detailed design documents is part of the system design definition process, not the system analysis process, which focuses on evaluating and analyzing system options. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5.6, pp.129-132 and section 2.3.5.5, p.124.
C)Conducting trade-off studies to evaluate alternative solutions. ✓ Correct
Conducting trade-off studies is a key component of the system analysis process. This activity involves evaluating different solutions to determine the best approach based on various criteria such as cost, performance, and risk. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5.6, pp.129-132.
D)Deciding about what alternative should be further developed.

333 [ID-4080] The understanding of the complexity of systems by looking at the big picture and focusing on the relationships and interactions between different components is better achieved by: ▼
A)Holism
Holism is the idea that systems and their properties should be viewed as wholes, not just as a collection of parts. While holism emphasizes the importance of considering the entire system, it does not specifically focus on the relationships and interactions between different components, making it incomplete for understanding system complexity in that aspect. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.5, pp.21 and 23.
B)Systems thinking ✓ Correct
Systems thinking is the approach of understanding how different components within a system interact with each other to achieve a specific goal. It emphasizes the big picture and the interconnectedness of components that are part of the system. This focus on relationships and interactions makes systems thinking a more effective approach for understanding the complexity of systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.5, pp.21 and 23.
C)Systematic thinking
Systematic thinking involves approaching problems in a methodical and logical way, breaking them down into smaller, more manageable parts. While systematic thinking is important for analyzing and solving complex problems, it does not necessarily emphasize the relationships and interactions between different components within a system, which is crucial for understanding system complexity. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.5, pp.21 and 23.
D)Design thinking
Design thinking is a human-centered approach to innovation that involves understanding the needs, redefining problems, and creating innovative solutions. While design thinking is valuable for creating user-centric solutions, it does not specifically address the complexity of systems by focusing on the relationships and interactions between different components within a system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.7, p.212.

It allows for adjustments and modifications to be made to the processes to better align with the specific goals, scope, and constraints of the project at hand. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.1, pp. 215-219.

The statement is incorrect because the tailoring process does not require all system life cycle processes and activities to be used at all. 338 [ID-4006] Choose the right sentence regarding Reliability, Availability & Maintainability (RAM) concepts. ▼
A)If a system's downtime increases, its availability increases.
Availability = Uptime/(Uptime + Downtime). Thus, an increase in downtime would promote a decrease in availability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, p.178.
B)If a system's MTBF increases, its reliability decreases.
An increase in MTBF actually indicates an improvement in reliability, not a decrease. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, pp. 176-178.
C)A system can have extremely high reliability and still have low availability. ✓ Correct
This statement is correct. Reliability refers to the ability of a system to perform its intended function without failure over a specified period of time, while availability refers to the readiness of a system to perform its function when required. A system can have high reliability (low failure rate) but still have low availability if undergoing long preventive maintenance periods due to difficult maintenance tasks (low maintainability) or considerable delay times. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, pp. 176-179.
D)A system's operational availability is always higher than its achieved availability.
Operational availability considers delay times into the equation while achieving availability does not. This means that a system's operational availability will never be higher than its achieved availability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, pp. 178 and 179.
339 [ID-4089] All of the following are key elements of featured-based PLE factory, EXCEPT: ▼
A)Feature catalog.
A collection of the available features for each selectable product. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.2.4, p.227.
B)Independent detailed design for each delivered product. ✓ Correct
The designs are not independent since they all have a base structure (common content) upon which the variation points are mounted. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.2.4, p.227.
C)Bill of feature.
The features that are actually selected for each system in the system family. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.2.4, p.227.
D)PLE factory configurator.
An automated software tool that applies ta given bill-of-features to all shared assets. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.2.4, p.227.
