341 [ID-4036] Choose the INCORRECT sentence: ▼
A)A system element can be either atomic or a system itself.
An atomic system element is one that is not (considered to be) further decomposed into other system elements. A system element can also be a system itself, as systems can be composed of other systems (e.g. a combat system as part of a naval ship). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.5, p.13.
B)The highest hierarchy level of the SoI has only one element.
The highest hierarchy level of the System of Interest (SoI) is composed of only one element, as it represents the top-level system (the SoI itself) being considered. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.5, p.13.
C)A "white box" representation gives full visibility of what is outside a system element. ✓ Correct
A white box representation of a system refers to viewing the system from an internal perspective, where the observer has visibility into the internal workings and details of the system. This is in contrast to a black box representation where only the external properties/behavior are considered. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.1, p.9.
D)A system viewed as black box is, in that same representation, considered to be atomic.
When viewed as a black box, the internal workings of the system are not visible, and it is treated as a single entity, aligning with the concept of atomicity. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.5, pp.12 and 13.
342 [ID-4073] Choose the option that best fits the following definition:"A digital surrogate of a system that evolves through the system's life cycle and incorporate models to emulate it". ▼
A)Virtual reality
Virtual Reality immerses users in a completely virtual environment, often used for simulations and training. It does not necessarily serve as a digital surrogate for a real-world system that evolves over its life cycle.
B)Digital twin ✓ Correct
A digital twin is a virtual model designed to accurately reflect a physical object. It evolves along with the system's life cycle, incorporating data and models to emulate the real-world counterpart. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.4, p.273.
C)3D modeling
3D modeling involves creating a digital representation of an object or system, but it does not necessarily evolve through the system's life cycle or incorporate models to emulate real-world behaviors dynamically.
D)Augmented reality
Augmented Reality overlays digital information on the real world, enhancing the user's perception of their environment. However, it does not create a digital surrogate of a system that evolves over time.

This sums up the principle of biomimicry. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.8, p.213.
D)To forge the connection between human and nature by leveraging or inserting instances of nature, natural patterns, or spatial conditions into the built environment.
This is the concept of biophilia, not biomimicry.
350 [ID-4099] What is the name of the analysis used to determine the various combinations of events that can lead to a specific system failure? ▼
A)FMEA
FMEA, which stands for Failure Mode and Effects Analysis, is a systematic method for identifying and potential failure modes of a system or component and the effects of these failures. It is not used to determine the events that can lead to failures. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.6, p.174.
B)FMECA
FMECA, which stands for Failure Modes, Effects, and Criticality Analysis, is an extension of FMEA that includes criticality analysis to prioritize failure modes. It is not used to determine the events that can lead to failures. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.6, p.174.
C)LORA
The Level of Repair Analysis (LORA) is used to determine the appropriate location to execute maintenance tasks. It is not used to determine the events that can lead to failures See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.6, p.174.
D)FTA ✓ Correct
The question statement describes the Fault Tree Analysis (FTA). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.6, p.174.
351 [ID-4019] Fill in the blank:_______ is an approach that enables system support for its entire life cycle. ▼
A)Logistics Engineering ✓ Correct
The proposed definition fits Logistics Engineering. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.6., p.172.
B)Manufacturability/Producibility Analysis
Manufacturability/Producibility Analysis is an approach that enables system production in a responsible and cost-effective manner.
C)Affordability Analysis
Affordability Analysis is an approach that optimizes value while providing cost-effective capability over the system's life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.2., p.160.
D)Sustainability Engineering
Sustainability Engineering is an approach that supports the circular economy considering social and environmental aspects over the system's life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.10., p.184.
352 [ID-4060] Choose the option that correctly fits an incremental life cycle approach: ▼
A)Requirements fully set at the start; multiple planned iterations. ✓ Correct
In an incremental approach, there are successive incremental iterations even though requirements are already known from the beginning. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.36 and 37.
B)Requirements partially set at the start; single planned iteration.
In an incremental approach, there are successive incremental iterations even though requirements are already known from the beginning. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.36 and 37.

Requirements should be set from the start. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.36 and 37.

353 [ID-4048] All of the following sentences are applicable to the maintenance process, EXCEPT ▼
A)It is active from the concept stage.
Early definition of maintenance and logistics support concepts/strategies are important to influence system requirements for maintainability and supportability. "Prepare for maintenance and logistics" is part of the maintenance process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.155.
B)It normally occurs concurrently with the operation process.
This is often the case, with the maintenance process being responsible for sustaining the system's services. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.154.
C)The modes of maintenance considered by the process are: corrective, preventive and predictive. ✓ Correct
Different modes of maintenance that should be considered include: corrective maintenance, preventive maintenance, predictive maintenance and system modification. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.156.
D)"Perform logistics support" is one of its activities.
"Perform logistics support" is an activity of the maintenance process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.154.
354 [ID-4069] Which of the following factors contribute for a adopting a COTS (Commercial Off The Shelf) system element? ▼
A)There is a sole source supply for the COTS product.
Having a sole source supply for a COTS product can be a risk factor for adopting it. Sole source suppliers may lead to dependency issues, lack of competition, and potential challenges in terms of pricing, support, and future availability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.3.3, p.231.
B)Use of proprietary data formats by the vendor.
The use of proprietary data formats by the vendor can pose challenges when adopting a COTS system element. It may lead to compatibility issues, vendor lock-in, and difficulties in integrating the system with existing infrastructure or other software components. This factor may hinder the adoption of a COTS product. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.3.3, p.231.
C)The COTS product is well documented. ✓ Correct
Being well-documented is a contributing factor for adopting the COTS products as it provides information on how to use, integrate and maintain the product. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.3.3, p.231.
D)Delivery times are variable.
Unpredictable delivery times for a COTS product can impact project timelines, resource planning, and overall project success. Timely delivery is essential for organizations looking to adopt COTS systems to meet their business needs efficiently. Inconsistent delivery times may lead to delays, increased costs, and potential disruptions in project implementation. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.3.3, p.231.
355 [ID-4078] Which of the following is a key characteristic of an engineered system? ▼
A)It includes only hardware components designed for a specific task.
Engineered systems often include a combination of hardware, software, people, and processes designed to work together for a specific purpose. Limiting the definition to only hardware is incorrect.
B)It is developed with a predefined structure and evolves organically over time.
"Evolving organically" is not a prerequisite for a system to be considered as "engineered". See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.1, pp.2 and 3.
C)It is designed to fulfill specific requirements and achieve a defined purpose through the integration of various elements. ✓ Correct
This accurately describes a key characteristic of an engineered system, which is intentionally designed to meet specific requirements and achieve a defined purpose. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.1, pp.2 and 3.
D)It operates without human intervention.
While some engineered systems can be autonomous, this is not a key characteristic of all engineered systems. Many engineered systems require human interaction or oversight.

356 [ID-5089] Choose the right sentence regarding decision making and system Life Cycle Cost (LCC): ▼
A)Early decision-making is critical in systems engineering as it influences a substantial portion of the total Life Cycle Cost (LCC). ✓ Correct
This option correctly reflects the importance of early decision-making in systems engineering, as decisions made early in the process commit a significant portion of the LCC. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, p.7.
B)The majority of LCC can be influenced by decisions made after 20% of the costs have been accrued.
This option is incorrect because the statement highlights that most LCC is already committed early on, leaving less room for cost influence later (around 80% of costs should be already committed by then). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, p.7.
C)Early decision-making has little impact on total Life Cycle Cost (LCC) since most costs are incurred later in the system's life cycle.
This option is incorrect because early decision-making significantly impacts LCC, as a large portion of costs is committed early in the program. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, p.7.
D)LCC is mostly determined by decisions made in the utilization stage.
Most of the LCC is usually spent during the utilization stage, but as a consequence of decisions made way earlier in the system's life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, p.7.
357 [ID-5055] The ability to realize a specific objective under stated conditions is called: ▼
A)Attribute

B)All the other options are wrong.
There is a correct option to be chosen among the others.
C)Capability ✓ Correct
Capability refers to the ability to achieve a specific objective or goal under given conditions. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., Appendix C, p.312.
D)Behavior
Behavior refers to the actions or reactions of an entity in response to stimuli or inputs. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., Appendix C, p.312.
358 [ID-5012] The following are basic verification methods, EXCEPT: ▼
A)Analysis
Analysis is a basic verification method that involves the use of analytical data or simulations to confirm compliance. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.142.
B)Inspection
Inspection is a basic verification method that involves examining an item against visual or other evidence to confirm compliance. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.142.
C)Simulation ✓ Correct
Simulation is included in "analysis" and it is not considered a basic verification method. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.142.
D)Demonstration
Demonstration is a basic verification method that involves showcasing a qualitative exhibition of the system's functionality or performance to stakeholders to confirm compliance. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.142.
359 [ID-5063] The Vee model is an example of a: ▼
A)Evolutionary approach
In an evolutionary approach, both the system and its requirements are delivered into smaller increments as new needs and requirements arise with each incremental deployment. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.38.
B)Incremental approach
In an incremental approach, there are successive incremental potential deliveries even though requirements are already known from the beginning. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.36 and 37.
C)Sequential approach ✓ Correct
The Vee model is a sequential approach. In a sequential approach, there is a single delivery and the SE activities are broken down into linear sequential stages where each of these stages depend on the deliverables of previous stages and on the feedback from subsequent stages. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.2.1, p.35.
D)All the other options are wrong.
There is a correct option to be chosen among the others.
360 [ID-5109] To be considered "singular", a requirement statement has to... ▼
A)refer to a single system.
Not necessarily. A requirement such as "All cars shall have one seatbelt for each passenger" is still singular. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.3, p.116.
B)refer to a single system element.
Not necessarily. A requirement such as "The chairs shall be black" is still singular. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.3, p.116.
C)refer to a single property/function/constraint. ✓ Correct
To be considered "singular", the requirement should focus on a specific aspect or characteristic of an entity, such as a property, function or constraint. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.3, p.116.
D)be unique.
Being unique is important for requirement statements to avoid duplication and inconsistencies, but it is not related to the "singular" characteristic of requirement statements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.3, p.116.
361 [ID-5006] Which of the following factors would be decisive for the adoption of an Agile Systems Engineering life cycle model? ▼
A)The SoI is complex.
The complexity of the System of Interest (SoI) may influence the choice of a systems engineering life cycle model, but it may not be a decisive factor. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.2, p.221.
B)The SoI is a SoS.
The SoI being a System of Systems (SoS) can add complexity to the systems life cycle processes, but it doesn't necessarily mean that an Agile Systems Engineering life cycle model is the best choice. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.2, p.221.
C)The SoI is part of a SoS.
Being part of a System of Systems (SoS) does not directly dictate the use of an Agile Systems Engineering life cycle model as it could be a stable SoS. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.2, p.221.
D)System environment is dynamic. ✓ Correct
The dynamic nature of the system environment is a key factor that would favor the adoption of an Agile Systems Engineering life cycle model. Agile methodologies are well-suited for environments that are likely to change frequently, as they emphasize iterative development, flexibility, experimentation and continuous feedback. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.2, p.221.
362 [ID-5088] Fill in the blank:The ____________ is responsible for segregating what is part of a system from what is not. ▼
A)system architecture
A system architecture defines the structure and organization of a system and may have different types of focus (e.g. physical, functional). It does not specifically segregate what is part of the system from what is not.
B)system context
The system context shows what is beyond the system boundary, describing the environment and conditions in which the system operates. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp.8.
C)system boundary ✓ Correct
The system boundary is responsible for defining and segregating what is part of the system from what is not. It establishes the limits of the system and its interactions with external elements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp.8.
D)system lifecycle
The system life cycle describes the stages through which a system passes from concept to retirement, not specifically segregating what is part of the system from what is not.

368 [ID-5061] Fill in the blanks:While _____________ focuses on improving processes to prevent errors from happening, _____________ provides defect rates and indetifies the source of the defects. ▼
A)quality control ; quality assurance.
It's the other way around. Quality assurance focuses on improving processes to prevent errors from occurring in the first place. It is a proactive approach that aims to ensure quality throughout the entire process. On the other hand, quality control provides defect rates and identifies the source of the defects after the process has been (partially or fully) completed. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.8, p.100.
B)quality management ; verification.
Quality management is a comprehensive approach that encompasses both quality assurance and quality control. Quality assurance focuses on improving processes to prevent errors and defects, while quality control involves checking the final product to identify and correct any issues. Verification is a process that ensures that the product meets the specified requirements and standards, but it is not directly related to providing defect rates or identifying the source of defects. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.8, p.100 and section 2.3.5.9, p.138.
C)quality assurance ; verification.
Verification is a process that ensures that the product meets the specified requirements and standards, but it is not directly related to providing defect rates or identifying the source of defects. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.9, p.138.
D)quality assurance ; quality control. ✓ Correct
Quality assurance focuses on improving processes to prevent errors from occurring in the first place. It is a proactive approach that aims to ensure quality throughout the entire process. On the other hand, quality control provides defect rates and identifies the source of the defects after the process has been (partially or fully) completed. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.8, p.100.

372 [ID-5005] All of the following are considered to be reliability engineering objectives, EXCEPT: ▼

B)To find ways to cope with failures that do occur.

C)To prevent or reduce the likelihood of failures.
By identifying potential failure points and implementing measures to mitigate risks, reliability engineers aim to increase the overall reliability of a system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, p.177.
D)To identify and correct causes of failure.
By understanding the root causes of failures, engineers can implement design improvements and preventive measures to enhance system reliability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.8, p.177.
373 [ID-5111] The Concept of Operations (ConOps)... ▼

The ConOps describes how an organization should operate to carry out its missions and achieve its objectives. It is a high-level description that includes existing systems and potential future systems. In other words, a system of interest to be acquired by an organization should be part of the ConOps, although it is not its sole focus. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.5.1, p.106.
B)is another name for Operational Concept (OpsCon), with the same meaning.
ConOps and OpsCon do not mean the same thing. The OpsCon describes how a system will be used during its operations from the perspective of its users. Section 2.3.5.1, p.106.
C)has a system of interest to be acquired by an organization as it sole focus.
The Concept of Operations (ConOps)does not have the system of interest to be acquired by an organization as its sole focus. It has a broader scope, including the organization itself as well as its existing and future systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.5.1, p.106.
D)describes how a system will be used during its operations from the perspective of its user.
This is applicable to the OpsCon. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.5.1, p.106.

Indeed. See the other explanations.
B)The Fault Tree Analysis (FTA) is a systematic approach to identifying potential failure modes of a system, determining their effects on the system, and assessing the criticality of those failures.
The description fits the Failure Mode Effects and Criticality Analysis (FMECA, not the FTA. Just reading carefully the name of each of these analyses can lead to this conclusion. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.6, p.174.
C)To provide logistic support to systems during utilization and support stages is not within the scope of Logistics Engineering.
Providing logistic support to systems during utilization and support stages in a cost-effective manner is a core responsibility of Logistics Engineering. This includes activities such as maintenance planning, spare parts management, and improvement actions based on failure recording. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.6, pp.173-175.
D)Logistics Engineering should start being applied at the development stage.
Logistics Engineering should start being applied at the concept stage of the system life cycle. The sooner it is applied, the higher is the allowance to design for supportability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.6, pp.172 and 173.
380 [ID-5058] All of the following are Human Systems Integration (HSI) perspectives, EXCEPT ▼
A)Habitability
Habitability is a perspective in Human Systems Integration (HSI) that focuses on designing environments that are suitable for human occupation. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.4, p.168 and 169.
B)Training
Training is a perspective of Human Systems Integration (HSI) that focuses on ensuring that individuals have the necessary knowledge and skills to effectively act within a system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.4, p.168 and 169.
C)Occupational health
Occupational health is a perspective within Human Systems Integration (HSI) that addresses the physical and mental well-being of individuals within a system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.4, p.168 and 169.
D)Interoperability ✓ Correct
Interoperability refers to the ability of different systems or components to work together effectively and is not considered a Human Systems Integration (HSI) perspective. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.4, p.168 and 169.

388 [ID-5114] What is the name of the analysis used to determine the appropriate location to execute maintenance tasks? ▼
A)FMECA
FMECA, which stands for Failure Modes, Effects, and Criticality Analysis, is an extension of FMEA that includes criticality analysis to prioritize failure modes. It is not used to determine the best location for maintenance tasks. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.6, p.174.
B)FMEA
FMEA, which stands for Failure Mode and Effects Analysis, is a systematic method for identifying and potential failure modes of a system or component and the effects of these failures. It is not specifically used to determine the appropriate location to execute maintenance tasks. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.6, p.174.
C)LORA ✓ Correct
The question statement describes the Level of Repair Analysis (LORA). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.6, p.174.
D)FTA
FTA, which stands for Fault Tree Analysis, is an analysis used to determine the various combinations of events that can lead to a specific system failure. It is not related to determining the appropriate location for executing maintenance tasks. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.6, p.174.

390 [ID-5079] A shared boundary between two systems defined by functional physical or other characteristics is called: ▼
A)Interface ✓ Correct
An interface is a shared boundary between two systems, allowing them to interact with each other. It is defined by functional, physical, or other characteristics that enable the exchange of information, data, energy, etc. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.313.
B)Subsystem
A subsystem is a secondary or subordinate system that is part of a larger system. It does not refer to the shared boundary between two systems.
C)Connector
A connector is a mechanism that may facilitate the establishment of an interface between different systems, but does not cover the concept of interface as a whole.
D)Context
In systems engineering, context refers to the circumstances or setting in which a system operates. It includes all the external factors that influence the system but does not specifically refer to a shared boundary between two systems.

392 [ID-5054] Which of the following is NOT a technical leadership behavior? ▼
A)Communicating effectively.

B)Enabling others to succeed.
Enabling others to succeed is a technical leadership behavior that involves empowering team members, providing support and resources, and creating opportunities for growth and development within the team. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.1.3, p.264.
C)Fostering collaboration.
Fostering collaboration is a technical leadership behavior that involves promoting teamwork, sharing knowledge and expertise and creating a supportive environment where team members can work together towards common objectives. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.1.3, p.264.
D)Having the final say about all technical matters. ✓ Correct
Having the final say about all technical matters is not a typical technical leadership behavior. Effective technical leaders often seek input from team members, encourage diverse perspectives, and make decisions collaboratively rather than unilaterally. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.1.3, p.264.

394 [ID-5117] The following characteristics are useful when deciding whether a SoI is a System of Systems, EXCEPT: ▼
A)Geographical distribution of constituent systems.
The geographical distribution of constituent systems is a useful characteristic for identifying a System of Systems. When the systems are spread across different locations or regions, it can indicate that they are relatively independent, pointing towards the existence of an SoS. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 4.3.6, p.235.
B)Managerial independence of constituent systems.
The managerial independence of constituent systems is a key characteristic that helps in determining whether a System of Systems (SoS) exists. When the constituent systems have their own management structures and decision-making processes, it indicates that the SoI should be classified as an SoS. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 4.3.6, p.235.
C)Operational independence of constituent systems.
The operational independence of constituent systems is an important factor in determining whether a SoI is a System of Systems. When the constituent systems can operate independently while still contributing to the overall goal of the larger system, it indicates that the SoI should be classified as an SoS. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 4.3.6, p.235.
D)High number of constituent systems. ✓ Correct
Having a high number of constituent systems is not relevant for a SoI to be classified as a SoS. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 4.3.6, p.235.

B)system life cycle processes ✓ Correct
Tailoring involves customizing system life cycle processes to align with the specific needs and constraints of the organization. This ensures that the processes are tailored to fit the organization's unique traits, rather than forcing a one-size-fits-all approach. It also allows for adjustments and modifications to be made to the processes to better align with the specific goals, scope, and constraints of the project at hand. C)project
The tailoring process is used to customize the system life cycle processes, not the project. This doesn't mean that the project can't be adapted as well, but this adaptation wouldn't be within the scope of the tailoring process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.1, pp.215-219.
D)system of interest
The tailoring process is used to customize the system life cycle processes, not the system of interest. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.1, pp.215-219.

403 [ID-5091] Choose the statement that reflects a reasonable heutistic for Systems Engineering ▼
A)Modeling before building is losing time.
Modeling before building is not a waste of time; in fact, it is a fundamental practice in systems engineering. Modeling allows for the visualization, analysis, and validation of system requirements, components, and interactions before physical implementation. It helps in identifying potential issues early on and ensures that the final system meets the desired objectives. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.4, p.20.
B)In the early stages of the project, problems are a bigger issue than the unknowns.
It is the other way around. In the early stages of a project, it is common for the unknowns to outweigh the known problems. This is because as the project unfolds, new challenges, requirements, and complexities may arise that were not initially identified. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.4, p.20.
C)All the other sentences reflect unreasonable heuristics. ✓ Correct
Indeed. See the other explanations.
D)Start by assuming that the original problem statement is the best.
Starting by assuming that the original problem statement is the best may lead to overlooking the real problem and potential improvements. It is important to remain open to revising and refining the problem statement not only at the beginning, but also as the project progresses. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.4, p.20.

A)Support

B)Disposal ✓ Correct
"Disposal" is a system life cycle process, not a system life cycle stage. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, pp.26-29
C)Development
"Development" is a generic life cycle stage according to ISO/IEC/IEEE 15288. In this period, the engineering design effort is performed until the solution can be built under acceptable risks. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, p.28.
D)Production
Production is a generic life cycle stage according to ISO/IEC/IEEE 15288. This is the stage where the assembling and/or construction of the system occurs. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.2, p.28.

Affordability analysis has broader scope (the whole life cycle), not being specifically focused on the production. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.2, pp.160-165.

This is a technical system life cycle process that deals with the realization of system elements. it is not specifically focused on the aspects mentioned by the question. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5.7, p.132.
411 [ID-5099] Which of the following is NOT part of logistics engineering scope? ▼
A)Ensuring that the SOI attends its performance requirements ✓ Correct
This is a broader topic that is not under the reach of logistics engineering activities.
B)Designing the system for supportability
Designing the system for supportability involves creating a system that is easy to maintain, repair, and sustain throughout its lifecycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5 , p.173.
C)Determining logistics support requirements
Determining logistics support requirements involves identifying the necessary support elements and resources to ensure the system functions effectively and efficiently. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5 , p.173.
D)Acquiring support elements
Acquiring support elements involves procuring the necessary resources to support the system's operation and maintenance. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5 , p.173.
412 [ID-5033] Choose the correct option regarding emergence. ▼
A)Emergence can be observed in a single element.
Emergence is the phenomenon that happens when a system exhibits properties that are not observed in any of its elements by themselves. By definition, emergent properties are the result of interactions between elements and cannot be observed on single elements.
B)All the other options are wrong. ✓ Correct
Indeed. See the other explanations.
C)Only desirable system properties are considered to be emergent properties.
Emergent properties can be desirable or undesirable. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.2., p.9.
D)Only undesirable system properties are considered to be emergent properties.
Emergent properties can be desirable or undesirable. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.2., p.9.
413 [ID-5043] Choose right sentence regarding the risk management process. ▼
A)All the other options are wrong. ✓ Correct
Indeed. See the other explanations.
B)Risk measurement is one of the basic risk treatment approaches.
Risk measurement is not considered one of the basic risk treatment approaches. The basic risk treatment approaches are: acceptance (acknowledging and living with the risk), avoidance (changing the course of action to eliminate the risk), mitigation (reducing the impact or likelihood of the risk), and transference (shifting the risk to another party). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.4.4, pp.83-86.
C)Every risk has 3 components: cause, likelihood and consequence.
Risks have 2 components only, those being likelihood and consequence. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.4.4, p.85.
D)Contingencies should be planned for every identified risk.
While it is important to plan contingencies for identified risks, it is not always necessary or feasible to plan for every single identified risk. Contingency planning should focus on the most critical risks. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.4.4, p.87.
414 [ID-5066] What name is given to an external system that has interfaces with the SoI? ▼
A)Interfacing system ✓ Correct
An interfacing system is an external system that interfaces with the SoI. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.3, p.10.
B)Interoperating system
An interoperating system is a system that interfaces with the SoI in its operational environment to perform a common function that contributes for achieving the purpose of the SoI. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.3, p.10.
C)Intersectioning system
A made up term.
D)Enabling system
Enabling systems are external systems that facilitate a life cycle activity of the SoI but are not an element of its operational environment. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.3, p.10.

417 [ID-5009] All of the following are organizational project enabling processes, EXCEPT: ▼
A)Infrastructure management
Infrastructure management is an organizational project enabling processes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.154. A good reasoning to adopt in order to associate the management processes to their right group would be: "If it relates more to the organization as a whole than it relates to the system, it is an organizational project enabling process. If it relates more to the system than it relates to the organization as a whole, it is a technical management process".
B)Information management ✓ Correct
Information management is a technical management process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.154.This is very tricky because:1- There are some management processes that fit into the technical management processes group and others that are part of the organizational project enabling processes. Normally, a good reasoning to adopt in order to associate the management processes to their right group would be: "If it relates more to the organization as a whole than it relates to the system/project, it is an organizational project enabling process. If it relates more to the system/project than it relates to the organization as a whole, it is a technical management process". This works very well with all the other management processes but not with information management. In this particular case, I suggest you just memorize that information management is a technical management process.
C)Quality management
Quality management is an organizational project enabling processes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.154.This is very tricky because:1- There are some management processes that fit into the technical management processes group and others that are part of the organizational project enabling processes. A good reasoning to adopt in order to associate the management processes to their right group would be: "If it relates more to the organization as a whole than it relates to the system/project, it is an organizational project enabling process. If it relates more to the system/project than it relates to the organization as a whole, it is a technical management process".2- It can get mistaken for quality assurance. To avoid this, remember: quality assurance deals with the effective application of the quality management process to the project - so it is a technical management process - while quality management relates to the quality objectives of the organization, thus being an organizational project enabling process.
D)Knowledge management
Knowledge management is an organizational project enabling processes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.154. A good reasoning to adopt in order to associate the management processes to their right group would be: "If it relates more to the organization as a whole than it relates to the system, it is an organizational project enabling process. If it relates more to the system than it relates to the organization as a whole, it is a technical management process".

419 [ID-5022] It is a good practice to make the technical reviews be: ▼
A)risk-driven ✓ Correct
Making technical reviews risk-driven ensures that the focus is on identifying and mitigating potential risks in the system or project. By prioritizing areas of high risk, technical reviews can help address critical issues early in the development process and improve overall project outcomes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.4., p.32.
B)cost-driven.
Cost-driven technical reviews may prioritize cost-saving measures over quality and risk management. While cost is an important factor in project management, focusing solely on cost may lead to overlooking critical issues that could impact the success of the project. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.4., p.32.
C)Schedule-driven.
Schedule-driven technical reviews prioritize meeting project deadlines over quality and risk management. While meeting deadlines is important, rushing through technical reviews to adhere to a schedule may result in overlooking critical issues and compromising the overall quality of the project. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.4., p.32.
D)All the other options are wrong.
There is a correct option to be chosen among the others.
420 [ID-5097] All of the following are critical elements in an agile architecture, EXCEPT: ▼
A)Encapsulated modules
Encapsulated modules are essential in agile architecture as they promote modularity, reusability, and maintainability of the system. They help in isolating components and reducing dependencies, making it easier to make changes and enhancements to the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.3, p.166.
B)Active infrastructure
This is responsible for sustaining the agile operational capability by designating specific responsibilities. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.3, p.166.
C)Passive infrastructure
This is the minimal infrastructure that provides "plug and play" connectivity between modules. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.3, p.166.
D)Tightly coupled system elements ✓ Correct
Tightly coupled system elements are not desirable in agile architecture as they increase dependencies between components, making it challenging to make changes and adapt. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.3, p.166.
421 [ID-5068] All of the following are part of the minimum set of attributes that should be associated with a requirement, EXCEPT: ▼
A)Rationale
Rationale is an essential attribute that provides the reasoning behind the requirement and helps understanding its purpose and context of the requirement. It is an attribute that is always very helpful. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Table 2.9, p.117.
B)Author
The author attribute is important to identify the individual or group responsible for creating or defining the requirement. It helps in clarifying ownership, accountability, and communication regarding the requirement. It is an attribute that is always very helpful. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Table 2.9, p.117.
C)Unique identifier
A unique identifier is necessary for each requirement to distinguish it from other requirements and ensure traceability throughout the project lifecycle. It helps in referencing, tracking changes, and linking requirements to other project artifacts. It is an attribute that is always very helpful. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Table 2.9, p.117.
D)Country ✓ Correct
Country should be a very situational attribute, only adding value when it is relevant to know where the requirement will be applied or other similar situations. The location or country of origin is typically not a standard attribute for defining, managing, or tracing requirements in a project. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Table 2.9, p.117.
422 [ID-5059] Choose the right sentence regarding System Safety Engineering: ▼

B)An intrinsic hazard is a hazard that happens within the system of interest.

423 [ID-5023] Choose the right sentence regarding the acquisition process. ▼

The acquisition process revolves around the acquirer's perspective, as it involves the selection, negotiation, and management of suppliers to fulfill project requirements. This statement is incorrect. A change request can be initiated by either the acquirer or the supplier during the acquisition process. It is a formal process used to request changes to the agreed-upon scope, schedule, or budget of the acquisition agreement. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.2.1, p.46.

This is an activity of the portfolio management process. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.3.3, p.58.

426 [ID-5038] Considering the concepts about system hierarchy, it can be said that: ▼
A)A system viewed as white box is, in that same representation, considered to be atomic.
In a white box representation of a system, the system is viewed as a collection of interconnected components, and it is not considered to be atomic. Atomic systems are viewed as indivisible entities with no internal structure visible in the representation. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.5, pp.12 and 13, and section 1.3.1, p.9.
B)It is a good practice to limit the number of subordinate elements of a decomposed entity within a system hierarchy. ✓ Correct
Having too many subordinates can lead to excessive interfaces and complexity, making it difficult to understand, analyze and manage the system. It is suggested the optimum number of 7+/-2 subordinates for a given system element. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.5, p.13.
C)A "black box" representation of a system gives no visibility of what is outside of its boundary.
A black box representation of a system refers to viewing the system from an external perspective, where the internal workings and details are not visible or relevant to the observer. This allows for a focus on the system's inputs, outputs, and behavior without needing to understand its internal mechanisms. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.5, p.13.
D)A system element can be either atomic or plural.
An atomic system element is one is considered as a fundamental, indivisible unit within the system hierarchy, though it doesn't mean that it is really (physically) indivisible. "Plural" is a made up term though. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.5, pp.12 and 13.

A)Visualizing and analyzing the interfaces and interactions between system components. ✓ Correct
The N² diagram is specifically used to visualize and analyze the interfaces and interactions between system components, making it easier to identify and manage these interactions. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.4, pp.204 and 205.
B)Managing N system requirements versus N verification methods.
This is a made up application.
C)Risk assessment.
A consequence x likelihood matrix is used for this purpose. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.4.4, p.85.
D)Tasks x schedule control.
A gantt chart is typically used for this purpose.
428 [ID-5030] Choose the WRONG option regarding Model Based Systems Engineering (MBSE) ▼
A)SysML is an example of a modeling language.
Not only an example of, but the most widely used system modeling language. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.1, p.220.
B)Within a MBSE approach, the system descriptive model is only one of many artifacts produced by the systems engineering process and does not hold greater importance than the others. ✓ Correct
In MBSE, the system descriptive model has no secondary role and is a key artifact that captures the system requirements, architecture, behavior, and other essential aspects of the system under development.
C)MBSE (Model-Based Systems Engineering) is an approach to SE that focuses on creating and using models as a primary means of communication and understanding within a project or organization.
This is a good description of what MBSE is. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.1, pp.219 and 220.
D)Model Based Systems Engineering is usually contrasted with Document Based Systems Engineering.
In a document-based SE approach, the information about the system is contained in separate documents such as specifications, interface control documents, reports, and verification plans. It contrasts with MBSE, where the model is the single (or main) source of information about the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.1, pp.219 and 220.
429 [ID-5103] The characteristic that reflects what aspects of the SoI (or its context/environment) are represented in a given extent is called: ▼
A)Model breadth ✓ Correct
Model breadth refers to the characteristic that reflects what aspects of the System of Interest (SoI) or its context/environment are represented in a given extent. It determines the scope and coverage of the model in terms of the elements and relationships included. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.1, p.197.
B)Model fidelity
Model fidelity refers to the accuracy and faithfulness of the model in representing the real-world system or environment. It assesses how closely the model mirrors the actual characteristics, behaviors, and interactions of the SoI and its context. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.1, p.197.
C)Model granularity
Model granularity refers to the level of detail or resolution at which the elements and relationships of the model are represented. It focuses on the depth and specificity of the information captured in the model, rather than the breadth or coverage. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.1, p.197.
D)Model interoperability
Model interoperability refers to the ability of a model to work seamlessly with other models, tools, or systems within a given environment. It focuses on the compatibility, exchange of information, and integration capabilities of the model with other components in a larger system or ecosystem. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.1, p.197.
430 [ID-5087] Choose the correct sentence regarding the Return On Investment (ROI) of the Systems Engineering effort (% of SE cost in relation to total program cost). ▼
A)There is no noticeable relationship between SE effort and project cost.
The other explanations show why this is incorrect.
B)The higher it is the Systems Engineering effort, the lower should be the total project cost.
There is an optimum SE effort equivalent to a normalized range of 10% to 14% of the program/project cost. Beyond these percentages, data shows that the program/project cost rises as the Systems Engineering effort increases. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, p.6.
C)The ROI of going from 0% to 5% SE effort should higher than going from 10% to 15% SE effort. ✓ Correct
The Return on Investment (ROI) of increasing the Systems Engineering effort from 0% to 5% should be higher than increasing it from 10% to 15%. This is because the initial investment in Systems Engineering at lower levels has a more significant impact on the overall program cost compared to incremental increases at higher levels of effort. There is an optimum SE effort equivalent to a normalized range of 10% to 14% of the program/project cost. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp.6.
D)The ROI of going from 0% to 5% SE effort should be lower than going from 15% to 40% SE effort.
Increasing the Systems Engineering effort from 0% to 5% has a substantial impact on project outcomes, while the incremental benefits of increasing from 15% to 40% are usually not as significant. There is an optimum SE effort equivalent to a normalized range of 10% to 14% of the program/project cost. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 1.2, pp.6.
431 [ID-5029] Which of the following statements is FALSE? ▼
A)Uncertainty can always be reduced by gathering more knowledge. ✓ Correct
This applies to epistemic uncertainty, but not to aleatory uncertainty. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.1, pp.15 and 16.
B)Systems Engineering practitioners must be able to decide under uncertainty.
Even though epistemic uncertainty can be eliminated, a certain degree of aleatory uncertainty will always be present. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.1, pp. 15 and 16.
C)There are two types of uncertainty: epistemic and aleatory.
These are the two types of uncertainty considered in the handbook. The former is related to lack of knowledge while the latter is due to randomness. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.1, pp.15 and 16.
D)Cognitive bias can cause engineers to overlook critical information and make flawed decisions.
Cognitive bias are judgment errors under uncertainty caused by simplistic information processes, being potentially harmful to information processing and decision making. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.2, p.17.
432 [ID-5007] What is the name of the approach that aims to deliver the best life cycle value with a complex system while producing minimal waste? ▼
A)Lean Systems Engineering ✓ Correct
The question presents the purpose of Lean Systems Engineering. Keywords: minimal waste. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.13, p. 191.
B)Manufacturability analysis
Manufacturability analysis is focused on enabling system production (not in the whole life cycle) in a cost-effective and responsible manner (not necessarily with minimal waste). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.7, p.175.
C)Sustainability Engineering
Sustainability engineering involves assessing the environmental, social, and economic impacts of a system to ensure its long-term viability. It does not specifically address the cost-effective production of the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.10, pp.184 and 185.
D)Loss-Driven Systems Engineering
Loss-Driven Systems Engineering is driven by avoiding losses and doesn't have the focus on minimizing waste. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.3, p. 224.
433 [ID-5040] Choose the WRONG option regarding system complexity: ▼
A)SE approaches and techniques that work well with complicated systems won't work with simple systems. ✓ Correct
A traditional SE approach to a system that systematically breaks down the problem into its parts, solves it and reconstructs it into the whole solution can work with both complicated and simple systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.7, p.15.
B)In a simple system, relationships are easy to understand and the relation of cause and effect is straightforward.
In a simple system, the relationships between elements are straightforward and easy to understand. There is little uncertainty between cause and effect, making it easier to comprehend and predict outcomes within the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.7, p.15.
C)In a complicated system, there are many interconnections between possibly many parts, but there is sufficient certainty between cause and effect.
A complicated system may have many interconnected parts, but the relationships between elements are more structured and deterministic compared to a complex system. In a complicated system, cause-and-effect relationships are more predictable with reduced levels of uncertainty. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.7, p.15.
D)In a complex system, the relationships between elements are not fully comprehended and there is considerable uncertainty between cause and effect.
In a complex system, the relationships between elements are intricate, leading to a high level of uncertainty and unpredictability. This complexity makes it challenging to fully comprehend the system and determine clear cause-and-effect relationships. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3.7, p.15.
434 [ID-5074] The following factors contribute for adopting a COTS (Commercial Off The Shelf) system element, EXCEPT: ▼
A)The COTS product is well documented.
Being well-documented is a contributing factor for adopting the COTS products as it provides information on how to use, integrate and maintain the product. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.3.3, p.231.
B)There is a sole source supply for the COTS product. ✓ Correct
Having a sole source supply for a COTS product can be a risk factor for adopting it. Sole source suppliers may lead to dependency issues, lack of competition, and potential challenges in terms of pricing, support, and future availability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.3.3, p.231.
C)The COTS product is available in multiple, identical copies.
The availability of a COTS product in multiple, identical copies is a beneficial factor for adoption. It ensures consistency in the product's features, functionality, and performance across different instances, making it easier to scale and maintain. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.3.3, p.231.
D)Delivery times are short and predictable.
Short and predictable delivery times are advantageous when adopting a COTS product as they help in meeting project timelines, reducing lead times, and ensuring timely implementation. Predictable delivery times also contribute to better project planning and resource allocation. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.3.3, p.231.
435 [ID-5101] Which of the following quality characteristics is less related with Loss Driven Systems Engineering: ▼
A)Safety
Safety is a loss-driven quality characteristic as it is concerned with preventing accidents or incidents that could lead to significant losses, both in terms of human life and material resources. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.13, p.191.
B)Maintainability
Maintainability is also a loss-driven quality characteristic, as it affects the ease and cost of keeping the system operational, impacting downtime and associated losses. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.13, p.191.
C)Reliability
Reliability is a loss-driven quality characteristic because it directly impacts the likelihood of system failures, which can result in operational losses. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.13, p.191.
D)Modularity ✓ Correct
Even though it could affect other loss driven characteristics such as maintainability, modularity is more inclined to be capability driven in order to add flexible structure and functions to a system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.13, p.191.
436 [ID-5072] A foundational structure (or set of structures) used for developing a wide range of different architectures is called: ▼
A)System model
A system model is a representation of the system that may be physical, digital, formal, informal, etc. It is not a foundational structure used for developing architectures. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.1, p.192-196.
B)Architecture framework ✓ Correct
The question describes the definition of an architect framework. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.2.5, p.206.
C)Conceptual design
The conceptual design is an early proposition that covers the overall form of the design solution. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5.5, p.129.
D)Detailed design
The detailed design further details the preliminary design to the point that the designed solution can be realized unambiguously. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5.5, p.129.
437 [ID-5115] Which of the following aspects is not assessed by a FMECA? ▼
A)Failure causes ✓ Correct
Failure causes are not assessed in a FMECA. The focus of a FMECA is on identifying failure modes, their effects, and criticalities, rather than the underlying causes of those failures. Identifying failure causes is usually done in a separate analysis, such as a Fault Tree Analysis (FTA). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.6, p.174.
B)Failure criticalities
The FMECA (Failure Modes, Effects, and Criticality Analysis) assesses failure criticalities, assigning a level of criticality to each identified failure mode based on its effects. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.6, p.174.
C)Failure modes
The FMECA (Failure Modes, Effects, and Criticality Analysis) assesses failure modes, identifying all possible ways in which a system or element can fail. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.6, p.174.
D)Failure effects
The FMECA (Failure Modes, Effects, and Criticality Analysis) assesses failure effects, evidentiating the consequences of each identified failure mode. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 3.1.6, p.174.
438 [ID-5118] Fill in the blank with the best available option:In complicated systems, the interactions between elements are governed by ________ interactions. ▼
A)obvious
"Obvious interactions" is a characteristic of a simple system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.7, p.15.
B)fixed ✓ Correct
In complicated systems, the interactions between elements are governed by fixed interactions, meaning that the relationships and behaviors between the elements are predetermined and consistent. This stability in interactions helps in understanding and predicting the system's behavior. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.7, p.15.
C)uncertain
Uncertain interactions imply that the relationships and behaviors between elements in a system are unpredictable and variable. In complicated systems there may be many and convoluted interactions, but they are still fixed. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.7, p.15.
D)intensive
While intensive interactions may occur in complicated systems, they are not the defining factor in governing the system's behavior. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.7, p.15.
439 [ID-5075] In which of these sectors the customer requirements are not expected to be assumed by the manufacturer? ▼
A)Automotive
In the automotive sector, the customers don't explicitly elaborate their requirements. The manufacturers typically assume customer requirements and preferences when designing and producing vehicles. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4, p.246.
B)Defense ✓ Correct
In the defense sector, customer requirements are not expected to be assumed by the manufacturer. This is due to the sensitive nature of defense projects, where requirements are often dictated by government agencies or military organizations with specific security and performance criteria that must be met. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4, p.246.
C)Customer requirements are always assumed by the manufacturer.
Not really, as it can be seen in the other explanations.
D)Consumer electronics
In the consumer electronics sector, even though manufacturers often take into account customer preferences and market trends when developing new products, the requirements are not explicitly elaborated by the customers. The requirements are assumed by the manufacturers. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 4.4, p.246.
440 [ID-5025] According to the information management process, information assets are either: ▼
A)tacit or explicit
This applies to knowledge, not information. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.6, p.91 and section 2.3.3.6, p.67.
B)implicit or explicit
"Explicit" applies to knowledge, not information. "Implicit" applies to neither. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.6, p.91 and section 2.3.3.6, p.67.
C)tangible or intangible ✓ Correct
Information assets may be tangible (e.g. a specification) or intangible (e.g. stakeholder needs that are yet to be recorded)). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.4.6, p.91.
D)solid or ethereal
These are made up terms.

442 [ID-5046] The following sentences apply to "system architecture", EXCEPT: ▼
A)Architecture pattern is a configurable and reusable architecture that comprises a minimal set of elements applicable for certain situations.
An architecture pattern provides a high-level structure for implementing similar systems and helps in handling recurring system solutions efficiently. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5.4, p.123.
B)Functional architecture defines a breakdown of the functions of the SoI into successive lower levels.
This is the correct definition of a functional architecture. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.4.1, p.74.
C)An effective system architecture permits only one system design solution. ✓ Correct
System architecture should be as agnostic as possible in relation with possible design solutions, allowing the necessary flexibility to pursue optimum solutions. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.5.4, p.123.

443 [ID-5004] Choose the WRONG sentence regarding Reliability, Availability & Maintainability (RAM). ▼
A)Reliability block diagrams are used in reliability engineering analyses.
Reliability block diagrams are commonly used in reliability engineering analyses to model the reliability of complex systems by breaking them down into smaller components. They help in assessing the overall reliability of a system by analyzing and associating the reliability of individual components. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, pp. 176-178.
B)RAM requirements should be used to influence both the system of interest and its support system definitions.
RAM requirements play a crucial role in influencing both the system of interest and its support system definitions. By considering reliability, availability, and maintainability requirements early from the concept stage, engineers can ensure that the system meets the desired performance and operational goals. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, pp. 176.
C)A low value for a system's availability is evidence that its reliability is also low. ✓ Correct
A low value for a system's availability does not necessarily indicate that its reliability is also low. Availability refers to the proportion of time that a system is ready to operate and can be affected by other factors such as long maintenance periods and administrative delays. Reliability, on the other hand, measures likelihood of a system to performing its intended function without failure. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, pp. 176-179.
D)Mean time to repair is a maintainability metric.
Mean time to repair (MTTR) is a key maintainability metric that measures the average time it takes to repair a failed system and restore it to operational status. It is an important indicator of how quickly maintenance activities can be performed to minimize downtime and ensure system availability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.8, p. 179.
444 [ID-5116] An internal view of the system that shows his elements, properties and interactions is called: ▼
A)System of Interest
A System of Interest may or not be represented as a white box, a black box or other partially descriptive view.
B)System boundary
The system boundary is the demarcation between what is part of the system and what is not. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 1.3.1, p.8.
C)White box ✓ Correct
The question statement describes the concept of a white box. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.315.
D)Black box
A black box is an external view of the system that does not show its internals. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.312.
445 [ID-5037] Choose the type of system that fits inside the SoI boundary: ▼
A)Al the other options are wrong. ✓ Correct
The boundary separates the SoI from its context, with the latter containing the systems presented in the other options. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3, pp.8-10.
B)Enabling systems
Enabling systems are external systems that contribute to any life cycle activity of the SoI, but are not an element of the operational environment. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3, pp.8-10.
C)Interoperating systems
Interoperating systems are external systems that have at least one interface with the SoI in its operational environment in order to perform a common function that contributes to the SoI's purpose. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3, pp.8-10.
D)Interfacing systems
Interfacing systems are external systems that have at least one interface with the SoI. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.3, pp.8-10.

447 [ID-5053] A maintenance task that is performed based in the collection of data during the system operation prior to breakdown, is called: ▼
A)Corrective maintenance
Corrective maintenance involves repairing a system after it has already malfunctioned or broken down. It is reactive in nature and aims to restore the system to its normal functioning state after an issue has occurred. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.156.
B)Preventive maintenance
Preventive maintenance is a proactive approach to maintenance that involves regularly scheduled tasks to keep a system in good working condition and prevent unexpected failures. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.156.
C)Predictive maintenance ✓ Correct
Predictive maintenance utilizes data collected to predict when maintenance should be performed to prevent potential failures. By analyzing data trends and patterns, maintenance tasks can be scheduled at optimal times to maximize system reliability and minimize downtime. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.156.
D)System modification
System modification refers to making changes or enhancements to a system to extend its useful life or to improve its capabilities. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.5.13, p.156.
448 [ID-5041] Which of the following correctly describes a Systems Engineering principle? ▼
A)Systems Engineering only support the system's related activities performed until it is activated at its operational environment.
Systems Engineering encompasses the entire lifecycle of a system, from concept to retirement, including all system related activities within its life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.
B)Complex systems can be engineered by any organization.
Not all organizations may have the expertise or resources to effectively engineer complex systems. Systems Engineering principles recognize the need for collaboration and coordination among various disciplines and stakeholders (thus, a complex organization) to successfully engineer complex systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.
C)All the other options are wrong. ✓ Correct
Indeed. See the other explanations.
D)A model can be used as a perfect representation of a system.
Only the real system can perfectly represent itself. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.4.3, p.19.

452 [ID-5105] Choose the correct sentence in relation to Systems Engineering transformation in an enterprise ▼
A)Transforming document based SE to model based SE is a matter of going from textual documents to 2D and 3D visual representations of the system.
Transforming from document-based Systems Engineering to model-based Systems Engineering involves more than just converting textual documents to visual representations. It includes a shift towards using models as the primary means of communication, analysis, and decision-making throughout the system life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.5, pp. 274 and 275.
B)All the other options are wrong. ✓ Correct
Indeed. See the other explanations.
C)Going from traditional SE to agile SE is all about faster production of work products throughout the system's life cycle.
Transitioning from traditional Systems Engineering to agile Systems Engineering is about increasing flexibility and ability to deal with uncertainties, not necessarily increasing the speed of work product production. Agile SE emphasizes iterative and incremental development, customer collaboration, and responding to change over following a plan, which fundamentally changes the approach to the system life cycle processes to be used. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.5, pp. 274 and 275.
D)Going from no SE to full use SE requires strict enforcement from the higher administration.
Moving from no Systems Engineering to full use of Systems Engineering does not require such brute enforcement. It rather involves cultural changes, training, process improvements, and organizational buy-in to fully integrate Systems Engineering practices into the enterprise. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.5, pp. 274 and 275.
453 [ID-5018] Choose the right sentence. ▼
A)When a constituent system of a SoS enters into its retirement stage, the SoS also enters into its retirement stage. ✓ Correct
To enter into the retirement stage, the SOI (in this case, a SoS) doesn't have to be entirely retired. It is enough that one its elements enters into the retirement stage. A constituent system of a SoS functions as one of its elements. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.1., pp.25 and 26.
B)System life cycle stages can only be entered once each.
System life cycle stages can be re-entered multiple times, as it happens when an evolutionary life cycle approach with multiple development and utilization stages is adopted. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.1., pp.25 and 26. See also section 2.2., p.33.
C)System life cycle stages cannot occur concurrently.
System life cycle stages can occur concurrently and the most commonly observed situation is the parallelism between utilization and support stages. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.1., pp.25 and 26.
D)Once a system enters its retirement stage, it can't go back to its utilization stage.
Systems may be reactivated, repurposed, or undergo refurbishment to extend their operational life or address changing requirements. Systems can transition between different life cycle stages based on organizational needs and strategic decisions. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.1.1., pp.25 and 26.

455 [ID-5067] In relation to a patrol ship, a friendly and intercommunicating aircraft carrier that is taking part in the same mission is a: ▼

456 [ID-5080] What would be the purpose of a Physical Configuration Audit? ▼
A)To validate that the system meets all safety and regulatory standards.
This is not the primary focus of a Physical Configuration Audit. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.314.
B)To confirm that the configuration documentation accurately reflects the operational system. ✓ Correct
A Physical Configuration Audit (PCA) aims to verify that the operational configuration of the system matches the configuration documentation, ensuring that all components are correctly built and installed as specified. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.314.
C)To ensure that all project management processes are being followed correctly.
This would be the purpose of a process audit aiming to check that project management processes and procedures are being followed as planned. It wouldn't specifically focus on the physical configuration of the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.314.
D)To verify the system's operational performance against specified requirements.
This would be the purpose of an Operational Test and Evaluation (OT&E), which assesses the system's performance in a real-world operational environment to ensure it meets the specified requirements. This is not the primary purpose of a Physical Configuration Audit. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Appendix C, p.314.
457 [ID-5076] Which of the following traits is associated with hard skills? ▼
A)Measurable. ✓ Correct
Hard skills tend to be quantifiable, observable, and objectively measured through assessments, tests, or demonstrations. This makes them more concrete and verifiable compared to soft skills. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.1.1, p.263.
B)Difficult to test.
Soft skills can be challenging to measure and evaluate objectively. Hard skills, in contrast, are typically easier to test and assess through specific criteria and performance metrics. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.1.1, p.263.
C)Subjective definitions.
Subjective definitions are more related to soft skills, which involve interpersonal skills, emotional intelligence, and communication abilities. Hard skills, on the other hand, tend to be more tangible and quantifiable. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.1.1, p.263.
D)Social applications.
Social applications are typically associated with soft skills, which involve how individuals interact with others and navigate social situations. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 5.1.1, p.263.
458 [ID-5045] A breakdown of the SoI into the lower level details of its physical architecture is called: ▼
A)Work Breakdown Structure
The Work Breakdown Structure (WBS) is a hierarchical decomposition of the project scope into work packages that are deliverables-oriented. It focuses on breaking down the project work into manageable components, but it does not specifically address the breakdown of the SoI into its physical architecture details. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.4.1, p.74.
B)Product Breakdown Structure ✓ Correct
The Product Breakdown Structure (PBS) is a hierarchical decomposition into lower-level physical architecture details of a product (e.g. a SoI). See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.4.1, p.74.
C)All the other options are wrong.
There is a correct option to be chosen among the others.
D)Functional Breakdown Structure
The Functional Breakdown Structure (FBS) is a hierarchical decomposition of the functions of the SoI into lower successive levels. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.4.1, p.74.

464 [ID-5013] Choose the WRONG sentence regarding the stakeholder needs and requirements definition process: ▼
