C)Cost-effectiveness measures costs versus monetary outcomes.
This should be cost-benefit. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.2, p.163.
D)Cost-effectiveness and cost benefit mean the same thing.
While both involve assessing costs in relation to outcomes, cost-effectiveness specifically focuses on performance returns, while cost-benefit is focused on monetary outcomes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.2, p.163.
467 [ID-5020] Choose the right option regarding Life Cycle Costs (LCC): ▼
A)Development costs tend to be higher than production costs.
It's way more common to observe the other way around. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.2.,Figure 3.4., p.164.
B)Top down, bottom up and expert judgment are LLC analysis methods. ✓ Correct
The three of them are indeed LCC analysis methods. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.2., p.165.
C)Life Cycle Cost (LCC) and Total Cost of Ownership (TCO) are the same thing.
LCC includes all costs incurred through the system's life cycle while TCO may only include costs related to the period after purchasing/realizing the system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.2., p.164.
D)When performing LCC analysis, the parametric method compares the proposed system with one or more similar ones.
The analogy (not parametric) method involves comparing the proposed system with one or more similar systems to estimate costs and benefits. This method helps in making informed decisions based on past experiences and existing systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 3.1.2., p.165.
468 [ID-5028] Choose the correct option regarding intersection between Systems Engineering (SE) and other disciplines. ▼
A)There is a full overlap between SE and PM.
There is an intersection, but noth a full overlap. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.3.3, pp.268 and 269.
B)SE and PM have an intersection, but the technical management processes are not part of it.
Technical management processes involve planning, organizing, and controlling technical aspects of a project, which is a common area between Systems Engineering and Project Management. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.3.3, pp.268 and 269.
C)There is no overlap between SE and PM.
There is a relevant intersection. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 5.3.3, pp.268 and 269.
D)All the other options are wrong. ✓ Correct
Indeed. See the other explanations.
469 [ID-5095] Which system life cycle process is responsible for authorizing a project to proceed through a gate when assessments are favorable? ▼
A)Decision management process
The decision management process involves establishing a decision-making framework to evaluate different alternatives throughout the system life cycle, but it doesn't specifically deal with assessing and authorizing a project to proceed through a gate. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.4.3, pp.78-81.
B)Life cycle model management process
The focus of this process is to establish, assess and improve system life cycle processes within an organization. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.3.1, pp.51 and 52.
C)Project planning process
The project planning process focuses on creating a detailed plan for how the project will be executed, including defining objectives, scope, schedule and budget. It is not responsible for authorizing a project to proceed through a gate. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.4.1, pp.71 and 72.
D)Project assessment and control process ✓ Correct
The project assessment and control process is responsible for evaluating project progress and determining if the project is ready to proceed through a gate. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 2.3.4.2, pp.75-77.
470 [ID-5031] Which of the following SE approaches would be specifically fit when dealing with the life cycle of a family of similar systems with variations in features and functions? ▼
A)Lean Systems Engineering
Lean Systems Engineering focuses on maximizing value while minimizing waste in the system life cycle. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.3, p.224.
B)Agile Systems Engineering
Agile Systems Engineering is a principle-based approach to design, build, sustain, and evolve systems under uncertainty. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.2, p.221.
C)Product Line Engineering ✓ Correct
Product Line Engineering is an approach that provides models, tools, and methods for holistic engineering of system families. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.4, p.226.
D)Model Based Systems Engineering
MBSE (Model-Based Systems Engineering) is an approach to system engineering that focuses on creating and using models as a primary means of communication and understanding within a project or organization. MBSE could help a lot, but is not specifically fit for the provided context. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 4.2.1, pp.219 and 220.
471 [ID-5014] Which of the following best defines a "system"? ▼
A)A collection of hardware and software designed to operate independently.
This doesn´t capture the essence of a system, which involves components working together towards a common goal, producing more than its individual parts are up to. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, p.2.
B)Any arrangement of elements.
A system is more than just a random arrangement; it involves a structured organization of components that work together to achieve specific purposes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, p.2.
C)A product designed to perform a specific function.
A product designed to perform a specific function is a narrow definition that does not necessarily encompass the interrelationships associated with a system. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, p.2.
D)A set of interrelated components working together toward a common goal. ✓ Correct
The definition of a system emphasizes the interconnectedness and interactions between components within a system to achieve specific objectives or outcomes. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 1.1, p.2.
472 [ID-5098] Choose the INCORRECT sentence regarding system interoperability. ▼
A)Only systems that are fully integrated can be considered to be interoperable. ✓ Correct
Interoperability does not require systems to be fully integrated; rather, it involves the ability of systems to operate together effectively, even if they are not fully integrated. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5, p.172.
B)It takes into account the social, political and organizational factors.
System interoperability does not solely rely on technical aspects. The broad definition of interoperability also encompasses social, political, and organizational factors that can influence the successful integration and collaboration between systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5, p.172.
C)Adopting published standards for interface definition is one of the main means for achieving system interoperability. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5, p.172.
Adopting published standards for interface definition ensures that systems can communicate and work together effectively if they follow the established protocols and formats. This approach promotes consistency and compatibility among different systems. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5, p.172.
D)Adopting custom interfaces is one of the main means for achieving system interoperability.
An agreed custom interface enables interoperability when standards aren't suited or don't exist. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. section 3.1.5, p.172.
473 [ID-5024] Choose the right sentence regarding explicit and tacit knowledge. ▼
A)Explicit knowledge i found within the individuals and teams.
This applies to tacit knowledge. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.3.6, p.67.
B)Tacit knowledge is the conscious realization of the knowledge.
This applies to explicit knowledge. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.3.6, p.67.
C)Tacit knowledge is usually captured in training, procedures and policies.
This applies to explicit knowledge. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., section 2.3.3.6, p.67.
D)All the other options are wrong. ✓ Correct
See the other explanations.
474 [ID-5010] A requirement states a single capability/trait. We can say that this requirement is: ▼
A)Consistent
"Consistent" is a requirement set characteristic. Consistent sets have unique requirements that do not conflict or overlap. Besides, consistent terminology is used throughout the requirement set. See INCOSE SE Handbook Table 2.8, p.116.
B)Conforming
"Conforming" is about the requirement being written according to a previously agreed pattern. For example: [condition] [subject] [action] [object] [constraint of action]. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed., Table 2.7, p.116. See INCOSE SE Handbook Table 2.7, p.116.
C)All the other options are wrong. ✓ Correct
"Singular" would be the right choice. See INCOSE SE Handbook Table 2.7, p.116.
D)Correct
A correct requirement is an accurate representation of the needs/sources/requirements from which it was transformed. See INCOSE SE Handbook Table 2.7, p.116.
475 [ID-5001] Two life cycle processes interact with one another a few times in a given system hierarchical level. This is called: ▼
A)Iteration ✓ Correct
This happens, for example, when risks are identified (Risk Management Process) due to the elicitation of system requirement (System Requirement Definition Process) and are further mitigated due to chances in the previously elicited requirement. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.1.2, pp.42-44.
B)Interaction
Interaction is a general term that can describe any form of communication or exchange between two entities. While life cycle processes do interact with each other, the specific scenario described in the question, where two processes interact multiple times at a given system hierarchical level, is more accurately referred to as an iteration in systems engineering terminology.
C)Concurrency
Concurrency is the parallel application of different system life cycle processes in the same hierarchical level. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.1.2, pp.42-44.
D)Recursion
Recursion happens when the same system life cycle process is applied to sucessive hierarchical levels. This happens, for example, while applying verification to single equipment and then verifying the integrated set afterwards. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.1.2, pp.42-44. See INCOSE SE Handbook 5th Ed. Section 2.3.1.2, pp.42-44.
